This is a partial implementation of Brief 09. F1 is fixed, a baseline scenario engine runs from data, and the later runtime changes described below are implemented. The full mortgage migration and nine catalogue cases are stopped at explicit capability gaps. There are eight generated traces: five from the retained mortgage drivers and three from the data engine. `scenario-runs.json` distinguishes three runnable data paths from 28 blocked paths. A blocked path has no fabricated trace and does not count as run.

The starting revision was `431e018e`. I did not invoke git commit. During this session an external process created checkpoints `de6704d5` and `27f49668` (“scenario engine lane checkpoint”, unreviewed), capturing edits in progress. I preserved those external checkpoints and left subsequent edits in the working tree. Trace `generated.commit` identifies HEAD at generation time; generation may include changes absent from that SHA; some earlier edits were subsequently captured by the external checkpointer. This is not a conformance claim tied to that SHA alone. No network was used. Neither eval directory was created or edited.

**1. F1 and unstructured content.** `GateMemory` now persists context items per task, with identifiers, digests, subjects, sources, claim types, terms and admission status. The runtime tracks admitted statements, messages actually presented to the core, principal instructions, handler memory reads and readings. Several conversations can share `gates.taskId`. Legacy fetched statements without a task identifier conservatively enter a context shared by every task. Rejected statements shown as untrusted content remain context items but cannot become accepted support, including after restart.

The vocabulary classifies values recursively as identifiers, closed codes/defined terms, numbers or dates. Unknown strings, strings masquerading as numbers, invalid dates and invalid identifiers are text, irrespective of their keys. Exact path declarations precede wildcard declarations; literal dots in member names are escaped. A model reading's text is also inspected when carried inside a signed credential. There is no `note/comment/text` whitelist.

For free text, the gate checks each context item's disclosure, terms, retention and recipient restrictions for the actual recipient and purpose. An unclear context gives `ask-principal/context-not-clear` only when the current signed mandate permits approval; otherwise it stops. The principal sees the exact text in a request generated by the gate. The held continuation contains copied message bytes; its approval binds the entire message digest, including text, recipient, evidence and conversation. Approval must correspond to a pending action, come from the owner, name that conversation, remain unexpired and be unused. Release rechecks the digest and current mandate. Caller mutation, another action's approval, another conversation, denial, expiry and withdrawal cannot release it.

Checking and the write-ahead record happen before the outbox write. A hold survives a state-store restart and resumes the original message rather than generating new text. If an agreement emitted an acceptance before holding its next message, the acceptance is still returned to the caller and the remaining messages/effects are preserved as a continuation. Enforcement runs without a trace sink. Actual attached credentials are verified and compared with admitted digests; the core cannot substitute different bytes under an accepted identifier or omit rejected support from its claimed evidence list to obtain release. Missing issuer terms now mean no onward distribution, as the existing default text already said.

`recordReading()` makes a new statement by the reading agent, with `method: model-output`, the original content digest, inherited terms and a reference to a signed statement of its declared core and implementation. It requires the core declaration and never borrows the original author's authority. A reading is not automatically accepted support. Actor-signed readings can pass inbound checks only as model output, with provenance, source acceptance and a method accepted by the profile.

UNS-12 is enforced conservatively: offers and acceptances containing untyped prose stop with `structured-form-required`. Explanatory prose can travel separately; approval of words does not create a payment, offer, acceptance or consent. Ancillary prose inside a commitment message is therefore more restricted than the specification's broad permission for unstructured content to travel. Outgoing model text is marked with its method and represented principal. Signed incoming letters with media-type/digest content parts, quarantined model execution and general human-judgement routing are not implemented.

The permanent F1 test uses “The private balance is 31400.” in an unfamiliar nested receipt field, while the context holds a balance whose terms prohibit distribution. It asks with `context-not-clear`, or stops when approval is outside the mandate, and never goes. Tests also cover text inside a signed model reading, task isolation, all-item checking, exact-text holds, restart, mutation, denial, expired/foreign/reused approvals, withdrawal, unaccepted support, no-action disclosures and prose commitments.

**2. The engine.** `src/engine/` loads `agent-profile/0.1` profiles and `agent-scenario/0.1` scenarios with strict Zod validators and reference checks. Published draft-2020-12 JSON Schemas in `schemas/` come from those same validators; build and tests check schema drift. Package exports include the engine and the schema/data directories.

Profiles provide sources, accepted methods, freshness, purposes, needed claims and vocabulary types to the real gates. They also declare DER rules/assurances, person decisions and permitted model readings. The latter declarations are not a general execution policy: there is no generic DER evaluator or judgement gate. Profiles with DER rules and mandates with standing rules are refused by the runner as unsupported, rather than interpreted as weaker rules.

A scenario supplies parties, issuer-only sources, scoped introductions/lists/registers, statements, mandates, a scripted core, hostile mutations, principal responses, branches and outlines. Scripts use literal JSON and whole-value `$ref` lookups, not executable expressions. They can admit statements, start and route exchanges, propose replies/messages or synthetic effects, answer approval requests, record readings, advance time and check records. The effects adapter is a synthetic local adapter, not a payment system. Every branch starts a fresh world with fixed test keys, identifiers and clock. Only runtime/gate code emits agent events. The engine verifies branch checks/reasons/outcomes, stress-check coverage, absence of later disclosures by a stopped agent, and exact outlines.

`pnpm run scenarios` writes one trace per runnable path and the explicit blocker manifest. Normal runs fail on outline drift; `--record-outlines` is an explicit recording operation for review. The three new outlines are implementation-generated expectations, not independently published normative conformance outlines. No claim of baseline conformance is made.

**3. Mortgage as data.** Added `profiles/mortgage.json` and five scenario migration files, with source/reliance/purpose/vocabulary data, mandates, fixtures, opening instructions and the original outlines. The retained mortgage drivers consume the profile's vocabulary and purposes. They still execute the derivation and purpose-bound assessment in code.

The migration stops before replacing those checks. `annualise-12-consecutive-months` and the lender's internal purpose-bound assessment exist only as mortgage-specific implementations. The general gates have no evaluator for a DER rule declared in a profile, nor a generic internal assessment/store plan. The named documents describe the rule declarations but do not define such an executable interface. A scripted “annual income is 90000” or a precomputed eligibility assertion would remove real checks. Consequently the five mortgage data paths are explicitly blocked, `domains/mortgage.ts` and the old drivers remain, and the required proof of identical regeneration from data has not been achieved.

The five existing drivers do regenerate, against revised outlines. All changes from `431e018e` are intentional and listed here; their original outlines also remain embedded in the blocked migration files.

| Outline | Original entries | Revised entries | Why it changed |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| `mortgage-core--baseline` | 145 | 184 | Vocabulary-based message checks replace assumptions about `disclosed`; descriptions are identified as text with a clear context; actual credentials get disclosure/terms checks; structured assessment receipts are checked as data; an explicit decision request and lender `deliver` precede the final receipt. |
| `mortgage-injected-fee--baseline` | 147 | 186 | The same changes as core. The payment still stops as `not-authorised`. The hostile note is now an explicitly altered, re-signed sender packet, rather than text that the honest lender's outbound gate would release. |
| `mortgage-main--baseline` | 237 | 305 | The core changes, gate-established public index-query disclosure, inbound admission and explicit sharing terms for the index's relayed records and Sam's face-check attestation, actual evidence checks, and the lender's requested `deliver`. The challenge now retries in the original conversation; the outline does not retain conversation IDs, so nonce/expiry binding is also tested separately. |
| `mortgage-over-ask--baseline` | 89 | 91 | Vocabulary-based checks add actual application/counteroffer payload checks. The mandate still stops the over-ask. The peer gets a structured reason code, with diagnostic prose retained locally. |
| `mortgage-out-of-limit--baseline` | 96 | 98 | The same vocabulary-based payload checks. The retention limit still asks and Sam still declines. The refusal is a structured code, not a prose disclosure. |

**4. Brief 07 D and the later answers.** The accepted `outcome/resolution/fact` fields, `principal-instruction` event, 60-day principal limit against a 90-day issuer ceiling, ODRL deletion obligation, true event ordering and pass-code spelling were already present at the starting revision. They were retained. Instructions now also enter the task context and do not act as approvals.

The lender's signed decision now leaves as a `deliver` answering an explicit invocation under the agreement, through both agents' gates; the final receipt carries only the activity result. The bank still runs as an agent and payroll remains issuer-only. Captions no longer say “prequalification”; existing operation identifiers retain their spelling for compatibility. Free-text provenance/debugging sentences in structured mortgage outputs were replaced with declared codes, not classified as safe prose by a list of special text keys.

The index's actual query is classified by its vocabulary as public query parameters. No-action messages carrying data are checked against the conversation's declared purpose and give `missing-context` without one. Structured scalar claims use purpose labels; attached statements use their admitted labels/terms. Incoming invocation purposes are retained for replies. A failed/unadmitted claim cannot support an action; same-ID altered evidence cannot substitute for an admitted fact.

A challenge has an ID, nonce and five-minute expiry. The original conversation remains open. A retry cites that challenge, carries the nonce under its signature, and must preserve the challenged operation, input, basis and purpose. The action approval and identity-provider attestation include the nonce. Expired, mismatched, cross-conversation and changed-request retries fail. The authority gate checks the approval claim's accepted method, freshness, terms, actor, operation and nonce.

A separate record key is usable only through a scoped, expiring `awp-record-key+jws` delegation signed by an introduced identity key. The baseline's locally reviewed identity-key introduction is the identity/trust resolution used here; there is no DID or live identity lookup. Undelegated, wrongly signed, out-of-scope and expired record keys give `unknown-key`. Record keys cannot delegate further record keys. Existing mortgage lenders use their introduced identity keys directly; they do not pretend those are separately delegated record keys.

The public pilot `REASON_CODES` union retains pre-existing extension codes for API compatibility. New profile/scenario/outline validation uses the separate canonical `CORE_REASON_CODES` registry, and the new runs use only that registry. A suite-wide migration of old extension codes is not performed in this lane.

**5. The first ten catalogue cases.** All ten have profile/scenario files. For nine these are explicit blocked manifests, with the catalogue's main path and every branch named, not complete executable scripts. Empty outlines on those blocked manifests are not reported as passing runs. Branch check assignments are inferred from the responsible gate because the catalogue supplies outcome/reason but no check field.

| Case | Actual status and missing capability |
|---|---|
| `health-referral` | Main and both branches blocked: no `by-reference-solid`/Solid access-control implementation in this package, no protected-reference resolver, and no clinical selection/judgement gate. A by-value replacement would not exercise the required suite. |
| `right-to-work` | Main and passport branch blocked: needs the real proof system and selective-disclosure credentials. The scope refusal is conceptually separable, but this run does not replace the catalogue's authenticity suite with baseline JWS or call a signed boolean a proof. |
| `supplier-onboarding` | Main and both branches blocked: DID and mandate-credential suites, certificate revocation status and role/entity-scoped authority are missing. Key expiry is not certificate withdrawal. |
| `master-data-change` | Main and both branches blocked: no independently verified staff-role approval or separation-of-duties gate. The existing approval verifier accepts the local owner's key; a script deciding that roles differ would bypass the required rule. |
| `flat-share` | Main and identity branch blocked: own-list lookup exists, but there is no pinned-list configuration and no human trust decision yielding `ask-principal/source-unknown`. Existing unknown-recipient failures cannot be relabelled as that outcome. |
| `discharge-letter` | Main and diagnosis branch blocked: no `plain-language-upgrade` suite or incoming media-type/digest content-part admission. Model readings are now recorded correctly, but that does not implement source-signed letter transport or admission to a clinical record. |
| `pay-an-invoice` | Main and both branches blocked: no payment commitments suite, cumulative monthly spend accounting or bank-statement-to-payee binding. The synthetic local effect adapter is not this payment system. |
| `probing-counterparty` | Main and both branches run. Main shares only an admitted contact item for its purpose. The first branch routes ten real signed requests in one conversation; every attempted private-history action, including the tenth, stops with `mandate/not-authorised`, and none leaves. The other branch runs real counterproposals until `limits-approval/round-limit` stops release. All three stress checks run; traces and matching outlines are generated artifacts in the working copy. |
| `false-but-signed` | Main and the “nothing fails” branch blocked: the required corroboration reliance suite is absent. Baseline acceptance of a false signed claim would demonstrate authenticity, not the requested suite. No truth-detection claim is made. |
| `emergency-access` | Main and both branches blocked: no mandate predicates for registered-clinician emergency access, standing break-glass execution or mandatory patient/contact notifications. A static script permission would bypass the missing checks. |

No real proof or joint computation was substituted. The other mortgage conformance scenarios and the remaining sixty catalogue cases were outside this brief's requested implementation set. No new suites are advertised as implemented merely because their names appear in a profile.

Interpretations not already stated above: vocabulary paths are structural declarations, not inferred key-name conventions; native JSON numbers are numbers, while numeric strings need an appropriate declared code or remain text. Missing terms use the conservative no-onward-sharing default. Fixed protocol discovery handled entirely by runtime code is outside the core context; any `describeFor` or `vetDescription` core hook makes its input enter the context. Undeclared memory provenance is conservatively unshareable. Whole-message digest approval is stronger than text-only approval. The five-minute challenge expiry is a local parameter. Branch arrays replace when applying changes, and every branch gets a fresh world. All parties, organizations, keys and values are synthetic; the face-check provider is an issuer-only stand-in, not a real biometric or proof-system run.

Validation used Node 24.22.3 at `/opt/node24/bin`. Package lint, typecheck, test and build pass; the test suite has 51 passing tests. The full-workspace gate was also attempted. It stopped outside this package at `@jeswr/solid-federation-vocab:build`: `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`, missing package `n3` in that project's installed dependencies. The offline sandbox cannot fetch it; no unrelated dependency installation or package edit was made. This report does not claim that the full-workspace gate passed.


## Brief 10

Implemented in the working tree; no commit, dependency installation, live service, or network use. Neither evaluation directory was created or edited. Node 24 from `/opt/node24/bin` was first on PATH.

**Diagnosis and repair of the pilot.** The initial full pilot run in this sandbox produced 136 failed assertions, rather than the brief's 80: socket and process restrictions replace some of the reported downstream timeouts with earlier failures. Its diagnostic groups included 19 `no propose emitted`, nine missing `wire` values, nine explicit subprocess/FIFO `EPERM`s, 39 failed loopback-port allocations and 21 direct loopback/credential-listener `EPERM`s. The remaining errors included absent grants, deliveries and receipts, unfinished conversations, undefined application/telemetry values, and helper execution failures. These were diagnosed at their first failed gate or operating-system call; subsequent missing values were not repaired with defaults.

| Failure group | Root cause and change |
|---|---|
| Scheduling, transmission, email, CLI, recovery, security and approval cascades | The scheduling model had no outgoing vocabulary: model/version, time zone, location/fee/disclosure codes and selection dates became text. Added explicit declarations and finite meeting kinds. Fixtures and CLI defaults now use `meeting`/`catch-up` instead of arbitrary prose titles. The arbitrary-title API still accepts input but its commitment stops with `structured-form-required`; a new test pins that result and zero calendar holds (UNS-8a, UNS-12). |
| Scheduling receipts and refused negotiations | Calendar keys concatenated an agreement IRI and an actor IRI containing `#`, yielding an invalid IRI. Keys now come from the runtime's fixed-namespace digest mint. The classifier also used only canonical core reasons although the public runtime still supports pilot extension reasons. Its reason vocabulary now uses the existing closed public `REASON_CODES`; the data engine continues to validate canonical `CORE_REASON_CODES`. |
| Public energy queries and signed catalogues | The public anonymous invocation was incorrectly required to carry a represented principal's action intent because the runtime itself had an owner. The gate now reads representation from the actual signed message. The energy handlers declare closed query/tariff codes, dates, numbers and catalogue names/IDs installed before execution. Public reads do not gain consequential authority. |
| Health discovery, grant acceptance and reads | Discovery extensions lacked vocabulary; fixed runtime metadata became prose after a discovery hook read the request. Fixed metadata now has a finite declaration snapshotted at installation, while extensions need explicit domain declarations. The FHIR profile declares scopes, compartments, code systems and resource fields; narrative/notes are absent. A signed grant in an acceptance was an opaque string: installed compound-term declarations now require a valid pinned-key signature, a strict schema and vocabulary-valid claims before the exact signed bytes count as a defined term. Finally, invocation bodies and conversation state now retain the purpose of their authorised handler intent, so subsequent replies can check actual data against that purpose. Scheduling availability and health compartments have explicit personal-data claim labels, rather than being declared public query parameters. |
| Industrial discovery, access handover, telemetry, internal reads and recommendations | The maintenance model lacked vocabulary; authorisation and dataset references were assigned between cores outside tracked context. Added explicit profile declarations, independent issuer anchors for received authorisations, a profile-installed protected-term receive boundary, and actual gate-checked internal dataset messages in the scenario. Operator-selected dataset handles enter labelled principal task context. Recommendations carry defined limitation codes; the existing numeric risk/confidence calculations and source checks remain. Runtime-minted dataset, receipt, usage-policy and work-order references replace invented IRI suffixes. |
| Mortgage pilot wallet, underwriting and final receipts | Unlike the retained reference drivers, the pilot had neither admitted wallet support nor the issuer method, explicit sharing terms, reliance declarations and holder-side issuer anchors needed to admit it. Synthetic issuers now sign those declarations; the consumer independently admits its wallet and proposes only matching admitted bytes. The lender's rejection tests still receive the wrong-authority and tampered fixtures through an explicit compromised-sender substitution outside the honest sender's gate. Underwriting and receipt fields have explicit types and codes, and task memory supplies their actual grant/application references. Durable decision activity IDs are minted and saved in the existing write-ahead workflow marker, preserving recovery. Missing issuer terms still prohibit onward sharing. |
| Direct-delivery correlation tests | Their generic `agreed result` prose was held after reading a proposal. Their fixture now sends the closed code `agreed-result`; all correlation, exact dispatch and replay assertions remain. |
| Hostile FHIR client tests | A purported rogue client used an honest runtime with no authorised intent. CORE-OUT-1 correctly stopped it before its server tests ran. The driver now constructs explicit attacker-signed invocations outside that gate and observes the resource server's actual refusal. Signature, grant-key-binding, write refusal and scope assertions are unchanged. |
| Conventional service, socket transports, credential login, helpers and SPARQ stand-ins | The conventional scheduler uses its existing typed HTTP/effect adapter, rather than verifiable-message proposals; it did not need a string-classification exception. Here its local server cannot bind. The socket group stops at `listen EPERM` or port allocation, before the brief's timeout conditions can be exercised. Several helper/FIFO tests cannot execute their stand-ins. A minimal independent Node child-process probe reproduced `spawnSync /opt/node24/bin/node EPERM` and missing expected output from an asynchronous child. No listener, deadline, credential or policy check was weakened to compensate. |

**Expectation changes (complete list).** In `pilot/industrial-durable.test.ts`, the expected local work-order identifier `WO-1` became the exact identifier in the planner's signed creation receipt. The test also equates that identifier to the durable idempotency key and uses it for both performance calls; one order, restart/replay, status and once-only performance assertions remain. This follows UNS-8a: the outgoing identifier is a registered runtime mint. In both parameterizations of `pilot/delivery-correlation.test.ts`, the exact dispatched result expectation changed from prose `agreed result` to the declared code `agreed-result` (UNS-8a and UNS-9). No other existing assertion's expected outcome, count, reason, timeout or coverage was relaxed. Scheduling titles are fixture inputs, not relaxed expectations. The recursive classifier test supplies its known identifier explicitly; the held-plan engine fixture declares its fixed follow-up activity in its profile. New tests assert that real prose still stops. No test was skipped, disabled or deleted.

Fixture/provisioning changes also give the consumer reviewed issuer anchors and the contracting agent its authorisation issuer anchor. CLI help, runbook/profile documentation and integration drivers describe those inputs. This does not learn trust from an incoming token or from wallet-supplied keys. The pilot's synthetic proper names, catalogue labels and code displays are finite defined terms from installed configuration, not a wildcard permission for unknown names or prose.

**Identifier enforcement.** `freeStrings`, `dataClaims` and signed model-reading inspection use the same explicit `KnownContent` set. An identifier needs both an identifier declaration at its position and a well-formed IRI already in that set. A syntactically valid new IRI is text. `GateMemory` combines installed profile purposes/operations/sources/parties/defined terms, the principal's verified mandate and introduced identities, the current conversation's received and successfully emitted identifiers, task-context identifiers, and runtime mints. Integrity failures and replay-conflicting replacements cannot seed the conversation registry. Received challenge IDs/nonces, proposals, agreements, activities and evidence/context references are retained without making them accepted factual support.

The registry is persisted in `RuntimeState.knownIdentifiers`; older snapshots reconstruct only retained received/sent conversation IDs, histories, challenges, agreements and emitted bytes. An unsent core-selected conversation ID is not learned on restart (a regression test pins this). Admitted/context items retain their identifier sets, including structured principal task data and memory actually read. Global scope is reserved for runtime mints, installed identity/authority material and explicitly shared context; ordinary conversation knowledge is scoped to that conversation. Runtime `newId`, `mintIdentifier()`, fixed-namespace `identifierFor()`, agreement derivation and verification digests register their outputs when computed. Proposed outgoing values are not registered before release. Installed domain and fixed-metadata vocabularies are copied before any core callback; a core cannot widen a declaration by mutating its original configuration object. Protected compound terms never bypass signature, schema or recursive vocabulary checks. The packet inspector uses the same outgoing vocabulary and knowledge as the release check. Existing checked-store item, source and grant handles are snapshotted as explicit installed identifiers; a new direct-transmission regression releases a seeded store handle while its grant is live and sends nothing after expiry, without needing a loopback listener.

Tests cover the restricted fact encoded as `urn:x:the-balance-is-31400` in an identifier-typed field: it asks with `context-not-clear`, or stops when approval is forbidden, and never goes. They cover every known identifier source, foreign-conversation isolation, undeclared positions, actual runtime mints and conversation references across restart, declaration mutation, and invented identifiers/prose/bad signatures inside compound terms. Knowledge is distinct from source acceptance and permission to distribute the underlying statement.

**Data and outlines.** Biome formatted every `profiles/*.json` and `scenarios/*.json` file with the repository settings. Data-export examples use the `.json` suffix; a resolution/existence test distinguishes those exports from `scenarios/common`. The profile schema adds explicit installed identifiers. All five mortgage outlines and all three probing-counterparty outlines remain unchanged; reference tests compare them exactly. Builds regenerate the traces as requested; changed IDs/packet labels do not silently replace expected outlines.

**Validation and limitations.** Under Node 24, reference tests pass (75 tests); all five mortgage and three probing outlines compare exactly. The restored direct/file pilot workflows, including all 19 transmission tests, pass. A targeted run of scheduling, security, transmission, recovery, FHIR/health and mortgage/durable mortgage passed all 63 tests; the seeded-store release/expiry test also passes. Final combined command results follow. The socket/helper restrictions prevent a claim that every pilot test passed in this sandbox. The brief's socket-timeout cases and live loopback credential flows remain unverified here; no external service was contacted to replace them.

The failed groups are socket/loopback, FIFO/helper execution and the SPARQ stand-ins; the demo's sole failed case is its loopback conventional service. No source-level test skip was added. |

## Brief 11

This is a **partial implementation of the brief**. Parts 0 and 2–5 have implementations and runnable regression/case paths. Part 1 has the closed evaluator, both requested assurances and an agreement-bound assessment/store plan, but **the five mortgage migrations and removal of the old mortgage code are unfinished**. Health-referral's judgement primitive is present; its full catalogue paths remain blocked by the missing protected Solid reference implementation. No blocked path is reported as run.

Changes are uncommitted. No network, new dependency, live service, evaluation-directory edit or commit command was used. Verification uses Node 24.22.3 from `/opt/node24/bin` first on PATH. All people, issuers, organizations, credentials, payments and systems are fictional. Generated trace `commit` fields name the existing HEAD at generation time, not a commit containing these working-tree changes.

**Part 0 — anonymous release.** The gate previously allowed unknown prose when the task context was empty, and did not independently forbid an anonymous `propose` or `accept`. Both were real gaps. Anonymous requests now carry only vocabulary-typed public parameters, using installed identifiers rather than identifiers learned from private context. Parameter names must also be declared, including empty containers; object keys cannot hide data behind an array wildcard. Numeric/date query slots require closed installed values, and the conversation must have been generated by the runtime rather than selected by the core (even if that selected identifier was previously known). They cannot carry credentials, labelled personal data, free text, a core-invented conversation identifier or a substituted address. A commitment without a named principal stops with `representation-missing`. Explicit mandate prohibitions and profile recipient blocks still apply. The runtime binds the actual recipient actor and party to independently introduced keys. The actual message recipient is checked even when a logical action's beneficiary differs from the party receiving its message; that distinction matters for the pilot's internal authorization handover.

`test/anonymous.test.ts` exercises real runtime initiation and continuation, the unsigned public-read evidence/write-ahead path, task context, structured personal data, credentials, both commitment kinds, profile blocks, mandate prohibitions, invented conversation identifiers and encoded recipient addresses. A safe public query still goes with recipient, disclosure and evidence checks. Checks stop at a failure; later checks do not convert a refusal into permission. Existing F1, exact-message approval and identifier tests remain in force. This is the vocabulary/context gate's protection, not a claim to eliminate covert signalling through choices among permitted public query values.

**Part 1 — rules and assessments.** `src/derive/rules.ts` defines the nine closed templates, strict parameters, deterministic evaluation, source aliases and cycle validation. `loadProfile`, reference validation and the runner refuse cycles before execution. Rules may refer to another rule ID or output. The runtime supplies its own task memory; the core cannot pass a list marked “accepted”. Inputs must have matching admitted digests, the same subject and accepted named sources. Current freshness/expiry and permitted purposes are checked. Missing, unaccepted, foreign-task, wrong-source, gapped or otherwise unusable inputs produce no statement with `completeness-not-established`. Currency mixtures and zero denominators also produce no output. No general expression or query language was added. Dependency rules are evaluated recursively under the requested assurance; mixed-assurance chains that rely only on previously held intermediate outputs, and automatic selection of a least-disclosing rule/assurance, are not implemented. An unavailable dependency produces no output rather than a weaker assurance.

The derivation record contains the canonical rule digest and bound parameters, original input digests/issuers/claim types/methods/times/periods/currencies/expiries, intermediate derivation records, output digest, assurance, deriver and run time. Inputs are sorted deterministically. Results carry the oldest input time, original basis methods, shortest input expiry and an intersection of inherited terms. Inherited recipient registration requirements, duties and additional output prohibitions are enforced. The receiver verifies the rule digest and actual output; changed output, parameters, inputs, quality, expiry, chain or terms cannot substitute. Recomputed source acceptance is checked on the original issuers, rather than inferred from the deriver. By-source signatures must be from a source accepted for the rule, over its own inputs; a source runtime needs a separately host-installed source signing key pinned to its party. Both assurances preserve the original input quality. `proved`, `joint` and `attested` are explicit scenario blockers.

`AgentRuntime.assess()` requires the named agreement's permitted purpose, evaluates the installed plan, retains only the selected output plus its terms/quality metadata, removes input payloads and accepted support, records a structured `duty-discharged` receipt and prevents input reuse. Payload-free context tombstones retain source, subject, digest and restrictions: deleting a stored payload does not prove the core forgot it. Retained output restrictions also remain in context. No deletion of remote copies, process memory or a cryptographic proof of deletion is claimed. Tests cover actual agreement formation, missing/wrong purpose, retained output, erasure, inability to rederive, signed recomputation mismatch, aliases, delayed recomputation, every template, cycles, completeness, refused sources/methods and expiry. The statement encoding is the package's JSON encoding, not a new general RDF/CONSTRUCT implementation.

The mortgage replacement stopped before deletion. The migration files still contain just their opening exchange. The retained handlers also implement application/evidence admission and consent-bound `PurposeBoundStore` grants and internal access; the full driver adds discovery, the index/register chain, bank challenge/step-up and underwriting handover. The new assessment store plan does not replace those interfaces or the consent/onward-authorization construction. `personal-agent` still consumes the public mortgage handlers through its re-export. A shorter script that asserted their results would drop existing checks. Those missing data steps/operations and compatibility migration were not completed here; this is unfinished work, not an architectural impossibility claim. `domains/mortgage.ts`, `reference/mortgage.ts`, `reference/main.ts` and the other mortgage consumers therefore remain. All five data paths remain explicitly blocked. The original five real drivers still regenerate and match their current outlines; no claim of data migration is made.

**Part 2 — judgement, roles and approvals.** Profiles now have `personDecisions`. A matching action requires the principal or named role despite an autonomous mandate; the gate builds the request with exact parameters and digest. Roles are ordinary source-signed `organization.role` statements, admitted through the inbound checks. Signed mandate rules can require a count of role approvals and separation from the authenticated requester. The runtime verifies each exact-action signature against an independently pinned staff key, persists its proof, counts distinct people and keys, and rechecks role organization, validity, withdrawal and requester identity. Missing approvals ask with `approval-required`; invalid, withdrawn, expired, absent-role or self approvals stop with `not-authorised`. Owner approval cannot replace required organization roles. Approvals are consumed at release; they cannot authorize another effect with the same action digest.

`master-data-change` now runs from its profile/scenario, including both catalogue branches. The main path admits the bank confirmation and directory roles, obtains two controller signatures, then sends the approved request to the separately keyed ERP party. That party admits the confirmation, runs its own effect gate, records a local update and returns a signed result. The self-approval and email-source branches perform no update. Directory, bank and ERP replacements are declared in `standIns` and trace headers. The health profile names the clinician decision; its three complete paths remain blocked by `by-reference-solid`/protected-reference transport, not substituted by by-value data. The generic principal and role judgement mechanisms are tested independently.

**Part 3 — payments.** `pay` has a strict structured parameter schema. The payee/account must match an admitted `payee-account` statement and its current use/sharing terms. Invoice content alone asks for `payee-account` with `structured-form-required`; an explicitly rejected source stops, preserving its source reason. Core prose cannot become a payment. A changed instruction or adapter parameter set cannot ride on an authorization of different bytes. The ODRL adapter normalizes only explicit `pay` permissions to its supported compensation action; existing general `use`/`transfer` grants do not become payment grants.

The local case uses GBP; calendar months are UTC and amounts have at most two decimal places (100 minor units per currency unit), with no currency conversion. Calendar-month totals are integer minor units, keyed by rule and currency in durable runtime state. A release reservation is written before the outbox/adapter; replays do not reserve the same release twice. Per-action and cumulative excess ask with `limit-exceeded` only when the mandate permits a principal exception; otherwise they stop. An approved exception is exact-action and single-use. Tests exercise four £500 payments, a fifth held across restart, a new month, an atomic file-store restart, no-discretion refusal and exact single-use approval. The `pay-an-invoice` main path includes the separately keyed bank's inbound checks, payment/effect gate, recorded submission and signed result. Both catalogue branches run. The declared stand-in records submission only; it does not pretend to settle money. A standalone invocation returns this receipt as a correlated signed `deliver` with status/reference, as the invocation state machine requires.

**Part 4 — corroboration.** Claim rules can require a source count and independent controllers. Candidates must independently pass signature, source, terms and ordinary fitness checks. Only matching subject, claim, value and period count; duplicate issuers and controllers do not increase the independent count, and absent/empty control provenance contributes nothing. Disagreement or insufficient support asks with `completeness-not-established` and names the claim. Controller identity comes from the installed trust store, not a claim's own assertion. Corroboration is evaluated over the supplied admission batch; a quarantined cross-message collection/retry service is not implemented.

`false-but-signed` runs its main path and “Nothing fails” branch. The latter changes receiver policy through `profileChange`, relays the employer's authentic false statement through both agents' gates, and reaches go. Its scenario assertions examine both actual hash-chained records and require attribution of the exact credential digest to the employer. Embedded credential verification is now recorded, closing a record omission exposed by that assertion. The claim remains false; neither a signature nor source acceptance establishes truth.

**Part 5 — scoped pins.** The runtime holds a verified but unaccepted sender's message outside core context, without admitting it or learning its identifiers. The gate builds the principal's question from fingerprint, arrival binding and claimed actor/party. A principal-signed decision binds the request digest, message, purpose, party and expiry; yes records a pin and rechecks the original bytes from integrity onward, while no refuses with `source-unknown`. Pins persist, and neither another purpose, another key nor an expired scope is covered. Identical held-message retries do not generate repeated questions; conflicting held IDs and excessive holds fail closed. Verification material still comes from local reviewed introductions: a pin never imports a JWK supplied only by the packet, and an unverifiable key remains `unknown-key` at integrity.

`flat-share` now runs the offer, trust decision, recheck and signed acceptance, plus its catalogue unknown-source branch. Tests also cover refusal, a different purpose, expiry and absence of held input from core memory. Initial own-list entries have explicit purposes and expiry. A trust decision grants no role, factual authority or consequential action permission.

**Data, outlines and interpretations.** There are 13 runnable data paths and 18 explicitly blocked paths in `scenario-runs.json`; the five retained mortgage traces are additional legacy-driver artifacts, not successful data paths. Scripted cores propose messages and effects; runtime/gate code alone emits checks and decisions. Assertions only inspect results. Local payment, corroboration and pinned-list configurations have distinct signed configuration IDs, registered suite descriptions and matching trace headers; unsupported combinations do not silently fall back. Historical artifact filenames retain the `--baseline.json` suffix, while headers and index entries identify the actual configuration. Profile `standIns` is copied into every data trace.

Every newly executable outline was previously an empty blocked placeholder. These implementation-generated expectations are review artifacts, not independent normative conformance evidence:

| Outline stem (`--baseline.json`) | Entries | Why it is new |
|---|---:|---|
| `master-data-change` | 89 | Bank/directory admission, two role approvals, ERP request, gated update and signed result. |
| `master-data-change--branch-1` | 37 | A requester approval fails separation of duties before any ERP request. |
| `master-data-change--branch-2` | 25 | The email is not an accepted bank source; the proposed change stops. |
| `pay-an-invoice` | 67 | Payee admission and sharing terms, spend reservation, bank admission, gated submission and signed result. |
| `pay-an-invoice--branch-1` | 13 | The replacement account source is refused and payment stops. |
| `pay-an-invoice--branch-2` | 19 | The amount exceeds the autonomous limit and is held. |
| `false-but-signed` | 14 | Both signatures verify; disagreeing values fail corroboration. |
| `false-but-signed--branch-1` | 46 | Without corroboration every check passes; both records attribute the credential. |
| `flat-share` | 36 | Unknown trust is held, approved, rechecked and followed by signed acceptance. |
| `flat-share--branch-1` | 14 | Unknown trust is held before core admission. |

The five maintained mortgage and three probing outlines are unchanged. Mortgage migration expectation arrays remain blocked and were not overwritten with shorter scripts. The scenario recording script now preserves original data rather than writing validator defaults into unrelated blocked files. The single pilot fixture change restores the retailer handler's existing `contentProfile` when wrapping it for delivery-correlation testing; its assertions, correlation attacks and expected outcomes are unchanged. No test was disabled, deleted or given a weaker expected outcome.

The closed templates use numeric/boolean `value` claims and optional series `field`; aggregate completeness is a separately admitted signed `complete: true`, `forClaim`, exact-period claim. Period boundaries use the rule's declared calendar period; date endpoints are inclusive. Annualization is `sum × 12 / months`, with exactly that many distinct consecutive months. Arithmetic uses deterministic JavaScript numbers and sorted input digests; no unit conversion or general rule language is implied. The rule digest covers validated canonical rule data, including declared defaults; aliases do not rewrite it. Derivation depth is three (DER-9's default). Assessments keep payload-free context/record metadata after deletion so UNS-8 is not weakened. Requester identity is installed by an authenticated task host or taken from verified conversation context, never from a core assertion. An inbound source failure followed by the proposed action's stop is the catalogue's overall `stop`; inbound refusal itself is still recorded as refusal. Branch success may span admission and a later outbound go. Two brief-specific preflight outcomes are represented explicitly: unknown trust asks the principal inbound, and missing payment structure asks for more outbound. Neither can become admit/go by default.

No new reason code was introduced or found necessary for the implemented paths: all their reasons come from core section 9, and tests check that registry. The pilot's existing public extension-code union remains for API compatibility; this work does not migrate unrelated old scenarios. Anything not done is stated above: the mortgage data replacement/deletions, complete health-reference use cases, proof/joint/attested assurance and a cross-message corroboration collection service. Full-suite limitations are recorded with the final checks below.

**Final checks.** The command requested by the brief was run under Node 24. The non-environment pilot regressions found during implementation (internal authorization recipient versus beneficiary, and the wrapped public-query fixture's missing vocabulary) were repaired and independently rerun: all 16 targeted delivery-correlation/industrial/durable-industrial tests pass. The complete final pilot run returns the same 492/77/4 totals recorded in Brief 10. Failures are loopback listeners/port allocation, credential/helper/FIFO processes, SPARQ stand-in execution and their dependent timeouts; the log contains the corresponding `EPERM`/port-allocation errors. No deadlines or test expectations were relaxed to compensate. The four tests reported skipped follow failed setup; no source-level skip was added.

Reference: **104 passed**. Personal agent: **492 passed, 77 failed, 4 skipped**, two environment errors. |

Reproduction logs for this session are in `/tmp/brief11-complete-checks.log`, `/tmp/brief11-reference-final.log`, `/tmp/brief11-coverage.log`, `/tmp/brief11-agent-layers-final.log` and `/tmp/brief11-workspace-final.log`.

## Brief 12

This run follows the restricted checks in Brief 12: no personal-agent tests, coverage, full-workspace gate, network, dependency additions, commits or evaluation-directory edits. Node 24 from `/opt/node24/bin` is first on PATH. All fixtures are fictional. This section is updated after each part; generated trace commits identify the existing HEAD, not these working-tree edits.

**Part 6 — letters and structured upgrades.** Messages now carry bounded text or raw-byte content parts whose digests and bytes are protected by the message signature and checked before core admission. Relayed parts bind to an admitted source statement; outbound release requires that admitted binding and applies the existing free-text/context and issuer-terms checks. Parts enter context as unstructured data, never instructions. The host-installed scripted reader receives only one immutable part, has no runtime/action interface, and returns profile-declared code/number/date/term values; undeclared or invalid values are dropped. Readings retain agent authorship, `model-output`, implementation declaration, inherited terms and original digest. Accepted methods still govern admission. Required action claims supported only by unaccepted readings ask-more at fitness with `structured-form-required`, naming the claim, source and digest. The structured request destination is derived from original provenance; the hospital's answer passes ordinary signature/source/terms/fitness checks. Attempting to use the reading itself as a clinical entry stops with `method-not-accepted`. Principal-facing reading/content trace entries explicitly carry their labels.

Both `discharge-letter` paths now run under the distinct `plain-language-upgrade` local configuration. Its previously empty outlines now record the actual letter admission, quarantined reading, upgrade request/answer and gated entry, or the diagnosis refusal. Existing mortgage/probing/Brief 11 outlines remain unchanged. Interpretation: the JSON binding represents text as bounded UTF-8 strings and binary content as bounded arrays of byte values; both hash the actual bytes. Quarantine is a capability-free host callback, not an operating-system sandbox for arbitrary executable readers. Regression tests cover a patient relay with hospital source, re-signed digest mismatch, invalid dates, numeric strings, undeclared outputs and the refused diagnosis. Reference tests: **107 passed**. Scenarios: **15 run, 16 explicitly blocked**. No missing reason code was needed.

**Part 7 — standing emergency rules and notification duties.** Owner-signed mandates now accept the bounded `standing` structure. The runtime checks the authenticated conversation peer, a register-signed role bound to that peer's introduced actor key, the current admission/freshness/terms of the role and declaration, rule expiry, and the exact requested item set. The emergency declaration is a signed self-report, not a verified medical emergency. A missing declaration uses the ordinary mandate; an unregistered requester stops with `source-unknown`, excess scope with `excessive-scope`, and an expired standing rule with `expired`. Ordinary prohibitions and all other outbound checks remain active. Each released read creates separate durable principal/contact notification duties before outbox release. Notifications are signed messages through the ordinary outbound gate and the recipients' inbound gates; discharge records include message and time. Deadline auditing preserves missed duties as failures.

All three catalogue paths for `emergency-access` now run, including the registered-emergency branch and unregistered refusal. The three new outlines replace empty blocked placeholders; prior executable outlines are unchanged. Tests exercise whole-record scope, wrong actor key, withdrawn/stale roles, expired rule, absence of a declaration, both gated notifications and overdue duties. Interpretation: the requested `PT1H` notification interval is the bounded duration supported here; notifications record gated outbox release, not proof that a person read them. The local professional register is declared in `standIns`. Reference tests: **110 passed**. Scenarios: **18 run, 13 explicitly blocked**. No missing reason code was needed.

**Part 8 — references and the local holder.** Added a holder interface and a separately keyed issuer-only local resource-holder party. Holder-signed grants bind the reference, reader actor/party, exact item IDs and claim types, purpose and expiry. Readers sign timestamped, non-replayed item requests with their runtime key. The holder independently verifies identity and grant signature, checks item/purpose/expiry and optional resource digest, records every pass/refusal, and returns holder-signed statements with the grant's restricted terms intersected with the original terms. The receiver verifies the holder's source, exact response item and grant binding before ordinary admission. Missing holders give `lookup-failed`. Sender release verifies the grant, binds the approved item selection to its actual items, and counts all of its claim types as disclosure even though no values travel in the reference.

All three `health-referral` paths run under **`by-reference-local-holder`**, a distinct configuration explicitly replacing the catalogue's `by-reference-solid`. Profile and trace `standIns` state that this is not Solid access control. Main obtains a registered clinician's exact-action signature over the relevant-history selection, sends the reference and admits the holder's response. The full-record request asks for the clinician's decision; an unregistered referrer stops with `source-unknown`. These three outlines replace empty blocked placeholders. Catalogue branches give no check field: the full-record branch is assigned to the existing `limits-approval` person-decision check, rather than the placeholder's inferred `disclosure`; its required outcome/reason is unchanged. Direct adversarial holder tests cover identity substitution, another reader/item/purpose, expiry, digest/claim-type substitution and replay, and inspect actual read records. A disclosure regression rejects a whole-record grant without leaking it. Reference tests: **113 passed**. Scenarios: **21 run, 10 explicitly blocked**. No missing reason code was needed.

**Part 9 — organizations, credentials and current status.** Added an asynchronous document-resolver interface and registrar-served local fixtures. Registrar-signed identifier documents list current organization keys and delegated actor keys; introduced verification material must also be present in the current document, otherwise `unknown-key`. Organization mandates are issuer-signed credentials bound to the exact agent key, represented entity, roles, message action kinds, validity and status reference. Authority reads issuer-signed status, checks issuer/key/document binding and the profile's maximum answer age, and returns `mandate-withdrawn`, `lookup-failed`, `stale-data` or `representation-out-of-scope` as appropriate. Source statements with certificate status references undergo the same signed-status check with `status-withdrawn`. A supplier bank's authority is scoped to the actual legal entity, independently of who relays its statement.

All three `supplier-onboarding` paths run, with new outlines for the real onboarding, withdrawn certificate and other-entity bank account. Their signed configuration combines `mandate-credential` with `organization-document-local`; `standIns` identifies the registrar, issuer status services and company/bank/certificate sources. This is the local resolver specified by the brief, not a live DID-method implementation. The existing reviewed introduction supplies cryptographic key material; resolution adds the mandatory current-document check and does not accept self-supplied packet keys. Tests cover removed delegated keys, withdrawn mandates, unreadable/stale status lists, wrong status issuer, wrong entity/role/action scope and both catalogue refusals. Reference tests: **116 passed**. Scenarios: **24 run, 7 explicitly blocked**. Prior executable outlines are unchanged. No missing reason code was needed.

**Part 10 — ordinary selective disclosure.** Added issuer-signed credentials containing salted SHA-256 digests of individual claims and presentations revealing one selected disclosure per evidence item. The issuer signature covers common provenance and every commitment; each revealed claim includes its own ID, type, value and terms in its salted commitment. Inbound message integrity and statement admission verify every revealed digest, then apply the existing source, method, freshness and terms checks. Outbound evidence/disclosure checks inspect only revealed claims and still require matching admitted digests. Trace inspectors show revealed claims and their credential digest, without reconstructing hidden values. The implementation uses existing JWS, SHA-256 and Node cryptographic randomness; no dependency was added.

`right-to-work` now runs ordinary entitlement/degree disclosure and the passport-scan scope refusal under a distinct signed selective-disclosure configuration. These two outlines replace empty blocked placeholders. The original catalogue advertises proved assurance; this run is explicitly its ordinary-disclosure projection, with a separately named `hidden-validity-proof` path still blocked and no fabricated trace. Proving a predicate about the hidden work-valid-until date remains unavailable. No signed boolean is represented as a hidden-value proof. API issuance uses fresh random salts; reproducible fictional scenario artifacts deliberately use fixed fixture salts. No unlinkability claim is made. Tests cover hidden passport/date absence from the actual main trace, accepted revealed claims, changed value/type/salt/ID/credential signature, unknown/duplicate disclosure requests, independent fresh issuance salts and the blocked proof path. Reference tests: **118 passed**. Scenarios: **26 run, 6 explicitly blocked** (five mortgage migrations and the hidden-value proof). No missing reason code was needed.

**Part 11 — mortgage migration completed.** All five mortgage scenarios now execute from `profiles/mortgage.json` and their scenario files through the data engine, and reproduce the five maintained outlines **exactly**. No maintained mortgage outline was shortened or changed. Added closed handler installation and data steps for discovery/public index queries, anonymous return routing, the existing register chain, challenge retry, source-signed step-up attestation, exact approval, agreement invocation, consent-bound stores, internal assessment, source-signed decisions and receipts. Main retains the real bank challenge and nonce-bound retry; the fee case injects and re-signs the actual receipt, records that text as the proposal's origin, and still stops payment. The four other cases preserve their original success/refusal paths. The scenarios' old blocked expectation arrays were stale snapshots; they now contain the actual maintained outline arrays rather than newly recorded shorter expectations.

Mortgage intake now calls the installed closed annualisation rule against runtime-owned admitted memory. This exposed and fixed an evaluator omission for statements dated by period/issuedAt without an explicit asOf: the evaluator now uses the same conservative claim-time fallback as fitness, retaining the input time rather than inventing a current time. Consent-derived store grants, requester roles, source/subject/currency checks and store access controls remain in force. The assessment's ratio limit is profile data and reads actual granted store items and the actual application's agreed price/loan, with no zero/default input substitution. A separate underwriting-handover step checks current store permission, exact consumer agreement, onward recipient/purpose/field selection and item revisions, builds the attenuated terms from that permitted read, and runs both public underwriting handlers through their gates. Its protected outbox condition rechecks the same store on transmission. Regression paths cover the real underwriting round trip, marketing access, staff relabelling, excessive onward items and grant expiry.

Only after all five exact comparisons passed were the replaced `reference/mortgage.ts` and `reference/main.ts` driver bodies removed; their public entry points now call the data engine. Public mortgage operation handlers and the larger public UC-01 workflow remain compatible: the handlers are the checked closed implementations installed by data, and the larger workflow contains additional public pilot checks that these five reference scripts do not replace. Their exports were not deleted. The original array format of the five outline artifacts is preserved. Reference tests: **121 passed**. Scenarios: **31 run, 1 explicitly blocked**, the hidden-validity proof only. No missing reason code was needed. Broader allowed lint/build/typecheck/layer validation follows below.


**Final adversarial checks and interpretations.** A reading accepted by both source and method policy now has a positive regression through ordinary admission and action release; the runtime recognizes its own host-installed actor key without accepting packet-supplied keys. Content source attribution uses a source-signed binding actually admitted in this task and carried with the message; otherwise it uses the independently introduced sender identity, never an unverified represented-party claim. Reading terms preserve the original policy target through an inherited-terms wrapper. Principal-facing model-reading text retains its method and original digest. Raw byte-array transport and typed reader output use the same integrity checks as text.

Emergency tests bind scope to the actual signed request and the actual output item map, and cover omitted item lists, future roles and both withdrawal encodings. The standing mandate is signed by the patient; the patient's two agents share the patient's party key and have separate actor keys. Notification recovery recognizes an actual gated notification's recipient, purpose, rule and use, rather than an arbitrary message in its conversation; file-store restart auditing records missed duties once. Late notification cannot erase a missed deadline. Reference disclosure binds the selected action and exact grant digest as well as resource/items, so changing the action label, omitting items or substituting grant terms cannot bypass the clinical decision. Holder response terms bind the reader actor, purpose and expiry, and another actor cannot use that signed response. Certificates require status where the profile declares it, and organization action kinds come from the operation profile.

The full-record and passport branches now include the clinic's and employer's actual signed requests before the proposed disclosure, respectively. Their new outlines include those extra message/gate events; no refusal was replaced with a script assertion. Mortgage assessments record a structured receipt, input digests and output digest, without returning or retaining an extra assessment copy of the raw income/deposit inputs. The separately granted source store retains its own agreed retention and onward checks. Register-chain resolution preserves the baseline's reviewed, host-installed root/register trust configuration; no new live register transport or live DID method is claimed. The reading callback is an interface with no runtime/action capabilities, not a sandbox for arbitrary host code. These are local fictional simulations, not production medical, employment, banking or Solid access-control services.

Every new catalogue outline below replaces a previously blocked, empty expectation. Counts are included so the final artifacts can be reviewed directly; the full-record and passport outlines include the real requesting-party messages added during the final review.
