**Names and traces (Part 0).** Every existing scenario now uses its catalogue title; branch titles append the exact branch `what` text. Profiles contain readable names for every party and agent, copied into trace party/agent lists and used in runtime captions. Every data trace has `useCase` and `path` headers (one-based branch index and original `what`), including the mortgage variants’ shared `mortgage-in-principle` id. Step labels are complete sentences. Trace and profile JSON Schemas are regenerated. Existing outlines are unchanged. Metadata and caption assertions run across all executable cases; the mortgage trust test follows the bank’s new display name and retains its trust-route assertion.

**Additions and interpretations.** No engine/gate check has changed. Local reference holders remain distinct from Solid, as in Brief 12. All parties and fixtures are fictional. Scenario IDs and trace artifact filenames remain stable. The five mortgage variants retain their scenario identities while their header names the shared catalogue line; their historical step descriptions remain, with sentence punctuation.

**fit-note-to-employer.** The clinician signs the narrow fitness/adjustment statement. Both agents verify it and the reply through their gates. The branch changes only the requested evidence to the diagnosis; its care-purpose admission is retained. The earlier disclosure refusal is reported as a disagreement, without changing the gate or widening purpose policy.

**wearable-data.** The device maker signs only the weekly total. The insurer signs its requested use; the source’s use/distribution policy permits the discount purpose. Both agents gate delivery and acknowledgement. The branch changes the requested use to underwriting and propagates it to the proposed disclosure; the unchanged mandate stops it first. No enforcement inside an insurer that bypasses its gate is claimed.

**trial-eligibility.** The proof-dependent main path remains blocked: needs a proof system. Its completeness refusal runs a source-check preflight with baseline JWS selected and no outbound disclosure. A small general reliance rule requires an already admitted completeness statement for the exact claim, subject and period, in the same checked admission batch. The gate asks for more when it is absent or false. Tests cover a valid complete list plus missing/false support, wrong subject/period/claim/source/method, stale/expired support and forbidden terms. This checks completeness asserted by an accepted source, not the truth of absence, and creates no eligibility proof.

**cohort-count.** The minimum-size refusal depends on a hidden joint result, so it remains blocked with the computation. Input-attestation refusal needs an attestation verifier/binding for private joint inputs; none is implemented. No local per-hospital count or signed boolean replaces the hidden computation.

**hardship-request.** The borrower’s original signed letter travels as content. The borrower approves disclosure and the exact held text; the lender receives it and its labelled model reading, then asks its principal about reduced payments. The diagnosis branch changes only the proposed supporting item and asks for the borrower’s disclosure decision. The existing person-decision gate implements human approval in the named local plain-language configuration; no new usage-control suite is advertised.

**employment-reference.** The former employer signs the original paragraph about the candidate and approves its exact bytes. The new employer’s agent records a model reading with its own authorship and original digest. Reviewing the opinion and rejecting on a proposed model score both require the new employer’s principal. The branch’s score is an explicit proposal for a human decision, not an accepted fact or an automatic rejection.

**contract-wording.** The proposed clause travels whole under its author’s signature. The receiving company’s agent records its reading, but both reviewing the clause and attempting to accept an uncovered clause go to its principal. This is the existing local content-and-person-decision configuration at the negotiation boundary: no machine interpretation of reasonable/best endeavours, negotiated-meaning suite, or completed confidentiality agreement is claimed.

**introduction.** The proof-dependent main path is blocked. A self-made profile can be checked without the proof system: its real author signature verifies, but that person is not an accepted identity authority. The source refusal is recorded as a disagreement; no policy accepts self-asserted identity to manufacture the catalogue’s later check.

**joint-mortgage.** The multi-owner commitment remains blocked. Both independent precommitment refusals run under the ordinary signed configuration: persisted owner revocation, and issuer-prohibited distribution of the other applicant’s payslip. A small data-step addition exposes the existing revokeMandate runtime operation; it does not alter authorization or reason mapping. Regression tests pin the withdrawal disagreement and zero applicant disclosures, and the prohibited payslip transfer. Mortgage trace headers also now identify catalogue branches 1–3 explicitly; their outlines remain unchanged.

**insurance-claim-photos.** The policyholder signs bindings for the original account and synthetic image. The accepted garage signs the estimate; the insurer checks all three and routes claim review to its principal. The photo reader is a declared scripted stand-in, not an image-recognition claim. Changing the reading to minor damage cannot override the garage. The other branch changes only the estimate issuer; the policyholder accepts its own garage, while the insurer’s scoped source rules reject that garage. Both gate disagreements are preserved.

**expenses.** Uses the catalogue’s permitted merchant-signed structured receipt route. The employee’s agent files that actual receipt; finance verifies the merchant, amount and terms, then submits a reimbursement through the separately gated bank agent. The bank stand-in records submission, not settlement. The changed meal price rises from GBP 40 to GBP 75 against a GBP 50 autonomous limit and asks the employer’s principal. No photographed receipt is treated as merchant evidence; a photo-only variant would need source upgrade or a person.

**repair-request.** The signed tenant report is approved as exact text. This profile explicitly accepts the landlord agent’s labelled model reading only for inspection booking; the existing content-reader path admits it through source/method/terms checks and the local booking actually completes. GBP 180 exceeds the GBP 100 cap and asks the landlord. Operation-specific principal commitment caps extend the existing principal-limit mechanism to bounded nonpayment effects: missing/non-numeric/negative/over-precision amounts and wrong currency stop, and actual adapter parameters must equal authorized parameters. Tests cover a valid GBP 80 booking, rejected model method, excess, malformed amounts, wrong currency and adapter substitution. No payment rests on the reading.

**permit-application.** The architect signs the original SVG drawing and the separately structured height about the homeowner’s proposed extension. Both travel through source/terms gates; the planning office routes design judgement to its principal. Replacing the accepted architect claim with the model’s reading of the drawing stops with method-not-accepted. The drawing reader is a declared typed scripted stand-in, not a verified measurement or a real planning decision.

**price-negotiation.** The fictional seller’s GBP 80 offer and buyer’s exact acceptance pass both agents’ gates and form the same agreement on both sides. The buyer’s GBP 100 cap remains private signed context. The seller’s actual request for that maximum is followed by a proposed disclosure; the purpose-based disclosure gate stops it with excessive-scope. There is no claim about bargaining strategy, delivery or payment settlement.

**disruption-rebooking.** The accepted amount is the additional fare: GBP 20 against a pre-agreed GBP 50 margin, on the same fictional travel date. Both parties verify the offer and exact acceptance and retain the same agreement. Changing only the extra fare to GBP 70 asks the traveller with limit-exceeded. Signed protocol messages expire; no live seat hold or guarantee that a seat survives a human decision is claimed.

**telecom-fault.** The principal’s observation is kept as self-reported, and a structured procedural request and amber-light response traverse both agents’ gates. No credential pretends to certify the device. Changing the provider’s request to account settings triggers the separate with-approval mandate; the effect remains held with approval-required.

**meeting-notes.** The original signed meeting record says the date is tentative. Its typed model date note is accepted as model-output for drafting and retains the original digest and age after two weeks. A local draft completes; changing the action to committing that date asks the principal. The model reading supplies a calendar date only; the transcript retains the time and other words. Storing notes does not turn them into a team-approved commitment.

**no-training-terms.** The main article travels whole with its publisher-signed binding and terms: reading is permitted and use for the training purpose is forbidden. No training action is run. In the branch the only change is the prohibition’s encoding as unsupported action training. Current item-term evaluation does not reject that unknown action when deciding reading; the reading goes and the original signed terms remain attached. This is a disagreement and an unsupported-policy-term finding, not a claim to enforce training restrictions inside another system.

**onward-leak.** The dishonest outside-gate leak is explicitly blocked as an observation/accountability gap. The independently runnable honest-gate branch admits the bank statement for lending, receives an actual marketing request, then stops the onward distribution with prohibited. The profile recognizes the marketing purpose but the source’s unchanged terms forbid distribution.

**platform-income.** The joint total remains blocked. Requiring a complete total has an independent preflight: the worker can name the platforms supplied but cannot establish that all platforms are covered. The receiver requires accepted tax-authority completeness for that scope and period and asks for more when none is supplied. No joint computation, raw platform earnings or fabricated total is used in the refusal.

**visa-application.** The main eligibility/funds proof remains blocked. The refusal can check the bank’s authentic signature and attempt its source route without a proof. The consulate’s root has no chain to that foreign bank; current source resolution treats that as source-unknown and refuses it. The catalogue’s lookup-failed outcome is retained separately as a disagreement.

**small-business-loan.** Current organization documents and scoped company mandates bind the actual actor keys and loan operation. The register signs the director’s borrowing role, accounting/tax sources sign their records, and the real approval gate verifies that director’s exact GBP 25000 application signature before it leaves. The lender verifies the application and signs its acknowledgement. This is an application, not a loan grant or disbursement. Role and method branches retain their actual earlier/later gate reasons as disagreements.

**insurance-quote.** The risk-proof main path is blocked. Both refusals are independent preflights using ordinary source-signed statements: the actual insurer request cannot widen quoting to full birth/address details, and an incomplete claims register cannot support an absence claim over five years. No raw claims history or signed boolean is offered as a proof.

**salary-account-change.** The employee’s real representation and a bank-signed account statement support the local payroll update. Both attacks traverse actual integrity/authority checks and cannot populate payroll support. The runner can now strip a message’s signature to produce a genuine unsigned wire arrival; hostile injection values can use the existing safe whole-value references to retain the source credential while removing representation before explicit re-signing. Tests pin both inbound refusals and the stopped update. Neither addition bypasses a gate, and pre-existing outlines remain unchanged.

**tenancy-application.** The income/rent proof remains blocked. A signed six-month-statement request cannot widen the tenant’s mandate, and repeated structured counteroffers reach the runtime’s round limit in a separate fresh world. Neither refusal needs the absent proof system or disclosure of raw financial data.

**home-purchase.** The complete purchase is blocked by multi-party completion and escrow capabilities. Both independent source/fitness preflights run: the new deposit account is not bank-authoritative, and the registry’s current owner is not the seller scoped by the source rule. The attempted next steps stop with source-not-authoritative before recipient/evidence release. Neither payment nor title transfer is released.

**benefit-claim.** All three claims retain their distinct authoritative source and purpose-restricted terms and pass both agents’ gates; the office signs its intake acknowledgement. The household-method branch does not relabel the rejected fact as complete. Its final structured-form request differs from the catalogue’s method reason.

**change-of-address.** One owner-signed mandate names exactly twenty autonomous recipients. All twenty receive the same narrowly scoped address proof through their own gates and return signed acknowledgements. The twenty-first organization has a verified key but no autonomous recipient grant; adding it asks the principal with approval-required. The fixture register is authoritative only for this person’s address, and no live organization database update is claimed.

**degree-recognition.** The proof-dependent main is blocked. Its independent trust-route refusal checks the university’s real signature and the foreign employer’s root without producing a proof. The missing chain remains a source-unknown disagreement, as in the visa preflight.

**shopping-agent.** The core compares two fictional signed prices and selects GBP 300 under the GBP 500 budget, then the payment provider verifies and gates the actual submission and receipt. The checkout-fee branch changes the total to GBP 600 and asks the shopper. Changing only the instructed account cannot override the bank-signed payee/account binding and stops with not-authorised.

**cancel-subscription.** The subscriber’s scoped instruction and service-signed subscription record support the local cancellation. In the branch the service instead signs a three-month retention offer; the subscriber’s core proposes renewal, but no mandate permits it, so the gate stops with not-authorised. The synthetic adapter records local cancellation only, with no live subscription service claim.

**return-and-refund.** The merchant’s signed receipt and the customer’s original synthetic photo traverse both agents’ gates. The photo keeps its customer authorship and exact digest and is not a coded fault diagnosis. A local refund credit succeeds inside the merchant’s signed November return window; advancing the clock thirty days changes only timing and stops the same request with not-authorised. No cash settlement is claimed.

**age-check.** The anonymous age-proof main remains blocked. The identity-document over-ask can be refused before any proof is built; the merchant’s signed request cannot widen the age-check purpose to the held document and birth date.

**recall-notice.** The real manufacturer’s notice and source terms pass both agents’ gates. Replacing only its issuer with the attacker preserves an authentic signature by that attacker but fails the scoped manufacturer source rule. Attempting to act on the refused notice stops with source-not-authoritative, and no link is followed.

**trip-booking.** The complete reservation workflow is blocked. Actual signed hostile hotel-listing bytes enter context as untrusted content; the other-hotel proposal stops at the mandate and names its real released message as origin. A separate actual passport request during search reaches excessive-scope before any document leaves. No reservation or ticket is fabricated.

**energy-switch.** The complete switch remains blocked. The half-hourly-data refusal runs before any supply agreement: the quote purpose allows only an annual total, and the actual signed readings never leave the household’s gate.

**subject-access-request.** The requester’s owner-signed representation is sufficient for the fictional company’s configured intake. The company signs its actual returned copy and source attribution, and both parties check the round trip. A passport demand is an independent signed over-ask and stops with excessive-scope. This fixture does not claim to inventory a live company or certify completeness of its holdings.

**request-for-quotes.** Five independently keyed suppliers issue source-signed quotes under recipient-specific confidentiality terms. The core selects GBP 90, and both sides gate and sign the purchase order. Attaching a rival’s actual quote to another supplier stops with prohibited; changing the order to two units at GBP 180 asks the principal against the GBP 100 signing cap. No supplier fulfilment or payment is simulated.

**recycled-content.** The proof-chain main remains blocked. The independently checkable input-method refusal verifies the tier’s source and signature before rejecting the self-reported method. Authenticity does not upgrade the material claim to audited evidence.

**cross-department-request.** The finance source is accepted for its payroll claims, not as a universal authority. The people team’s planning request receives only the finance-signed team total through both agents’ gates. Changing the requested item to a named salary reaches the earlier scope refusal; being inside the organization does not bypass the gates.

**production-change.** The signed log remains untrusted content, and the source’s exact-text release is recorded. Only a named local restart operation is pre-authorized within the signed mandate’s maintenance window. A hostile log proposes another tool and stops at the mandate; advancing outside the window asks the principal. No live commands or production changes run in this sandbox.

**agent-handoff.** The actual attenuating A2A handoff and excess-authority branch are blocked. The independent data-terms refusal admits the customer’s address for delivery, receives a different-purpose request, then stops the proposed onward distribution with prohibited. This does not claim an A2A transport run or delegation credential.

**find-a-time.** The attested calendar answer remains blocked. Refusing the other person’s actual request for a private appointment reason needs no attestation: the declared meeting purpose excludes that claim and the gate stops with excessive-scope.

**sell-a-car.** The sale with escrow remains blocked. The register’s different keeper is independently checkable: its authentic entry is outside the seller-scoped ownership source rule, and attempting the sale stops with source-not-authoritative. No absence-of-finance proof or settlement is fabricated.

**content-licence.** The full agreement-credential/licence lifecycle remains blocked. Its independent onward restriction uses the photographer’s actual signed asset binding and recipient-specific terms. The proposed original image transfer to the sister company stops with prohibited before any bytes leave; no issued licence credential, fee settlement or remote expiry enforcement is fabricated.

**Progress.** New catalogue use cases handled: **59 of 59**; **0 left**. Current manifest: **95 runs, 17 disagreements, 54 blocked paths**. Every completed case was followed by the reference tests and full scenario regeneration. Part 0: package build and 128 reference tests passed; no pre-existing outline changed.

**Part 5 — RL-8.** `personMayRelyOn` is now a validated profile setting. Refused evidence remains excluded from handler arguments and core context; `ctx.refusedEvidence()` exposes labels and reasons only. No personal-agent suite, coverage or full-workspace gate is run.

**Part 0 — ordered branch divergence.** Comparison now consumes ordered matches of agent, gate, check, outcome and reason against the main run, and judges the first unmatched non-pass/admit/go branch decision. Repeated decisions consume separate main positions. Later decisions remain consequences. A branch whose non-passing decisions all match is explicitly `disagrees`, with “branch does not diverge from the main path”. Main-path judgement is unchanged. Interpretation: where the main path is explicitly blocked and has no run to compare, independently executable branches retain their first-decision judgement; no main trace is invented. Tests cover shared approval, a changed reason, an additional repetition and no divergence. **152 reference tests pass; scenarios: 166 paths, 103 runs, 9 disagrees, 54 blocked.** No gate, profile, scenario or maintained outline changed.

The requested rule reports these even where outcome/reason cells match. None was changed to manufacture agreement. No reason code was added.

**Part 12 — chains of authority.** The inbound authority check now verifies every credential signature against introduced keys, the named parent and holder key, represented subject, action/operation/purpose scope, validity attenuation, and every ancestor's delegation depth. Every link's signed status is freshly resolved for each check; a revoked middle link stops the chain. Accepted roots are explicit profile data. A register-signed current holder statement supports `any` and `all`; another holder's recorded objection yields ask-principal/approval-required addressed to the profile's named role, with the exact message digest, before the handler receives anything. The assessment is durably recorded. This branch ends at the request; no unsupported automatic resolution of the objection is claimed. The local operational mandate remains a separate limit from the credentials presented to the receiver.

All main paths and branches now execute for `carer-acts-for-parent`, `tax-agent`, `consent-for-a-child` and `agent-handoff`: **9 newly unblocked paths, all agree**. Their real signatures, current-status documents, scopes and item terms determine the outcomes. The handoff uses direct local messages, explicitly replacing the catalogue's A2A transport; it does not claim an A2A wire implementation. Registers and services are fictional declared stand-ins with independently introduced keys. The handoff's address terms now permit delivery sharing and prohibit marketing, rather than prohibiting every distribution even on the previously blocked main path. Its existing marketing refusal is retained under its original branch profile and world, with its maintained outline unchanged. Authority-chain policy explicitly lists the operations it governs; the old independent request uses its original signed representation. All outlines filled in this part were empty placeholders. No previously maintained outline changes.

Interpretations: scope is a closed set of operations, subjects and purposes; no expression language is added. Unreadable, missing or unusably stale status answers give lookup-failed; malformed or wrongly signed status gives representation-invalid. Excess delegation depth is an invalid delegation structure; broader action/scope/validity is representation-out-of-scope. The objection route currently accepts the register's record, one of the two sources permitted by the design; a separate receiver-authored objection feed is not implemented. All requested failure reasons exist already. Tests cover three links, fresh repeated lookups, missing links, signature/key/root substitution, absent/excess delegation, expiry, widening at both delegated positions, revoked middle status, stale/unreadable answers, wrong subject, holder-all insufficiency and objection routing. **156 reference tests pass; TypeScript typecheck passes; scenarios: 166 paths, 112 runs, 9 disagrees, 45 blocked.**

**Part 13 — single-use registers and escrow.** Added a separately keyed register with its own integrity, source, authority, terms and fitness checks; signed requests and status answers; reserve, redeem and release operations; expiring reservations; explicit permitted redeemers/purposes; and fresh issuer-role status resolution. A synchronous read/compare/save occurs after asynchronous verification, with no await before the durable save. FileStateStore's exclusive writer lock extends that guarantee across process restarts. The runtime itself reads current signed status and redeems before calling an effect adapter; a core cannot omit that step. A consumed or withdrawn item gives status-withdrawn, and absent/unusable status gives lookup-failed. The durable redemption records who, when and the exact action digest. A crash after redemption but before effect execution conservatively prevents another execution; no atomic transaction with an external effect is claimed.

The escrow hold states the conditions and accepted terms digest. Release verifies independently signed, fresh current ownership and finance-search statements, the matching signed completeness claim, explicit absence of finance, each statement's use terms, and both parties' exact-digest acceptances. The runtime binds the release to the installed item/amount/currency parameters. All paths now execute for `prescription-dispensing`, `invoice-finance` and `sell-a-car`: **6 newly unblocked paths, all agree**. Both pharmacies/financiers have their own keys and local effect adapters; no real dispensing, financing, settlement or money movement is asserted. The existing sell-a-car keeper-refusal outline is unchanged; the six newly filled outlines were empty blocked placeholders. Tests cover concurrent requests through separate service instances sharing a store, file-store restart before and after redemption, reservation expiry/release, withdrawal, forged requests, lapsed prescriber status, missing escrow condition/acceptance, mismatched terms, financed vehicle, stale search, changed amount and unreachable register. **161 reference tests and typecheck pass; scenarios: 166 paths, 118 runs, 9 disagrees, 39 blocked.** No missing reason code or added dependency was needed.

**Part 14 — general duties.** Added a durable duty executor for delete-by-time/on-event, event-triggered notify and inform-onward, sourced from signed mandates or formed agreements. Agreement duties are closed schema data; unsupported duties fail before an offer leaves or is admitted. The runtime builds the transfer chain from actual validated credentials in write-ahead releases, with recipient, agreement, duties and message digest; core assertions do not create links. Subject requests are checked before handler admission, and an onward requester must match the receiver's own previously recorded agreed transfer. Local deletion removes factual support and payload-bearing context, handler state, pending continuations and retransmittable outbox copies, retaining digest/label tombstones. Assessment deletion now uses that same operation. The legacy emergency notification interface remains a compatibility adapter using the common monotone deadline rule; the new executor handles general agreement/mandate duties and signed acknowledgements.

Both paths of `erasure-request` and `incident-notice` now run and agree: **4 newly unblocked paths**. Erasure follows two onward hops through ordinary outbound and inbound gates. A transfer without an inform-onward duty is recorded as undischargeable; the signed structured requester receipt distinguishes local deletion, pending onward requests and stop/no-agreement. A missing duty in the middle is preserved in the receiving company's audit, rather than pretending that all downstream deletion completed. Receipts report local actions and pending work, not externally observed deletion; no remote erasure, forgotten model memory or deletion of an immutable historical evidence ledger is claimed. Notices are separate gated messages to two customers and a regulator, with signed correlated acknowledgement deliveries. Durable audits distinguish pending, discharged, acknowledged and missed, and late acknowledgement retains missedAt. Crash recovery reuses an already-written duty outbox message instead of creating a new request.

Tests cover subject substitution before erasure, a two-link onward chain, a missing middle duty, pending-duty file-store restart, three real gated notices, a missed acknowledgement and a late signed acknowledgement. **165 reference tests and typecheck pass; scenarios: 166 paths, 122 runs, 9 disagrees, 35 blocked.** The four new outlines replace empty blocked placeholders; no other maintained outline changes in this part. No new reason code was needed. The structured partial receipt is released before the local subpart's stop decision, so the stop never permits a later data release.

**Part 15 — records, claims, precedence and originals.** Runtime-owned exports now contain the conversation's original signed messages, a contiguous retained hash chain, and an actor-key signature for every exported entry. Entry proofs are made at export; this does not claim that the pre-existing ledger had entry signatures at append time. Checks and their citing decisions are now recorded independently of a trace sink. Handler-authored records are marked `producer: core` and cannot masquerade as gate checks, decisions or message records. The separately keyed local forum verifies each segment independently, each entry proof, the hash/sequence chain, each original message and its supported embedded statement/representation signatures, decision/check references and acceptance/terms binding. It then compares sent/received message digests, reports a discrepancy or a recorded breach, or signs that the records do not settle it. It never calls either core. A missing response at the durable deadline is record-unavailable; a late answer does not replace that ruling.

Record disclosure is an exact owner-approved effect through the releasing runtime's outbound gate. The adapter binds actual parameters, and the runtime supplies disclosure labels from its own factual memory, so the core cannot omit them. The forum's signed record request binds respondent, conversation, case and deadline; its answer must bind that same request. The protocol uses signed local control statements through the declared adapter rather than a network regulator endpoint or a new binding implementation. Exported messages are conversation-scoped; the retained ledger range is exported whole to preserve chain continuity, so minimal multi-conversation ledger redaction is not implemented. Signatures and a consistent chain do not establish that unrecorded events did not occur, or detect a withheld tail without an independently held later commitment. The scenarios do not claim either guarantee. Record bounds are explicit (2 MiB envelopes and bounded entry/node/string counts), not disabled checks.

`fee-dispute` now uses the actually accepted fee and the lender's later signed bill under that agreement. The forum identifies the bill for 995 against agreed fee 0; no bank debit is simulated. Its gap branch submits a deliberately dishonest signed segment and is refused with record-unavailable. `onward-leak` forms the actual restriction agreement, releases the original bank statement to the lender through both gates, and then copies the payload into a separate host-side marketing-system store **outside the lender's gate**. An independently keyed observer reads that store and signs its observation. The lender's record contains its acceptance and no onward release; the forum's breach decision uses those records and the observation. The old honest-gate prohibited-transfer branch and its outline are preserved. No event pretends that the outside copy was prevented.

Profiles can now declare source order and whether source rank or freshness takes priority for a claim. Conflicts are limited to the same subject, claim and period; only otherwise fit admitted statements participate. The selected statement remains factual support; the other is retained as superseded in gate-held state, excluded from handler evidence, and unavailable as action support. An explicitly unordered policy asks for more, naming both statements. **Stopped scope under the outline constraint:** precedence is opt-in per claim; legacy profiles without a precedence declaration keep their previous behavior, including the deliberately authenticity-only false-but-signed projection. Applying ask-more to every unordered legacy conflict would change that maintained path and its outline. That default rollout was therefore stopped rather than changing the outline or weakening its existing assertions; the new profiles and the explicit unordered-policy test exercise the implemented rule. There is no dedicated canonical unordered-conflict reason; completeness-not-established is used with both identifiers. Unsettled forum allegations use evidence-missing with a structured explanation. No new reason code was added.

`credit-file-dispute` selects the creditor's current statement over the agency's copy, forms a signed correction agreement and discharges its deadline-bound notification through a signed notice and acknowledgement. The non-answer branch ends with the profile's agreement deadline at stop/no-agreement; it does not pretend an agreement formed. This is a synthetic correction workflow, not a live credit-file edit. `records-to-new-practice` reads the patient's note, clinician's entry and old estimate as original source-signed statements inside holder-signed envelopes. The receiver verifies each original against its own source and keeps the grant's purpose/expiry restrictions in addition to the original's terms. The holder cannot rewrite an original and borrow its author's standing. The local holder explicitly replaces the catalogue's Solid transport; no Solid endpoint is claimed.

All eight paths of these four cases now execute, **seven newly unblocked**. Fee, credit and leak paths agree. The conflicting-allergy branch **disagrees honestly**: the profile chooses the clinician and marks the patient's note superseded with a passing fitness check; the catalogue expects ask-more/superseded-by-better-source. There is no new non-passing branch decision, so Part 0 additionally reports “branch does not diverge from the main path”. No ask-more was manufactured to hide that mismatch. Tests cover tampering, a sequence gap, a message on one side only, missing check references, mismatched accepted terms, non-answer/late answer, both precedence/freshness orders, an unordered conflict, different periods, original-source standing and a holder that alters and re-signs an original.

**Final review and validation.** Additional review tightened root scope and missing-middle-link handling, exercised valid all-holder authority, added signed issuer withdrawal after reservation without erasing a prior redemption, and bound escrow completeness to the search period. The tax case now exercises the actual three-link register/accountant/firm/agent path; the register is explicitly a fictional source for client-specific engagements. The carer residence branch changes the requested action while retaining the father as subject. Refused evidence still never reaches handlers: `ctx.refusedEvidence()` retains labels/reason only. **173 reference tests pass; package build (including schema drift and both trace generators), typecheck and scenarios pass.** Package lint passes with three warnings and fifteen informational diagnostics. No personal-agent suite, coverage, full-workspace gate, network operation, evaluation-tree edit, dependency addition or commit was performed.

Final manifest: **166 paths = 138 executable + 28 blocked; 128 runs, 10 disagrees, 28 blocked**. This brief unblocks **26 paths**. All paths named by Parts 12–15 are executable; the allergy branch is their one disagreement. The nine Part 0 disagreements remain recorded, not bent. The remaining 28 blocked paths belong to other capabilities and retain explicit blockers. All **112 pre-existing maintained outline files remain byte-for-byte unchanged**; 26 new outline files and matching scenario expectations describe the newly executable paths. The part-by-part totals above were recorded after each part's tests/scenarios; all changes remain in the working tree.

**Part 16 — multi-party agreements and plans.** Added a separately keyed local agreement coordinator with signed represented requests, immutable definitions, distinct named parties and required acceptors, exact terms digests, durable partial signatures and holds, withdrawal, current host-resolved mandate withdrawal, ended/replaced status and signed status resolution. No actor can accept for another party. Completion verifies every required acceptance and every condition's original signature, explicitly accepted source, subject, value, method, freshness and use terms. Failed conditions, missing acceptances or withdrawn authority void the pending agreement and release every hold, retaining the signed-so-far record and failing step. Restart resumes the same immutable definition. A completed credential carries every party's own acceptance proof, representation and a status reference. Each party collects its own copy through its gate. A receiver outside the conversation checks every proof, a fresh signed status, the presenter's signed audience-bound presentation and the requested asset, purpose and period. It rejects missing/tampered proofs, outsiders, ended agreements and out-of-terms uses.

All paths now execute for `joint-mortgage`, `home-purchase`, `energy-switch`, `content-licence` and `trip-booking`: **five newly unblocked main paths**. The four existing non-travel branch sets agree. Travel's existing hostile-listing branch retains its honest disagreement: the unmatched context-not-clear approval precedes its later not-authorised stop; the passport-search branch agrees. Pre-existing branch preflights remain precommitment refusals, with their maintained outlines unchanged. Newly implemented main paths use the new coordinator; they do not replace those refusals with different stories. Interpretations: the declared fictional coordinator also owns the local conditional escrow/reservation register. Its holdings, plan total (integer minor units, keyed by the immutable plan/agreement ID) and final confirmations share one durable snapshot, using the same read/compare/save discipline as Part 13. This models local escrow and reservations, not an atomic transaction across live providers or actual money movement. The conveyancer admits the land register's signed search-completion statement; switching admits the signed switch date. All provider parties finally accept after holds. The airline alone receives the passport through ordinary disclosure and inbound gates, after the plan completes and solely for ticketing. No raw passport travels during search or reservation. Credential encoding is the reference engine's multi-JWS JSON encoding, not a claim of VC wire-format interoperability. No missing reason code or dependency was needed.

Tests cover a missing signer, changed digest, impersonation, withdrawal, revoked authority, outsider presentation, missing/tampered signatures, asset/purpose/period limits, absent/current/ended status, cumulative-budget refusal, failed/successful conditions with rollback, and a file-store restart between signatures. **179 reference tests pass; scenarios: 166 paths, 133 runs, 10 disagrees, 23 blocked (143 executable).** All pre-existing maintained outlines remain byte-for-byte unchanged; five new outlines replace previously blocked placeholders.

**Part 17 — approved dataset questions.** Added a separately keyed fictional dataset holder and holder-signed grants bound to the reader's actor and party, dataset, purpose, exact closed rule/protocol list, minimum distinct people, related-query cap and expiry. The holder verifies the reader's signed represented request and the grant itself, runs the existing closed rule evaluator over its private source data and emits only a signed by-source derived answer. No row, person identifier or per-person input commitment is returned. The derivation uses a source-private salted snapshot commitment and names the grant digest and durable query number. The receiving agent independently checks the holder, rule, input-source standing, quality and terms before admission. Input source acceptance does not authorize row disclosure: the receiving purpose admits the count only.

All three research-data-access paths now execute and agree. Outside-protocol/purpose requests stop with purpose-not-permitted, over-cap requests with limit-exceeded, too-small cohorts with prohibited and expired grants with expired. Interpretation: every approved protocol attempt consumes one per-grant slot, including an answer later suppressed or an evaluation failure; an unapproved request consumes none. All approved cohorts share the same counter, so changing a request ID, rule or cohort cannot reset it. Changing a signed grant body under the same ID also cannot reset it. Distinct contributing people are checked for each input kind, excluding unrelated or out-of-period observations. The counter is saved atomically before answer signing/release; a crash can consume a slot without delivering an answer, never deliver an uncounted one. This is a closed approved-query and minimum-cohort policy, not differential privacy or a claim to prevent inference from arbitrarily permissive grants. The scenario's one-query cap deliberately prevents the approved full-cohort/minus-one differencing attack; a separate test covers an insufficient cohort. Tests also cover every reason, no returned rows, concurrent queries, a file-store restart and grant-body substitution. **184 reference tests pass; scenarios: 166 paths, 136 runs, 10 disagrees, 20 blocked (146 executable).** Three new outlines replace empty placeholders; no maintained outline changes. No missing reason code or dependency was needed.

**Part 18 — an email-only correspondent.** Added a scripted mailbox binding with no agent, keys, core or gates, and an explicit unsigned-email configuration selecting email, email-endpoint, plain-language-upgrade, human-approval and pinned-list. The agent checks owner-signed, purpose-scoped, expiring endpoint pins before every send and receipt. A runtime-built footer identifies agent/model authorship, the represented principal and the structured-reply URL. Every send, including one permitted autonomously by the underlying mandate, is held for exact-action principal approval; the gate shows all text and the footer. The actual adapter parameters are bound to the approved parameters, copied before asynchronous work, rechecked on resume and written ahead before delivery. A second message needs its own approval. Task-context disclosure and issuer-terms checks run for all content, including unaccepted sources, with context-not-clear visible to the principal. Approval is for those exact bytes, address and purpose; it does not create a general email permission.

A pinned reply is admitted only as unsigned address-only unstructured content. It creates no fact, instruction or agreement. The quarantined reader returns profile-declared values as model-output readings with the original content digest; prose claiming acceptance or giving instructions cannot execute an action. A proposed binding action still requires the principal; payments retain the existing structured payee/evidence requirements. Other addresses and expired pins are rejected before context admission. Interpretation: the local transport supplies the From address; this establishes only the endpoint string, not a verified human, authenticated mailbox control or email anti-spoofing. There is no live SMTP/MIME implementation or real email sending. The configuration explicitly declares that the prose has no cryptographic integrity; the local principal's pins and approvals remain signed. The mailbox is listed as a declared stand-in and is absent from trace agents.

Both no-agent-on-the-other-side paths now execute and agree. Tests cover no approval, approval/denial, an additional send, the exact footer shown to the principal, a purported acceptance, an instruction, a proposed binding effect, another sender, an expired pin and restricted task context. **189 reference tests pass; scenarios: 166 paths, 138 runs, 10 disagrees, 18 blocked (148 executable).** Two new outlines replace empty placeholders; no maintained outline changes. No missing reason code or dependency was needed.

**Part 19 — code-list mapping.** The inbound gate now resolves signed, versioned mapping tables for profile-selected receiver actors when an otherwise acceptable claim uses an unfamiliar code list. It verifies the registry's introduced key and scoped source authority, exact table ID/version and lists, table validity and fresh issuer-signed version status. The local mapped statement names the mapping digest/version, original statement digest, original code, original source and full method/time/period/expiry quality, and has a derived method and explicit deriver. The source remains the home practice; the registry never becomes the author of the clinical fact. Terms and original fitness must pass before mapping, and the profile must also accept the resulting derived method. The audit distinguishes the locally mapped record from the source-signed original; it never attaches the original author's signature to rewritten bytes. The safe admitted-statements API exposes only gate-held admitted task facts.

The care-abroad no-mapping branch now runs and agrees, without attestation. Missing routes/codes give ask-more/unsupported-term naming the code; an unreadable registry or unusably stale status gives lookup-failed; a withdrawn version gives status-withdrawn; tampering gives bad-signature. Approximate mappings either ask-more/confidence-too-low or are explicitly admitted with reduced-standing labels, as the profile specifies; neither changes the original source's quality. Interpretation: mapping is performed locally after verification of a public table fetched through the document resolver. The local clinical source rules replace the catalogue's federation trust route, as declared in standIns; no federation discovery or remote clinical endpoint is claimed. This part leaves the attested main path blocked for Part 20, and does not mislabel a recomputed mapping as attested. Tests cover exact, both approximate policies, absent codes, withdrawn versions, bad signatures, unavailable/stale registry status and stale/unaccepted original methods. **193 reference tests pass; scenarios: 166 paths, 139 runs, 10 disagrees, 17 blocked (149 executable).** One new outline replaces an empty placeholder; no maintained outline changes. Insurance's licensing key is accepted for licensing facts and the register's key for claims facts. Neither planned proof binds each issuer to each fact. Applying the brief literally therefore requires both keys to be authoritative for all those kinds, which they are not. The gate asks for better evidence; neither issuer's authority was widened to force success. The run records the failed source check and excludes the result from factual support. The hidden-validity branch's old expectation is likewise not rewritten to match its new, explicitly accepted limited attestation.

**Interpretations and implementation boundaries.**

- `JsonValue` is the existing core `Json` type. Verifier input/output and protocol JSON are opaque; the engine never infers a real backend API from an installed module. Literal optional imports deliberately suppress only TypeScript's absent-module diagnostic, not a verification failure. Runtime discovery still finds no adapter. The layer guard requires those imports to be string literals.
- Query predicates are the fixed encoding `urn:claim:<percent-encoded claim kind>`. Numbers, booleans, membership, canonical dates and bounded conjunction/disjunction conditions compile to receiver-owned `SELECT DISTINCT` text. Restricted `NOT EXISTS` implements the authenticated-RDF medication exclusion. The recycling rule uses the minimum certified fraction for the same fictional lot across three independently keyed tiers; it is a conservative stage guarantee, not an invented mass-balance calculation. Actual backend query admission and RDF encoding cannot be validated before delivery; a real adapter must refuse unsupported queries rather than substitute another query or a signed boolean.
- Tenancy uses one payroll statement and a fixed GBP 3000 threshold, three times the stated GBP 1000 rent. No sum is assigned to the limited result relation. Opinions and itineraries keep their distinct authorship/method; admitting them does not convert prose into an automated judgement. Direct local trust/source rules replace live federation discovery. Named/pseudonymous fictional actors are used for the gate fixtures; anonymity, cross-merchant unlinkability and holder binding are not established.
- Source-signed completeness is admitted before the medication/claims snapshot that relies on it. A canonical credential-digest agreement is independently fixed in receiver policy for the doubles. Its format is explicitly `engine-credential-digests/1`; it is **not** a native authenticated-RDF dataset commitment.
- **Stopped native preparation:** the supplied `ProofSystem` interface has no dataset-commit/preparation call, while the planned native CLI requires `commit` over native signed hashes, policy and salt before the verifier can agree the dataset. With opaque credentials and no delivered CLI, the engine cannot compute or translate that native commitment. No native commitment or native `complete` binding is fabricated. A native adapter must receive an independently agreed native commitment in receiver-owned policy, or a preparation interface must be added under review. Consequently the claim that *only* three thin adapters remain is not yet justified for the native verifier-agreed datasets. The canonical double-only agreement does not close this gap.
- Receiver nonce records bind rule ID/digest, subject, purpose, query, policy and accepted assurances. Issuance is saved before transmission. Source verification consumes the nonce only after successful proof/binding/source checks; later terms or fitness refusal does not undo successful proof verification. Unsuccessful verification leaves it pending. Restarts retain consumption, and seeded identifier reuse cannot overwrite a prior nonce. Two independent complete engine runs produce identical nonce-bearing events. Future adapters must also make synthetic issuance reproducible from their key seed, or persist the issued credentials; random reissuance must cause an exact cache miss, never a relaxed match.
- Step 2 requires a registered verifier and matching proof policy, with the explicit accepted-attester fallback of step 6. Insufficient confirmed bindings then fall back to an accepted attester or the `reported-by-subject` reliance label. That label is stored separately from the holder's immutable signed bytes; subsequent fitness/derivation checks use the gate's label. A proof never gains a subject binding from the outer signature.
- DER-21 holds raw inputs when an actually admitted request and installed profile permit a narrower proof or joint contribution. Each input's existing use and distribution terms must permit the purpose/recipient; proof output terms inherit those restrictions. `minimumGroup` is an explicit local item-term field, enforced before raw sharing or joint participation. No new interpreter for lighter `derive`/`aggregate`/`anonymize` ODRL grants was added; unfamiliar policy actions continue to fail closed. This is a conservative restriction of the existing policy subset.
- The profile's semantic rule digest and the protocol's digest of fixed `SessionParams` are distinct. The receiver stores their association before the open message and compares the entire session, roster and protocol digest. The compute-only party is explicitly on that roster. Contributions, received shares, partials and outcomes survive restart. Repeated local contribution/partial calls reuse their saved values. A sender-boundary check verifies outgoing outcome evidence again, so a core-substituted numeric outcome cannot bypass `finish`'s refusal. No `simulateSession` call exists.
- Toy proofs share an HMAC key and prove nothing. Toy shares are plaintext and provide no privacy. Their totals use the signed partial sums, and their verifier checks every roster signature, share binding, partial and total; a missing input never becomes a smaller valid total. Real aggregation still does not prove the holders' counts are true, guarantee delivery, prevent excessive collusion or solve repeated-query inference. No such guarantee is claimed.
- Email's canonical comparison uses the string itself for strings and canonical JSON for other values, case-insensitively, at length four or more. It requires an actually matching explicit policy prohibition, including inherited ones. Default/no-permission terms still go to exact-text review. Mapping's locally interpreted fact and source original have separate identities and digests, and the original proof remains available in gate-held state/audit for later verification.

**Reason codes.** No new core reason code was added. The specialist label `input-not-attested` is not a core reason: it maps to `derivation-not-verified`, retaining the backend explanation and party at fault. There is no `proof-cache-miss` code; an unavailable precomputed native proof uses `evidence-missing`. There is no `less-disclosing-method-available` code; attempted raw input release uses `excessive-scope` at disclosure with a DER-21 explanation. Unissued/consumed nonces use the existing `replay-conflict`; unsupported policy terms keep `unsupported-term`.

**What each real adapter must supply.** The exact common TypeScript contracts are in `src/derive/verifiers.ts`; the signatures below are repeated so this report is self-contained. All implementations advertise `kind: "real"` only when they actually call a real verifier over real evidence. Discovery alone never registers one.

```ts
type ProofBinds = "rule" | "issuers" | "subject" | "fresh" | "complete";
type ProofOK = { ok: true; released: JsonValue; binds: readonly ProofBinds[];
  issuerKeys: readonly JsonObject[] };
type ProofFail = { ok: false; reason: string };
issuerKeygen(seed: Uint8Array): Promise<{ publicKey: JsonObject; secretKey: JsonObject }>;
issue(input: { issuerKey: JsonObject; subject: Iri; claims: JsonObject;
  status?: JsonObject }): Promise<JsonObject>;
prove(input: { query: string; credentials: readonly JsonObject[];
  policy: JsonObject; nonce: string }):
  Promise<{ presentation: JsonObject; result: JsonValue }>;
verify(input: { expectedQuery: string; presentation: JsonObject;
  policy: JsonObject; nonce: string }): Promise<ProofOK | ProofFail>;
```

| Method | Function | Required real behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| `sparq-zk-result/5` | `issuerKeygen` | Map the seed to the delivered test-issuer key generator; return actual Baby Jubjub/commitment public and secret material in JSON, with fixture issuance reproducibility. |
| same | `issue` | Encode the declared claim values/datatypes into the delivered method-bound v5 credential format and actually issue them, including the supplied status reference. Preserve the issuer identity/key association independently in receiver trust. |
| same | `prove` | Call the offline WASM/Noir/bb successful-result implementation over the exact compiled query, issued credentials, receiver policy and nonce. Include hidden numeric/date members where needed. Normalize its released result to the exact statement value without exposing hidden inputs. |
| same | `verify` | Reconstruct the expected public inputs from **receiver-owned** query, key/status policy and nonce, call the real verifier, and report its actual public issuer keys and released value. Maximum bindings are `rule`, `issuers`, `fresh`; never `subject`. Translate backend failures to `ok:false`, without replacing verification with a Boolean attestation. The engine owns durable nonce consumption. |
| `sparq-authrdf/1` | `issuerKeygen` | Supply the actual Ed25519/RDFC issuer keys required by the native issuance format, seeded reproducibly for these synthetic fixtures. The delivered CLI key-format/issuance contract must be checked; no undocumented keygen command is assumed. |
| same | `issue` | Call the native `issue` implementation or its actual library equivalent to produce signed authenticated-RDF credentials, not package JWS credentials relabelled as RDFC. |
| same | `prove` | Invoke `authrdf_cli` through `execFile`, with real `r0vm`/guest pins, exact query, policy/key table, native dataset authority and nonce; return the genuine presentation and normalized released value. This runs in the `proofs` preparation command, not an hour-long synchronous scenario action. |
| same | `verify` | Call native verification now over the exact receiver request. Report actual issuer-table keys and `rule`, `issuers`, `fresh`, plus `complete` **only** for an independently agreed native `VerifierAgreed` commitment. Never report subject/status binding. Use an isolated backend nonce-check store as needed so the engine's durable consume-after-success rule remains authoritative. Reject the engine-only fixture commitment until the native preparation gap above is resolved. |

The joint adapter has these exact types:

```ts
interface SessionParams {
  sessionId: string;
  statistic: "count" | "sum" | "count-and-sum";
  minimumGroup: number;
  minimumContributors: number;
  threshold?: { statistic: "count" | "sum"; operator: ">=" | "<="; value: number };
  roster: readonly { party: string; publicKeys: JsonObject; computeOnly?: boolean }[];
}
type JointOutcome =
  | { type: "release"; count?: number; sum?: number; evidence: JsonObject }
  | { type: "threshold-verdict"; verdict: boolean; evidence: JsonObject }
  | { type: "below-minimum-group"; evidence: JsonObject }
  | { type: "too-few-contributors"; evidence: JsonObject };
generateKeys(seed: Uint8Array): Promise<{ publicKeys: JsonObject; secretKeys: JsonObject }>;
newSession(params: SessionParams): Promise<{ session: JsonObject; ruleDigest: string }>;
contribute(input: { session: JsonObject; party: string; secretKeys: JsonObject;
  count: number; sum: number }):
  Promise<{ contribution: JsonObject; shares: Record<string, JsonObject> }>;
receiveAndPartial(input: { session: JsonObject; party: string; secretKeys: JsonObject;
  contributions: readonly JsonObject[]; shares: readonly JsonObject[] }):
  Promise<{ ok: true; partial: JsonObject }
    | { ok: false; reason: string; partyAtFault?: string }>;
finish(input: { session: JsonObject; contributions: readonly JsonObject[];
  partials: readonly JsonObject[] }): Promise<JointOutcome>;
verifyOutcome(input: { session: JsonObject; outcome: JointOutcome;
  contributions: readonly JsonObject[] }):
  Promise<{ ok: true; contributors: readonly string[] }
    | { ok: false; reason: string; partyAtFault?: string }>;
```

| `sparq-aggregate/1` function | Required real behaviour |
|---|---|
| `generateKeys` | Produce the delivered Ed25519/X25519 key material, keep secrets with that party, and expose only public keys in the receiver-fixed roster. |
| `newSession` | Normalize the public session and its SHA-256 rule digest over all session parameters, including thresholds, minima and compute-only roster entries. |
| `contribute` | Make real Pedersen commitments/range proofs, additive shares and one encrypted share per other roster party, and sign the complete contribution using this party's own roster key. Normalize `party` attribution for the message handler. |
| `receiveAndPartial` | Check **every** contribution and this party's received shares, then produce only this party's signed partial; otherwise return the actual reason and party at fault. Never invoke another party's function. |
| `finish` | Combine actual verified contributions and partials. Normalize the four delivered outcome variants to the union above. Refusals contain no numeric total; threshold verdicts reveal only their Boolean result. |
| `verifyOutcome` | Verify every roster signature, range proof and commitment opening, the outcome's session/digest and the complete contributor set. Missing/unsigned/off-roster contributions fail, never reduce a total. Report the actual contributing party IDs. |

The joint adapter also supplies one explicit `assumption` sentence describing the delivered semi-honest privacy/collusion limit; it is included in the signed derivation record. Each future adapter is wired through the currently empty list in `src/derive/adapters/index.ts`. The external native preparation gap, actual backend format/query compatibility, and real cryptographic/performance checks remain unavailable; no tarball was installed, no native process/proof was fabricated, and no synthetic result was published as a real run.

The optional **research-grade, unaudited `sparq-aggregate/1`** adapter calls the real `sparq-verifiable-aggregate` CommonJS/WASM package at commit `61989f5`. `scripts/install-sparq.mjs` authenticates the complete tarball against SHA-256 `9832ef937efbcc8970db3f70ec4e72e3384a867f3ecb40a1be81ea002a6a6830` before creating an installation, then extracts the authenticated bytes into ignored `.sparq/sparq-verifiable-aggregate/`. The salary branch retains its original roster, minima, finisher, input values and catalogue expectation. The original double-only script skipped partials because it expected a refusal. It now performs the required private exchange and delivers the actual release through the requester's inbound verifier; its observation assertion records `release`. It does not change the catalogue's `expect`. An initial implementation interpreted the branch as two total roster slots; that interpretation was discarded before final recording because it would change the experiment to obtain the expected refusal. The independently tested genuinely two-slot session does produce a verified `too-few-contributors` refusal.

Each of the six recordings carries the real method, package name, package commit and authenticated tarball hash in `generated`, a research/unaudited notice, `assurance.kind: joint`, and the README's coalition limit: an input stays hidden from a coalition missing at least one other party, beyond its own inputs and the totals; contributors can reconstruct both totals, including on refusal or a threshold verdict. Fictional local organisations have independent identity and aggregate keys. No truth, disjointness, reliable delivery or protection against repeated-query inference is claimed.

**Caller responsibilities and seam changes, by file.**

- `src/derive/adapters/sparq-aggregate.ts` and `adapters/index.ts`: real optional loading; strict, unknown-field-rejecting schemas for every package result and all wire types used; conversion between the seam's numbers and canonical unsigned decimal sums; exact wrapper/native session agreement; ordered contribution/index/party binding; actual `verifyOutcome`, not simulation. Package errors retain their code, check and roster index. Results outside JavaScript's safe integer range and the seam's unsupported `<=` threshold operator fail closed. No `simulateSession` call exists under `src/`.
- `src/derive/verifiers.ts`: optional contributor-local state and audit opening, and finisher party/keys/local state in the protocol interface. The package needs these explicit private inputs; the previous interface's `finish` had no way to supply them. Separate durable consumption flags distinguish the bare outcome message from its attached derived claim.
- `src/mechanisms/joint.ts`: local shares and audit openings remain only in that party's private state store; public step outputs omit them. Compute-only roster members attest zero inputs. Each party recomputes the opened session digest before contributing. Duplicate opens/contributions and simultaneous/repeated outcome operations fail with `replay-conflict`; rule mismatches refuse. Only the partial encrypted to the current roster recipient is accepted. The finisher receives its own keys/local partial, never another party's secrets. Backend failures become gate refusals with public failure metadata.
- `src/core/limits.ts`: finite per-kind bounds for open, contribution, private partial and outcome payloads, sized from the roster. The ordinary envelope/depth/node bounds still apply before these checks.
- `src/gates/inbound.ts`: the requester and every participant independently verify each identity-signed aggregate-key/compute-role binding against pinned identity keys and source policy. Missing/mismatched bindings fail `source-not-accepted`. Bare outcomes, including refusals, additionally run the real verifier inside source admission before reaching a handler/core. Private partial routing and per-kind payload bounds are checked there too. Existing gate ordering is retained.
- `src/derive/verification.ts`: proof nonces are claimed before awaiting verification and released in `finally`; successful verification alone consumes them. The same protection covers joint derived claims and bare outcome messages. Corrected evidence remains usable after a failed verification. Tests run competing copies concurrently and require exactly one admission.
- `src/runtime/agent.ts`: the requester's own runtime draws 32 random bytes for each real session. It authenticates and fixes the complete roster/rules before use; sender-boundary outcome verification remains in place. Ledger records use the same private-message logging projection as traces.
- `src/mechanisms/joint-privacy.ts`, `src/trace/recorder.ts`, `src/trace/types.ts`: private partial payloads and nested signed wrappers are replaced in logs/traces with digest summaries; routing still names the peer. Secrets, shares and audit openings are excluded. The real-session test observes private values only in test memory and searches every trace from all six paths; only a Boolean leak result is asserted, so failed tests do not print secrets.
- `src/gates/outbound.ts`: the existing failed-evidence caption now includes the backend reason, check and roster index; gate decisions are unchanged.
- `src/engine/run.ts`, the three joint profiles/scenarios, their six new traces/outlines and `traces/index.json`: requester-created sessions, independently signed key-binding statements, zero-input compute attestations, recipient-specific partial dispatch, readable step captions, and real package provenance. New outlines/expectation arrays are implementation-derived observations, not independent conformance evidence. Previously maintained outlines are preserved.
- `scripts/run-scenarios.mjs`, `scripts/recorded-joint.mjs` and `scenario-runs.json`: store each real trace's SHA-256 plus `recordedWith` provenance. Without the package, the six rows retain their recorded status (including the salary disagreement), verify the exact trace bytes and do not rewrite those traces or outlines. Missing provenance or a hash mismatch is an error. Command output distinguishes retained recordings from live execution.
- The three closed-world proof profiles/scenarios, discovery and the test double now use `sparq-noir-closed-world/0`; native executable/guest probing is removed. Their reason is exactly **“Blocked: needs a Noir circuit for absence and totals, not yet delivered.”** The other six proof paths keep `sparq-zk-result/5`. Brief 21's **native dataset commitment/preparation gap remains open against the new method**: the engine's credential-digest fixture agreement is still not a native dataset commitment, and no delivered circuit/preparation contract closes it.
