The first run passed, giving 258 tests including the oracle. The snapshot is never refreshed by tests or build scripts.

**Part 1 checkpoint.** Added `vocab/answerable.ttl`, `vocab/answerable.shacl.ttl` and protected JSON-LD 1.1 `vocab/context/v1.jsonld` under the required namespace. The model covers the message kinds, core envelope/body/record structures, reason individuals, gate names, verdicts, outcomes and assurance/proof bindings. It reuses VC, ODRL, PROV, DPV and schema.org terms and retains the register’s weaker mappings. Every declared class/property/individual has a label and definition; every property has a domain/range; all classes have target shapes. The context has no implicit vocabulary/base, uses IRI/time/decimal coercions and kind-scoped bodies. Structural and bidirectional reason-register tests pass. At this checkpoint **262 reference tests pass**, including the unchanged oracle; scenarios report **151 executable (10 disagree), 15 blocked**, hence 141 agree. Remaining integration and model constraints are recorded below with the final status.

**Part 2 checkpoint — partly done.** `src/wire/` now admits bounded JSON-LD with four hash-pinned offline contexts, rejects inline/foreign/nested foreign contexts and undefined keys before RDF conversion, validates the selected root with the workspace-installed SHACL validator, and returns only a Store and root IRI. Writing compacts/frames the dataset in a stable key order and independently checks graph equality to prevent silent loss. One canonicalise/digest/sign/verify interface implements JCS + Ed25519 JWS and RDFC-1.0 + `solid-vc`'s `eddsa-rdfc-2022` Data Integrity suite. Tests cover context/key refusal, shape/path diagnostics, pre-parse limits, tampering, independent pinned keys, graph digests, the 64/65 blank-node boundary, stable output, and graph-equivalent reserialization. **273 tests pass**, including the unchanged oracle; scenarios remain **141 agree, 10 disagree, 15 blocked**.

Two requested details cannot be represented as stated. The standard Data Integrity context makes `proof` a named graph; Turtle cannot carry that graph. `toTurtle` explicitly refuses it, and a separate TriG round-trip test proves lossless verification without claiming the requested Turtle test passed. JCS ignores JSON key order and whitespace: those changes alone cannot invalidate a JCS signature. The JWS round-trip test changes the compacted JSON structure while retaining identical RDF and correctly fails; pure key/whitespace changes correctly verify. The full W3C ODRL context was unavailable offline, so only the SDK's exported ODRL subset is bundled under its own Answerable context IRI, never under a falsely claimed W3C URL. Runtime gate/carriage integration, embedded VC replacement of legacy evidence strings, and the two real mortgage Data Integrity traces remain unfinished; no scenario configuration is registered as running this new suite before those gates actually use it.

**Part 3 checkpoint — partly done; the pipeline migration is not complete.** `codegen.config.json` and `scripts/federation-codegen.mjs` read the one normative ontology and shapes. The full generation attempt is rejected with **119 admission errors** (108 at the initial Part 3 checkpoint, before adding kind/type consistency constraints); their exact subjects/messages are retained reproducibly in `src/generated/full-admission.json`. The script projects the complete, unchanged supported `GrantRecord` class (not a weakened shape) and generates it through `@jeswr/federation-codegen`; `src/core/types.ts` now aliases that generated wrapper. The lock also pins the complete original inputs. Regeneration into a temporary directory compares every artifact byte-for-byte, and the workspace `check:codegen` passes for eight generated projects.

Hand-written RDF views in `src/model/nodes.ts` are `MessageNode`, `ParticipantNode`, `AudienceNode`, `OperationNode`, `BodyNode` and `EvidenceNode`, plus the infrastructure base `RdfNode`. The generator rejects `MessageShape`, `ParticipantRefShape`, `AudienceRefShape`, `OperationRefShape` and all eleven `*BodyShape` classes with `[unsupported-constraint] sh:closed: no runtime projection` and `sh:ignoredProperties: no runtime projection`. Message/body/evidence links additionally produce `sh:nodeKind: only IRI and Literal node kinds are projected` for `BlankNodeOrIRI`, and `sh:node requires a projected composite link`. `BodyNode` is a shared, incomplete view of those eleven body classes, not eleven secretly hand-generated manifests. `RdfNode` only supplies infrastructure cardinality/term checks. No generator source is changed, no unadmitted generation is enabled, and the full SHACL remains enforced by the wire reader. Tests construct a message exclusively with wrappers, serialize/re-admit it, verify live dataset reads, and refuse unsafe, ambiguous and cross-dataset links.

**Unfinished integration, by file/function.** The gates and mechanisms still consume the legacy object model. `InboundGate.verifyIntegrity`, `assessClaim`, `checkStatement`, `admitStatements`, and `Admission`'s source/authority/terms/fitness checks; `OutboundGate.check`, `checkMessage`, `seal` and their disclosure/approval helpers; the statement/mandate/authority/reference/derivation mechanisms; engine handlers and runtime operations have not been migrated. `Message`, the body interfaces, `EvidenceItem`, `ActionIntent` and `Agreement` remain object types. Stores still retain their old message/evidence objects. `RDF-PIPELINE-STATUS.json` lists the precise file, function, line and rule for every remaining mechanically detected violation, including runtime functions; it is an audit report, not an exemption baseline. `scripts/check-rdf-pipeline.mjs` uses the TypeScript compiler API, resolves renamed JSON type imports, checks wrapper aliases and foreign JSON parsing, and permits only bounded function-specific exceptions in `runtime/agent.ts`. The allow-list is deliberately empty: this guard **fails on the current pipeline**, with no gate/mechanism exemptions. Its isolated fixture tests prove detection and bounded runtime exceptions, rather than claiming the package passes. At this checkpoint **278 reference tests pass**, including the unchanged oracle and generated drift; scenarios remain **141 agree, 10 disagree, 15 blocked**.


**Part 4 checkpoint — partly done.** `src/model/labels.ts` reads labels from the bundled ontology and per-run profile/party/fact RDF through SDK wrappers. `src/engine/run.ts` supplies operation labels, party names, purpose labels and statement labels; `src/engine/schema.ts` and the exported domain-profile schema permit a presentation-only `labels` map. Profile additions contain labels only. `TraceRecorder.bind` renders `caption` and `rule.text` and retains replaced identifiers as exact `refs[].id` values. Existing `rule.id` values stay intact. Ordinary approval questions use purpose and party labels. A test inspects all 151 produced traces: **zero captions and zero rule texts contain an HTTP(S) or URN identifier**. Four older caption assertions now assert readable names plus exact references; their decision/reason assertions and the decision oracle were not weakened.

The packet work is **not started**: `TracePacket` and `packetView` in `src/trace/packet.ts` still expose the existing object-based layers, without `jsonld`, `context` or `turtle`. Adding a superficially converted graph before the actual carriage/gates use it would not meet the requirement that it be the same graph. The blanket chat assertion is **not satisfied**: 29 produced `chat.text` entries still contain identifiers. These include exact original text/action parameters shown for digest-bound approval, XML namespace text in original SVG content, email reply metadata and trust-pin questions. `LabelIndex.annotations` deliberately leaves that exact review content intact; its preservation has a test. Finishing this needs an explicit presentation of original content separately from generated sentences, plus labelled trust-pin questions. No passing blanket chat test or packet RDF attachment is claimed. The explorer is unchanged. The final reference tests and required scenarios pass as recorded below.

**Final part status.** The oracle is done. Part 1 is **partly done**: the published message/core vocabulary and shape/context tests work, but the one complete model of everything gates read is unfinished. `ItemTerms`, admitted facts, legacy statement claim payloads, mandate/approval semantics, policy operands and several non-message enums still need complete shapes and scoped contexts; reused metadata classes currently have open structural placeholder shapes. Arbitrary profile payloads are not accepted by silently inventing a vocabulary. Part 2 is **partly done**: the independent JSON-LD boundary and both cryptographic forms work in tests, but carriage, legacy signed evidence/mandates, approval digests and authenticity configuration dispatch do not call them. `src/suites/registry.ts` has no running `authenticity-data-integrity` configuration, and `mortgage-main`/`mortgage-core` were **not** run as Data Integrity traces. Part 3 is **partly done**: the generated record, fallback views, drift test and failing migration guard exist; **no gate or mechanism has been completely migrated**. Part 4 is **partly done** as described above. The central objective, RDF through the whole running pipeline, is not achieved in this run. The unfinished integration is a limit of this implementation/run, not evidence that the design cannot be built.

The function-level mechanical inventory in `RDF-PIPELINE-STATUS.json` has **234 failures across 101 file/function pairs**, with **zero exemptions**. It is a lower-bound inventory of typed/parser violations, not a claim to detect every object access. In addition to that inventory, the unchanged runtime message path in `src/runtime/agent.ts` includes `loadMandateInto`, `selfCheck`, `outboundVerifier`, `checkTransmission`, `admitStatements`, `receive`, `onDescribe`, `onDescription`, `onPropose`, `onAccept`, `onClosed`, `onInvoke`, `onDeliver`, `onReceipt`, `admitToCore`, `applyReply`, `performReply`, `executeAgreementPlan`, `performPlanMessage`, `authorise`, `standingAccess`, `agreementDutyRules`, `installAgreementDuties`, `recordItemTransfer`, `erasureAuthorised` and `eraseRequested`; these still use legacy message/intent/agreement objects. `src/engine/handlers.ts:installHandler` still constructs JSON-based handlers and `src/runtime/operations.ts` still defines JSON operation/core contracts. The exception allowed by the brief for the remainder of runtime does not cover this implementation's unmigrated gates and mechanisms; the empty allow-list and failing guard make that shortfall explicit. Stores have not been migrated to datasets/N-Quads. No R2 store/profile/scenario redesign was begun.

**Hand-written class accounting.** `MessageNode` corresponds to rejected `MessageShape`; `ParticipantNode` to `ParticipantRefShape`; `AudienceNode` to `AudienceRefShape`; `OperationNode` to `OperationRefShape`. Each has the admission errors `sh:closed: no runtime projection` and `sh:ignoredProperties: no runtime projection`. `BodyNode` is the incomplete shared view for `DescribeBodyShape`, `DescriptionBodyShape`, `ProposeBodyShape`, `AcceptBodyShape`, `ChallengeBodyShape`, `RefuseBodyShape`, `InvokeBodyShape`, `DeliverBodyShape`, `ReceiptBodyShape`, `UnsupportedBodyShape` and `IndeterminateBodyShape`, each rejected for those same closed/ignored-properties constraints; linked operation/body fields also have the node-kind/composite-link errors listed in the Part 3 checkpoint. `EvidenceNode` corresponds to `EvidenceItem`'s credential link, rejected with `sh:nodeKind: only IRI and Literal node kinds are projected` and `sh:node: sh:node requires a projected composite link`. The complete source subjects and diagnostic wording are retained in `src/generated/full-admission.json`, including the later `sh:hasValue` rejections. `RdfNode` is infrastructure, and `LabelIndex` is a presentation index, so neither has a domain-class admission error. `CredentialNode` is reused from `solid-vc`, not reimplemented. There are no other hand-written domain wrappers.

`pnpm install --offline --ignore-scripts --filter @jeswr/agent-reference...` failed while resolving an unrelated existing `federation-trust` Git dependency: pnpm tried `git ls-remote`, but SSH was unavailable, before a connection could be made. No package was downloaded. Local links to already installed workspace/cache packages and the agent-reference importer in `pnpm-lock.yaml` were then updated; existing dependency resolutions were preserved. This is not reported as a successful clean install.

**Mandate package assessment.** `@jeswr/agent-mandate` was read but **not used**. Its VC issuer/principal/agent binding and ODRL content fit the basic spec-14 model. It is not a drop-in adapter for the current runtime: its verifier requires a status URL ending `/agent/mandates/status.ttl`, whereas the scenarios use the existing arbitrary signed-status references; its evaluator returns `confirm-required` for unmatched/unsupported rules, whereas the current gate's exact refusal/reason behavior must remain oracle-stable. Its approval helper uses P-256/`ecdsa-rdfc-2019`, while this brief's new suite is Ed25519/`eddsa-rdfc-2022`. Those are concrete integration differences, not a claim that spec 14 inherently rules out the package. The integration was left unfinished; **no substitute VC+ODRL mandate implementation was completed**, and legacy mandates still run. The existing `solid-odrl` evaluator was retained.

**Design and SDK issues encountered.** The Data Integrity/Turtle and JCS/key-order issues described in Part 2 are actual conflicts in the requested tests; neither is papered over. The full W3C ODRL context was unavailable offline, and its SDK subset has its own pinned IRI. The streaming JSON-LD parser behind `fetch-rdf` rejects repeated identical protected type-scoped contexts inherited in nested messages/VCs. `src/wire/document.ts:documentDataset` first uses the standard `jsonld.expand` with the same pinned loader, then parses the context-free expansion with `fetch-rdf`; original limits run first and finite expansion byte/depth/quad limits apply afterward. The Answerable bundled context references the bundled VC context rather than copying its protected scoped definitions, avoiding protected-context origin conflicts. Duplicate JSON keys (including escaped spellings), nulls, relative IRIs, numeric decimal amounts and unsupported directional strings are refused explicitly rather than silently losing their meaning. Directional literal canonicalization is unsupported by the installed SDK. These are bounded refusals with tests, not weakened admissions. A final semantic review also separated `awp:operator` (the party running software) from `odrl:operator` (a constraint comparison) and kept all local shape IRIs in the Answerable namespace; the wrapper round-trip test checks the actual predicate.

**Counts and immutable oracle.** The suite grew from **257 tests in 36 files** to **287 tests in 44 files**. The snapshot test passes unchanged after every checkpoint and in final validation. The starting snapshot's SHA-256 is `ed5c8a6d8fb201f44540572c0a836c449d830213c9f9274bfad65633895e4840`; it was not regenerated after model changes. The final manifest remains **166 paths: 141 agree, 10 disagree, 15 blocked**. All **151 executable paths** were run by the required scenarios command after each checkpoint and at close; blocked paths remain blocked, and no new cryptographic scenario is relabelled as executed. The pre-existing report bytes and maintained outlines remain intact. All work is uncommitted.

**Final status: partial; Brief 22b is not completed.** The unchanged oracle and all 322 package tests pass. Full JSON-LD carriage, complete proposal/credential graphs, packet fields and the two Data Integrity runs remain unfinished. The migration guard has 15 hard core-model failures and 213 reasoned function/signature exceptions (228 total findings). The final audit below supersedes the provisional green allow-list checkpoint.

Offline implementation, no commits. Starting reference tests: **301 passed in 46 files**, including the immutable 157-path oracle. The checked-in inventory predates the merged joint work: it records 234 findings; a fresh check against the exact starting HEAD is recorded separately below. No oracle, maintained outline, optional `.sparq/` installation, binding, suite-registry or scenario-runner edit is made.

**Part 1 checkpoint — partly done.** Foreign JWS decoding/verification now lives in `src/wire/jws-compact.ts`; `src/mechanisms/jws.ts` preserves the public exports. Bounded JSON parsing lives in `src/wire/bounded-json.ts`, with the existing limits-module export preserved. Untrusted diagnostic/JWS inspection lives in `src/wire/inspect.ts`; trace, gate, runtime, mechanism and hostile-fixture callers no longer call `JSON.parse` on those foreign bytes. Cryptographic checks, legacy bytes, schemas and decisions are preserved. This is boundary consolidation, **not completion of JSON-LD carriage**: `AgentRuntime.emit` still signs legacy messages, `InboundGate.verifyIntegrity` still admits legacy JWS payloads, and exact-document suite approval digests are unfinished. The existing evidence strings cannot satisfy `EvidenceItemShape`'s embedded-credential link, and unmodelled domain fields cannot pass strict context admission; switching these to the new wire reader at this checkpoint would change decisions. Reference tests: **301 passed / 46 files**, unchanged oracle included. Required scenarios passed (157 executable paths; 146 runs, 11 disagreements, 9 blocked). `check:rdf-pipeline`: **233 findings, no exemptions**, still failing. Subsequent checkpoints below supersede this provisional count/status.

**Part 2 checkpoint — partly done.** `InboundGate.verifyIntegrity` now reads sender/key, declared configuration, organization key binding, audience, reply reference and time-window operands through `MessageEnvelopeNode`. This class is explicitly an envelope projection, not a complete wire Message graph. Legacy content/evidence checks and `IntegrityResult.message` remain object-based. `fitnessOf` and `claimTime` now require `StatementQualityNode`; inbound admission, derivation, outbound freshness and standing-access callers construct that view at their existing legacy boundaries. Methods, validity, issue/as-of times and calendar periods are live RDF reads. Four adversarial/live-view/round-trip tests were added. The ontology, context and shapes now cover issue time and temporal coverage; codegen was rerun, recording **123** full-model admission errors. `StatementQualityNode` projects rejected `StatementSubjectShape` (BlankNodeOrIRI value/terms/period links); `StatementPeriodNode` projects `StatementPeriodShape` (closed/ignored-properties constraints). These are adapters around legacy ingress, not a claim that messages/credentials/core now share one dataset.

The attempted SDK ODRL graph migration was **stopped and reverted** when the unchanged oracle failed: `policyToRdf` rejects `rule.target = urn:scenario:age-check:document` because it requires an HTTP(S) target, whereas the existing evaluator accepts that target. No target, policy, scenario expectation or oracle was changed, and no second ODRL serializer was written. `solidOdrlEngine.evaluate`, `evaluateItemTerms`, mandate/representation/source admission, legacy credential verification and `admitStatements` therefore retain their old policy/body readings. They are not described as migrated. The final checkpoint package test command passes: **305 reference tests / 47 files plus 17 package evaluation-fixture tests / 1 file**; it does not run the `eval:e0` command. The 157-path oracle passes unchanged. Required scenarios passed: **146 runs / 11 disagreements / 9 blocked**. Pipeline check: **232 findings, zero allowances**, still failing. The one additional reduction from Part 1 excludes `.test.ts`/`.spec.ts` source files from the production-source guard; their local fixture/spec-file parsing is not a foreign-party runtime boundary.

**Part 3 checkpoint — partly done.** `OutboundGate.release` (the actual name of the brief's action `check`), `describeAction`, `exceededLimit`, `approvalRequest`, `strongerApprovalRequest` and `checkEffect` read scalar operation/action/target/purpose/recipient/conversation operands through `IntentScopeNode`. `checkMessage`, `seal` and `unsignedAllowed` read envelope operands through `MessageEnvelopeNode`. These are explicitly partial projections of the rejected ActionIntent/Message shapes, not complete proposal graphs: parameters, disclosed claim values, mandate policies, evidence and full-document approval digests remain legacy. `src/engine/handlers.ts:installHandler`, scripted/model core proposal construction, `AgentRuntime.admitToCore` and the operation/core contracts have **not** been migrated. No scope projection is sent or attached to a packet as if it were the complete signed document. Integrity failure extras were narrowed to their real digest/binding fields, removing four generic-JSON signature findings without renaming JSON model types.

After this part: **305 reference tests / 47 files + 17 fixture tests / 1 file pass**, including the unchanged oracle. Required scenarios pass: **157 executable; 146 runs / 11 disagreements / 9 blocked**. The checker now passes as a **bounded migration ratchet with 228 explicit remaining violations in 94 scopes**, not as evidence of a completed migration. Every allowance has an exact source file/scope/rule/count and an explanatory reason. `RDF-PIPELINE-STATUS.json` is the per-scope ceiling: neither a new scope nor an increased count can be allowed. Unused, oversized, duplicate, wildcard and reasonless allowances fail; `--write-status` only records a passing reduction. Direct triple construction cannot be exempted. Production scanning excludes test/spec files. Fixture tests cover growth, stale entries and invalid allowances. The fresh starting-HEAD inventory was **251**, versus **234** in the older checked-in inventory; consolidation, the test-source exclusion and narrowed diagnostics reduced the fresh count to **228**. The new ceiling was bootstrapped from that fresh reduced inventory, including the already-merged real aggregate adapter missing from the old inventory. The retained core interface exceptions mean the original Brief 22 model-type requirement remains unmet.

**Final audit of the Part 3 guard — the earlier green checkpoint is superseded.** The first allow-list was too broad: it exempted 15 core model declarations. Brief 22 requires those declarations to become real wrapper types, and Brief 22b permits function exceptions, so type-level exemptions would hide an unmet requirement. They have been removed and the checker now rejects attempts to exempt `object-model` or `direct-quad`. The **final guard fails on 15 core declarations**, with **213 bounded exceptions in 79 other scopes**. There are **228 total remaining findings**, down from the fresh starting count of 251. This implementation **does not complete Brief 22b** or its required passing final pipeline check. The README states that prominently. The mechanical list is a lower bound on object reads; unchanged typed legacy bodies can also be read in functions without a `Json` signature.

**Subsequent model/check refinements.** Source admission's ordinary issuer, subject, claim-type and method operands in `InboundGate.assessClaim` now read the credential/subject metadata views. Its non-IRI legacy identifier fallback is explicit; cryptographic claim values, status, derivations and the returned claim remain legacy. Validity and issue time belong to the credential; method, as-of time and period belong to its subject. Missing issuer metadata is not replaced with an invented issuer, and a partial graph fails full VC admission. Unknown subjects remain unidentified nodes. Calendar-period context is scoped to `StatementSubject`, avoiding a global meaning for unrelated `period` fields. The final new hand-written classes are `MessageEnvelopeNode` (rejected MessageShape), `IntentScopeNode` (rejected ActionIntentShape's BlankNodeOrIRI parameters), `StatementQualityNode` (rejected VC subject/proof BlankNodeOrIRI links), `QualitySubjectNode` (rejected StatementSubject value/terms/period links) and `StatementPeriodNode` (rejected closed/ignored-properties constraints). Exact admission messages remain reproducible in `src/generated/full-admission.json` (**123 errors**). No generator admission was weakened. Outbound `disclosure`, `dataTerms`, `checkEmailContext` and `commitmentShape` also read scalar action scope through wrappers; their values/terms/parameters still use legacy records. The oracle passes after these refinements.

**Part 4 — not completed.** No `jsonld`, `context`, `turtle` or `trig` fields have been attached to the legacy packets: the runtime still has no complete message graph that all its gates read. A read-only probe of the current mortgage-core packet header/body projections, with the prescribed context/types added, admits the simple describe/accept bodies but refuses description, propose, deliver, receipt and invoke with `undefined-term`. This probe is diagnostic only: it is not signed-document admission and ignores the legacy evidence strings. Full operation-model contexts and embedded credential construction remain unfinished; this is not a claim that those parts cannot be built. The explorer and the prior exact-content chat behavior are unchanged. The final Part 4 checkpoint passes **305 reference tests + 17 fixture tests**, including the unchanged oracle, and all **157 executable paths**; the pipeline count remains **228 (15 hard failures + 213 exceptions)**.

## Brief 26a

<!-- AUTHORED-BY Codex GPT-6 -->

Implementation is offline and uncommitted. The matrix is being built first. Baseline-only calls to `runScenario` retain their behavior so the immutable oracle remains untouched; artifact generation explicitly requests all configurations. A configuration uses the same literal world patch as a branch (objects merge; arrays replace). The existing email file contains only an unsigned mailbox stand-in, despite the brief describing an existing structured MIME binding; the signed MIME mode is being added separately without changing the mailbox journey. No files owned by the graph-gate lane are changed.

**Part 1 checkpoint.** Scenario schemas now accept single-layer registered alternatives, enforce their composed identifiers, and reject baseline/no-change entries. The runner explicitly requests the matrix; the API defaults to baseline-only to preserve the unchanged decision oracle. Each matrix cell runs the main path and its eligible branches independently. Manifest rows name a configuration only for alternatives. A shared patch additionally accepts an object keyed by list-item id (or mandate agent) to replace/add individual entries; literal arrays retain the existing replacement semantics. This works for both branch changes and configuration worlds. World advertisements are explicit and whole configurations are selected; a signed unsupported opening and a signed in-conversation step down have refusal tests. Feed declarations are transcribed from specifications 10–19; transport permits no verdict differences. The confinement tests pass for email, A2A and MCP, and the deliberately wrong fixture exercises real gates and produces readable mandate and final-outcome differences. The immutable oracle has passed unchanged twice after integration.

**Parts 2–4 checkpoint.** All three mortgage main paths and five configuration-only branches now run through separately keyed agents. Only Sam/lender messages change carriage; packet carriage labels show the bank and public index retaining direct transport. MIME email preserves the signed bytes and adds a verified-message readable copy, description header and structured-reply footer; altered prose is refused for human parity. A2A publishes a JCS/JWS-signed card with one JSON-RPC interface and a required Answerable extension, carries one opaque data part, and ignores text and task state for decisions. The lying-task branch delivers a genuine lender-key-signed refusal; an assertion checks the receiving conversation remains refused. MCP uses per-request revision/capability metadata, the required HTTP headers, one schema-bound exchange tool, structured results and the recommended serialized text copy. Tool-description instructions are dropped and the original principal approval still runs. A separately keyed fictional channel authorization server issues resource-bound channel tokens; a request to forward one elsewhere is refused. Transport protocol/adversarial tests and final command results are being added below.

**Confinement and outcomes.** Each alternative’s main path is compared with the baseline using the ordered `(agent, gate, check, verdict, reason)` tuples. Only checks fed by the changed layer may differ. The comparison also checks the last gate outcome and the final signed receipt/refusal, including the decision status in the mortgage receipt. Discovery, card verification, ignored metadata and task-state notices are transport records, never extra verdicts.

| Mortgage carriage | Confinement | Journey outcome | Finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Email | PASS; no check tuple differs | Completed, indicatively eligible | No engine/design difference. |
| A2A | PASS; no check tuple differs | Completed, indicatively eligible | No engine/design difference. |
| MCP | PASS; no check tuple differs | Completed, indicatively eligible | No engine/design difference. |

The negative fixture is deliberately outside confinement: it adds an unauthorized payment proposal to the email world. The real outbound gate refuses it, and the comparator reports the additional mandate verdict and changed final gate outcome. This is a test of detection, not a newly discovered engine or design defect. Transport failure branches are compared with their configuration’s own main path, not required to preserve the successful journey outcome. The A2A lying-state branch admits an authentic **refusal message** and checks that the receiving conversation is refused; admitting the message is not accepting the mortgage. Its manifest gate outcome is therefore admission, while the signed message and conversation retain the refusal. The two instruction-only branches intentionally have no gate-decision divergence: the dropped instructions are recorded, principal approval is still required, and the journey completes.

**Not from the specification.** These are local choices or gaps in the supplied sources; no network or additional protocol source was used.

1. The research provides no complete normative A2A data-part example. The required Answerable extension uses the local URI `https://w3id.org/jeswr/awp#message-format/v1`, declares `encoding: base64`, and puts the opaque `{bytes, mediaType}` pair in its one data part. The legacy signed-envelope media type is the local `application/vnd.answerable.signed+json`; JSON-LD round-trip tests use `application/ld+json`. Neither media-type registration nor independent interoperation is claimed.
2. A2A optional streaming, push notifications, tenant routing, cancellation, artifact/history persistence and extended-card access are omitted. A reply is carried directly as a message or as the status message inside one task. The gate-side host chooses the specified state mapping; the receiver never treats a state as a message outcome. Task and JSON-RPC identifiers do not replace the signed conversation and reply references. Local endpoint paths are chosen by this fixture, not attributed to the research.
3. The MCP revision date’s release status remains UNCONFIRMED, as supplied. Its `server/discover` response schema was also UNCONFIRMED: this implementation uses the smallest local object with `protocolVersion`, `serverInfo` and tool capabilities, and validates that object. There is no initialize exchange, session id or negotiated version. JSON responses are used; event streaming is not implemented.
4. The MCP tool’s input/output schema is the same opaque byte-pair schema. The local adversarial result metadata member `requestedTokenResource` models an attempted onward token request; it is not claimed to be a standardized delegation feature. Tool descriptions are discarded as untrusted metadata under CORE-L-10 and the brief: the research explicitly did not find a normative sentence about descriptions.
5. The channel authorization server is an issuer-only fictional party with its own signing key, local one-use authorization codes and an issued-token table. Both local grant and token requests bind `resource`, and issuer/resource mismatches are refused. Protected resource metadata, authorization metadata and the 401 Bearer challenge are provided; the local protected metadata URL is the tool resource followed by `/metadata`. This is an in-process authorization stand-in, not a deployed OAuth service or a claim of full authorization-server conformance. The channel token and any onward request are handled by carriage code; message gates never read the token’s claims or treat it as evidence.
6. The existing email file was a prose-mailbox stand-in, not the structured MIME implementation described by the brief. It now exports a separate bounded multipart/base64 implementation. The readable body is a deterministic gate projection of the verified signed envelope/body using the shared fictional profile labels, with the automation statement and structured-reply footer. It is a deliberately small MIME subset, not a general Internet-mail parser. The amount-changing branch alters the readable loan amount without changing the structured bytes.
7. No transport automatically retries an uncertain operation. The existing signed message ids, replay checks and transmission mechanisms remain responsible for safe duplicate handling. Channel correlation cannot authorize a message. All HTTP/mail exchanges run in process and the trace stand-ins say so.
8. Specification 10 and the brief name the three carriage refusal codes. They already existed in `REASON_CODES` but were absent from the scenario schema’s narrower `CORE_REASON_CODES` selection. The scenario expectation schema now accepts those existing codes; no new reason spelling, core check, vocabulary individual or gate rule was invented here.

**Matrix handover to other layers and Brief 22b.** The scenario suite catalog is now centralized in `src/suites/scenario.ts`, rather than repeated inside `runPath`. Add a suite there, provide its executable definition in `src/profile/configurations.ts`, implement the actual gate/mechanism dispatch, then add the scenario entry and its real failure branches. Merely registering a suite is insufficient: a missing or unresolved runtime definition produces blocked rows and no trace. Existing registry combination constraints also block an incompatible single-layer cell instead of silently substituting a baseline. The feed table must match the layer’s specification; updating the allowlist to conceal an unrelated verdict change is not acceptable.

The wire id convention for a single-layer entry is `alternativeWireId(entry.id)`, currently `https://w3id.org/jeswr/awp#config/<entry-id>/0.1`. The public trace id remains the required short composed id, with all eleven layers in the header. Call `runScenario(..., {configuration: "all"})` for the matrix, or select one composed id; the default remains baseline-only for the unchanged oracle and older consumers. Manifest baseline rows keep the configuration field absent. Alternatives have separate trace/outline names. Alternative traces do not compare their added branches against an unrelated baseline outline; the actual outline artifacts, declared failure witnesses, explicit adversarial tests and confinement comparison provide the checks.

A world starts with baseline-supporting agents advertising the selected alternative. Its explicit party patches can override those lists, including retaining baseline on ancillary conversations. Lists can be patched by keyed object: for example `world.parties["sam-agent"].configurations`, or `world.script["some-step"]`; new entries are added, existing fields merge, and array literals retain full-list replacement semantics. Mandates use the agent name as their key. Patching an item cannot rename its key. Whole-array replacement remains useful for a branch that ends early. A transport with more than two agents needs an explicit two-agent `carriage.parties` scope. MCP additionally requires the issuer-only `channel-authority` party; this prevents a missing authorization stand-in from masquerading as a run.

Brief 22b’s two `{id: "authenticity-data-integrity", change: {authenticity: "data-integrity"}}` entries are accepted unchanged; an omitted world becomes an empty patch. The Data Integrity suite is registered because its wire implementation exists, but this branch does **not** install an executable graph-gate configuration or publish a Data Integrity mortgage trace. On merge, provide its executable definition at `alternativeWireId("authenticity-data-integrity")` (or an explicit alias to that wire id), connect the lane’s signing/verifying implementation to the supplied gate configuration, and regenerate schemas/traces/manifest. The matrix supplies the selected whole configuration, agent accepted ids, and the baseline/public/selected configuration map. Public-read exchanges keep their existing public configuration: if the lane requires Data Integrity on those ancillary messages too, its public-read cryptosuite dispatch/definition must implement that explicitly. Neither an advertised layer name nor this matrix changes their cryptography by itself.

All binding APIs accept bytes plus a media type. Tests pass real `writeDocument` output, signed under either wire authenticity suite, through all three bindings and verify the recovered bytes unchanged. The temporary `src/wire/carriage.ts` reader still admits only the current legacy signed serialization; it expressly refuses to pretend the existing object gates understand JSON-LD. The graph lane must connect its actual reader at that one hand-off, and adapt the new gate-side readable projector to its wrapper view. No binding needs to parse or reserialize a signed message. `src/runtime/agent.ts` changes only the receive hand-off and its import. The other lane’s protected paths are untouched. No dependencies were added; no package-policy check or installation was needed. The mortgage main script is unchanged outside the appended configurations list, and payment work was not started.

The structured email mode also supplies ordinary From/To, Date, Subject and stable Message-ID headers using fictional channel addresses; these headers never establish message identity or reply authority. Both agents’ signed implementation descriptions advertise the actual selected binding and the fictional receiving endpoint, alongside direct carriage where baseline/public interaction is supported. The direct bus now routes an addressless reply from carriage conversation metadata, without inspecting the logical signed payload. For the new transport traces the inherited generated face-check question uses the operation’s label; captions, rule explanations and chat text in these mortgage alternatives contain no HTTP(S) or URN identifiers. Exact protected packet data remains available separately.

**Final validation and counts.** All four parts are implemented in this lane’s current serialization, with the graph-reader merge boundary described above. The A2A lying-state refusal is now proposed by the scripted lender core, checked and sealed by its ordinary outbound gate, and admitted as a refusal by Sam’s ordinary inbound gate; it is not made by re-signing a delivered packet in the scenario driver. `src/engine/scripted-replies.ts` supplies that general data-driven reply override without editing the other lane’s handlers. The outer-envelope decoder also reuses the existing wire duplicate-member-name guard, and A2A checks conflicting copies across both reply variants rather than silently choosing one.

The final reference suite has **325 passing tests in 49 files**. The unchanged oracle passes over **157 executable baseline paths**; neither its test nor its snapshot was edited. The snapshot SHA-256 remains `5352929414096fbaeaf0958d95338c2a4192f7fb7ae0c491d867c34a5a6d0b8e`. This working copy’s starting manifest was **166 paths: 146 runs, 11 disagree, 9 blocked**; the final manifest is **174 paths: 154 runs, 11 disagree, 9 blocked**, with **165 live executable paths**. The eight added rows are two email paths, three A2A paths and three MCP paths. All eight are runs, with separate traces and outlines. Every previous outcome, expectation, consequence and classification is unchanged, and baseline rows still omit `configuration`. Six existing real-joint trace hash fields were refreshed to match the regenerated trace bytes; their classifications and gate decisions did not change. Previously maintained outlines remain unchanged. The legacy manifest `actual` field on the successful mortgage main path still identifies its first bank step-up hold; the completed journey is visible in its final signed receipt and is independently compared by confinement.

Removing the new configurations member leaves `mortgage-main.json` identical as data to its original. Protected gate/mechanism/derivation/packet/handler paths have no diff, and the only runtime-agent change is its carriage hand-off/import. The build’s Node subprocess cannot invoke `git` in this sandbox (`spawnSync git EPERM`), so generated trace commit metadata honestly reads `unknown`; no commit id was invented. No network, dependency installation, commit, personal-agent tests, coverage, evaluation suite or full-workspace gate was used.
