## Brief 25p

<!-- AUTHORED-BY Codex GPT-6 -->

Standalone protocol objects and provider checks only. Work uses Node 24 from
`/opt/node24/bin`, offline, without commits. The only protocol source is
`payment-protocols-2026-11-13.md`; the complete Brief 25 was also read. This branch has no
Brief 24 report section or settlement directory. No SettlementSuite, gate, mechanism,
engine, runtime, wire, model, scenario, profile or trace is changed. The report append is
the sole requested exception to the new-files-only rule.

**Part 1 complete — 62 tests.** `settlement/eip3009.ts` implements EIP-712 domain and
TransferWithAuthorization hashing, deterministic secp256k1 signing, Ethereum signature
encoding, low-s enforcement and signer recovery. A locally computed vector is pinned;
every authorization and domain field has a signature-binding test. `http.ts` supplies
the in-process exchange, unambiguous case-insensitive header lookup, canonical base64 and
base64url encodings, bounded UTF-8 JSON parsing and duplicate-member rejection.
`ledger.ts` owns exact-decimal balances, immutable SHA-256 hash-chained entries and a
single-use set. Failed transfers leave balances and replay state unchanged. `money.ts`
provides integer conversion without floating point, including zero-decimal currencies.

**Dependencies.** The working copy already contained `@noble/curves` and `@noble/hashes`
2.4.0 and the corresponding manifest/lockfile edits. The task reports that
`check:packages` passed before installation; that is supplied evidence, not a new check
run here. No dependency was added or installed in this lane; those files are untouched.

**Not from the specification.** The source confirms the EIP-712 authorization fields
but not ABI byte construction or signature wire layout. This implementation uses 32-byte
ABI words, Keccak-256, the EIP-712 0x1901 prefix, and r/s/v with v=27/28, low-s only.
The pinned vector is computed locally, not an external conformance vector. Provider
accounts, exact-decimal arithmetic, globally scoped ledger once-keys, hash-chained
`sha-256:<hex>` transaction references and synchronous atomic updates are local stand-ins.
Precision is explicit caller-supplied profile data (0–36); no currency or asset registry
is inferred. Parsing defaults to 65,536 decoded bytes and rejects duplicate JSON members,
non-finite numbers, invalid UTF-8, ambiguous headers and noncanonical base encodings.

**Integration notes and final validation** are appended as the remaining parts finish.

**Part 2 complete — 29 tests.** `settlement/x402/objects.ts` has strict exact/EVM
requirements and all three v2 header payloads, base64 JSON codecs, facilitator request
and response schemas, a 402 exchange builder and bidirectional atomic conversion.
`facilitator.ts` verifies the configured domain, accepted requirements, signer, value,
recipient, time window, funds and unused nonce before settling on `Ledger`. A changed
recipient returns `invalid_exact_evm_payload_recipient_mismatch`; transaction is the
actual entry digest. Repeated verification is read-only; settlement is single use.

**Not from the specification — x402 additions.** This narrow exact/EVM profile requires
`extra.name/version`, rejects unknown object fields outside explicit extensions, and
uses all accepted requirement fields in an exact canonical comparison with server
configuration. Currency and token precision are configured separately from network and
asset. Authorization time uses strict EIP-3009 endpoints and remaining validity no
longer than `maxTimeoutSeconds`. Errors for invalid signatures, expiry, unknown accounts
and nonce reuse use the source's generic `invalid_payload`; no unconfirmed error codes
are invented. Failed settlements have empty `transaction`, optional payer and the
configured network (failure-field presence was not specified). EIP-3009 replay keys are
the canonical tuple of chain, contract, signer and nonce, shared across protocols.

**Second-half wiring — x402.** `paymentRequiredExchange` produces the request for
payment. Construct `PaymentPayload` with `signAuthorization(requirementsDomain(req),
auth, key)`, then `encodePaymentSignature` for the order. Call `X402Facilitator.verify`
then `settle`; `encodePaymentResponse` produces the receipt header. Read payee from
`accepts[].payTo` / `accepted.payTo` / `payload.authorization.to`, amount from `amount`
and `authorization.value`; convert with `convertX402Amount` using configured currency
and precision. `authorization.nonce` carries the engine once-key binding, with
`authorizationOnceKey` supplying provider replay scope. The integration must derive the
nonce from the engine once-key, bind the token/network/currency profile and the payee's
account, and authenticate the facilitator's receipt separately: x402 does not sign it.
There is no chain, and network/asset identifiers have no live-network effect.

**Part 3 complete — 48 tests.** `settlement/mpp/objects.ts` builds/parses RFC 9110
Payment challenges, canonical base64url/JCS charge requests, the recommended HMAC-SHA256
binding, challenge-derived nonces, EIP-3009 credentials with did:pkh sources, and
Payment-Receipt headers. `server.ts` checks the original challenge echo, HMAC, expiry,
single use, recipient/value/currency/nonce, signer, source, funds and provider nonce
scope. Its HTTP handler returns a fresh challenge on credential refusal; replay returns
402 `https://paymentauth.org/problems/invalid-challenge` without a second debit.
All research-listed problem types/statuses are exposed. Tests change every HMAC field,
rebind tampered credentials, exercise the alternate Payment-Authorization header and
verify zero-decimal currency conversion.

**Not from the specification — MPP additions.** Challenge hashing is SHA-256 of the
JCS form of the parsed recognized challenge parameters (including id and any description).
The HMAC uses exactly eight pipe-separated slots, including an empty header slot when
absent. Bound parameter values containing pipes are refused to prevent delimiter
ambiguity; server secrets must have at least 32 bytes. The local evm/charge profile
requires recipient, source and expires, limits authorization expiry to challenge expiry,
and uses configured token domain/currency/precision. The source permits those challenge
and request fields to be absent generally. Method details remain opaque JSON and do not
select the trusted domain. The caller supplies the originally issued challenge and a
fresh-challenge callback; it must make fresh challenges distinct, e.g. with random opaque
data. `digest` is HMAC-bound but this module does not interpret its unconfirmed body
binding semantics. Receipt chainId is a decimal string. Non-JCS requests, duplicate auth
parameters and unknown credential JSON keys are refused; unknown challenge auth
parameters are ignored as specified. This is a charge/authorization subset, not another
EVM payload type, policy-refusal flow or a live facilitator.

**Second-half wiring — MPP.** `createChallenge` + `challengeExchange` produce the
request for payment; put the agreement terms digest in `request.externalId`.
`createCredential` signs the order, and `buildCredential` encodes its authorization
header. `MppServer.verify` is read-only; `settle` returns the settlement/receipt and
`encodeReceipt` provides Payment-Receipt. `handle` composes those HTTP operations.
Payee/amount/currency are `chargeRequest(challenge).recipient/amount/currency` and
`credential.payload.to/value`; `challenge.id` and `challengeNonce(challenge)` identify
single use. The engine must record its once-key association with the challenge and
authenticate the merchant/provider through its ordinary envelope. The receipt is not
itself signed by MPP. Configure the same ledger/domain for EIP-3009 nonce protection
shared with x402.

**Part 4 complete — 40 tests.** `settlement/ap2/sdjwt.ts` issues ES256 SD-JWT issuer
commitments, salts disclosures, creates holder key-binding JWTs with `typ: kb+sd-jwt`,
and verifies issuer signature, holder `cnf.jwk`, aud, nonce, iat, sd_hash, each disclosed
claim's commitment and required disclosure presence. The existing selective-disclosure
mechanism was read; it is a different signed-statement encoding, so it cannot substitute
for this format. Attacks with tampered/missing disclosures are re-signed by the holder
in tests, proving that commitment/presence checks work independently of sd_hash.
`objects.ts` signs/verifies the merchant checkout, computes checkout_hash, defines strict
closed Checkout/Payment Mandates and the open Payment Mandate, and converts amounts.
`provider.ts` checks both closed mandates against a verified merchant checkout. A real
signed Payment Mandate for another checkout returns `terms-digest-mismatch`. Open
mandates verify the person's signature, agent holder key, short expiry, payee and amount
constraints. No FIDO claim is made.

**Not from the specification — AP2 additions.** The research has examples, not closed
field schemas. This profile uses decimal strings for checkout/product prices, integer
minor units for payment_amount and amount ranges, uppercase three-letter currencies,
safe-integer units, optional string shipping/return policies and strict example field
sets. Only the two required open constraint kinds are implemented; allowed payees use
`{type: payment.allowed_payees, payees: [id, ...]}`. Other researched constraint kinds
are refused rather than guessed. Open expiry is at most 900 seconds; key binding age is
at most 300 seconds by default. This profile chooses SHA-256 for checkout_hash and
sd_hash, unpadded base64url, top-level `[salt, name, value]` disclosures with 16 random
salt bytes, `_sd`/`_sd_alg` commitments and `issuer~disclosure~...~binding` serialization
(including the separator before the binding in sd_hash). Issuer typ is `sd-jwt`, holder
typ is the brief's `kb+sd-jwt`, checkout typ is `ap2-checkout+jwt`. Those serialization
details are not present in the research. Only public P-256 cnf JWKs are accepted;
verification uses pinned caller-supplied issuer/merchant keys and refuses embedded
verification-key headers. Checkout validation additionally compares payee id, currency
and converted amount. No receipt field schema was supplied, so no invented AP2 receipt
claim set or settlement method is added.

**Second-half wiring — AP2.** `signCheckout` produces the request for payment;
`verifyCheckout` and `checkoutHash` validate and identify its exact bytes.
`issueCheckoutMandate` / `issuePaymentMandate` produce issuer objects and `presentSdJwt`
produces the holder-bound orders. Set audiences to merchant and credential-provider,
respectively, and pass the expected nonce to each verifier. `checkCheckoutMandate` and
`checkPaymentMandate` authenticate them before use. Checkout payee/amount/currency are
`merchant.id`, `total_price`, `currency`; the Payment Mandate uses `payee.id`,
`payment_amount.amount/currency` and transaction_id = checkout_hash. `convertAp2Amount`
bridges minor units. Key-binding nonce carries the expected once-key association;
transaction_id identifies the checkout. The second half must retain consumed transaction
and nonce state, perform the provider ledger transfer and sign the processor Payment
Receipt and merchant Checkout Receipt with an explicitly documented local claim set.
No such settlement/receipt producer exists here because the source gives only their
JWT form and signing roles. `issueOpenPaymentMandate` and `checkOpenPaymentMandate`
provide the autonomous constraint form; the second half must pin the closed mandate's
agent signing key to the open mandate's cnf and lift its constraints into P5.

**Part 5 complete — 43 tests.** `settlement/acp/objects.ts` defines strict checkout
creation/session, delegate_payment/allowance, delegated token, complete/payment_data
and order objects. `signing.ts` creates/verifies detached Ed25519 signatures and requires
Signature/Timestamp under the local profile, with separate Authorization/API-Version/
POST Idempotency-Key header checks. `idempotency.ts` caches an immutable copy of the
first response for a canonical body, returns it on identical retries, returns 422
idempotency_conflict for changed bodies and 409 idempotency_in_flight for an unfinished
identical request. `provider.ts` issues vt_ tokens, checks their one-time allowance,
session, merchant, currency, amount, account, expiry and handler, and settles on the
ledger without consuming tokens on refused transfers. Tests cover replay, every
allowance binding, signature/body/key attacks, header requirements, concurrent retries,
and a complete retry that provably debits only once. JPY settlement uses zero decimals.

**Not from the specification — ACP additions.** Session totals use one
`{type: total, amount: integer}` entry and currency/merchant_id at session level; the
source supplies no complete session table. Items use the confirmed creation fields.
Non-ready statuses are limited to the six researched RFC values. Ready totals are
positive and completed sessions require the matching order. Buyer, fulfillment,
billing, risk and metadata structures remain JSON rather than invented field tables.
The payment_method stand-in is `{type: account, account_id}`. `created` is Unix seconds;
vt_ tokens contain random UUIDs, are stored by the fictional provider and are not chain
or card credentials. Merchant id is the local ledger payee account id. Instrument id
must match the delegated payer's account and handler ids must match the configured
provider. Token redemption is single use per token; the HTTP idempotency store must wrap
delegation as well as completion. There is no durable or distributed token/idempotency
store. A failed idempotent callback releases the key for retry; successfully returned
error responses, like any returned response, are cached. Store scope is per endpoint.
Currency is lowercase, precision is configured, and all minor amounts are safe integers.
The optional ACP signing scheme is fixed locally to a detached EdDSA/Ed25519 JWS over
the JCS JSON body. Timestamp is unsigned Unix seconds, checked within 300 seconds by
default. Method, path and other headers are outside that signature, as the source only
confirms body coverage; the engine envelope must protect them. Local allowance,
signature and header error labels are implementation choices, not ACP problem types.

**Second-half wiring — ACP.** Build checkout creation using `createSessionSchema`;
`checkoutSessionSchema` is the request-for-payment object. Sign requests and merchant
responses with `signAcpExchange`, verify with the pinned identity key using
`verifyAcpExchange`, and call `checkAcpRequestHeaders` (which checks bearer format, not
account authentication). Build delegate_payment using `delegatePaymentSchema`;
`AcpPaymentProvider.delegate` returns the token. The order is `completeSchema` with
`payment_data.instrument.credential.token`. `redeem` checks the allowance and returns
the ledger settlement entry. The second half creates the matching completed session's
`order`, and the provider's separately signed payment-receipt statement. Payee is
session.merchant_id / allowance.merchant_id; amount is totals[0].amount bounded by
allowance.max_amount; currency is session/allowance.currency. Use `convertAcpAmount`
before emitting engine decimal money. Pass the engine-derived Idempotency-Key and
parsed body to an endpoint-scoped `IdempotencyStore.execute` around every POST;
provider replay scope is `acp:<vt_token>`. The engine must associate that token with
its once-key, authenticate bearer accounts and protect routing headers in its envelope.

**Adversarial review and final test inventory.** The review added independent
read-only verifier refusals for insufficient funds, funded recovered-signer substitution,
HMAC checks independent of echo comparison, replay by a second funded signer, hidden
registered JWT claims, malformed uint256 schema input and ACP instrument substitution.
Registered JWT timing claims must be visible so jose verifies them before disclosure
reconstruction. MPP rejects non-HTTP whitespace rather than accepting folded headers.
The same real EIP-3009 authorization cannot settle once through x402 and again through
MPP, or vice versa, when both share the provider ledger. Ledger failure injection also
checks the source's insufficient-funds errors after verification, without consuming an
MPP challenge. The duplicate ledger account check was consolidated into one accessor.

The final inventory is **17 new source modules and 10 new test/fixture files**, with
**254 new tests in 9 test files**:

| Part | Final tests | Coverage |
|---|---:|---|
| Shared EIP-3009, HTTP, ledger/money | 67 | Vector, all signed fields, low-s, encodings, bounded/duplicate JSON, exact funds, atomic refusals, replay. |
| x402 exact v2 | 38 | Header objects, every facilitator binding, signed failures, verify/settle and post-verification ledger refusal. |
| MPP draft 01 evm/charge | 56 | Auth syntax, all HMAC fields, original echo, expiry, nonce, did:pkh, both replay scopes, problems, fresh challenges and receipts. |
| AP2 v0.2 | 47 | SD-JWT issuer/holder signatures, disclosure commitments/presence, aud/nonce/sd_hash, checkout binding and open constraints. |
| ACP 2026-04-17 | 44 | Strict objects, delegated allowance/redemption, signatures/timestamps, mandatory headers and concurrent/idempotent requests. |
| Shared x402/MPP replay | 2 | Both settlement orders debit once. |

A temporary AST mutation harness disabled **55 selected refusal guards one at a time**;
all 55 changes made their corresponding tests fail. Source files were restored after
each run, including on errors. The first mutation pass exposed five checks whose tests
were masked by later refusals; the final pass detected every selected mutation. The
harness and results are `/tmp/brief25p-mutate.mjs`, `/tmp/brief25p-mutations.json` and
`/tmp/brief25p-mutations.log`. This is targeted adversarial evidence, not a claim of
exhaustive protocol conformance. V8 coverage for the new modules is **625/625 statements**
and **437/439 branches**.

**Not from the specification — final scope notes.** Bounds on identifiers, arrays,
encoded objects and selective claims, positive payment amounts, schema strictness and
the local error classes are profile/implementation choices. All provider stores are
in memory; callers must retain provider instances across exchanges, and durability or
multiple processes require shared replay/idempotency state. API functions returning
protocol-native integer/base-unit money must be converted by their protocol's single
bidirectional amount helper before the engine reads them as decimal money. No public
protocol discovery, live HTTP, chain, card network or actual money movement is claimed.
The runtime must still pin each protocol key through an identity-signed statement,
authenticate every protocol envelope and supply the agreed terms and accepted payee
account. Those are second-half responsibilities; these pure modules confer no identity
or payment permission themselves.

**Final commands and results.** Commands below run with `/opt/node24/bin` first on PATH;
package commands run from `packages/agent-reference` unless prefixed with a filter.

| Last command | Result |
|---|---|
| `pnpm exec vitest run` | PASS: **579 tests, 58 files**, including the existing 325 tests. |
| `pnpm exec vitest run test/settlement --coverage --coverage.provider=v8 --coverage.include='src/settlement/**/*.ts' --coverage.reporter=json --coverage.reportsDirectory=/tmp/brief25p-coverage --reporter=json --outputFile=/tmp/brief25p-settlement-results.json` | PASS: **254 tests, 9 files**, 100% statements and 99.54% branches in new modules. |
| `node /tmp/brief25p-mutate.mjs` | PASS: **55/55 selected removed guards detected**; all source restored. |
| `pnpm exec tsc -p tsconfig.build.json --outDir /tmp/brief25p-dist` | PASS: all package source compiles, declarations emitted outside the working tree. |
| `pnpm exec tsc -p tsconfig.json --noEmit` | PASS: source and tests. |
| `pnpm --filter @jeswr/agent-reference run lint` | PASS: zero errors; 6 pre-existing warnings, 97 informational diagnostics. |
| `pnpm exec biome check packages/agent-reference/src/settlement packages/agent-reference/test/settlement` from root | PASS: new files only, zero diagnostics. |
| `node scripts/check-agent-layers.mjs` from root | PASS. Bare built-ins, literal ESM imports, no CommonJS loader. |
| `git diff --check` and final status inspection | PASS. Only the report append and the new settlement trees were authored here; the starting package.json/lockfile edits remain untouched. |

Logs are `/tmp/brief25p-{all-tests,settlement,settlement-coverage,compile,typecheck,lint,
biome,layers,mutations}.log`; structured coverage and test results are also in `/tmp` as
shown above. The package `build` script and `gate:full` were not run: they regenerate
existing schemas, traces and scenario artifacts, which this brief explicitly protects.
All source was compiled to `/tmp` and the standard package tests were run instead.
No evaluation run, network access, dependency installation, scenario/manifest change or
commit was made. The HTTP stand-in for the second half is: requests and responses pass
in process with their method, path, headers and body; the fictional provider keeps the
ledger and no real money moves. ACP delegation must be wrapped as a 201 response.


## Brief 24

<!-- AUTHORED-BY Codex GPT-6 -->

Implementation is offline, under Node 24, without commits. The merged Briefs 22, 22b,
26a and 25p were read before implementation. Payment model reads are real dataset
wrappers; legacy message bodies remain the explicitly supplied integration boundary.
The existing provider ledger is reused. Checkpoints and final results follow below.

**Parts 1–4 checkpoint.** The twelfth choice is registered, all previous configurations
select `rdf-order`, and four protocol choices are explicitly not run. `payment` is no
longer a commitments suite. Payment and receipt checks read `src/model/payment.ts`
wrappers; RDF documents and trace facts are written from those datasets by `src/wire/payment.ts`.
The existing ledger is reused. The invoice and shopping paths now exchange signed offers,
authorised acceptances, invoices, orders, provider receipts, forwarded receipts and
acknowledgements. The new mortgage stretch runs with separately keyed fictional valuer,
register and provider agents. All **333** earlier mortgage-main decision records remain
identical and in position. The three mortgage failures stop at P2/P3/P1; the price-only
shopping branch stops at P5. Focused checks initially passed **21** new payment tests.
The pipeline guard still reports **15 hard failures and 213 exact allowances**.

**An input-contract conflict, stopped rather than bypassed.** `scenarios/expenses.json`
also contains `pay` without an agreement (and names the removed payment suite). It is
not among the scenarios the oracle instruction explicitly authorizes changing. The
mandatory universal P1 refusal changes that path. Its existing signed exchange cannot
remain decision-identical while also forming the missing agreement through real gates.
P1 is retained; expense trace/oracle recording is stopped pending that scope decision.
This prevents claiming a passing global oracle/scenario gate. No expense agreement is
fabricated and no old decision snapshot is silently accepted as a new real run.

**RDF/proof and trace checkpoint.** The source now signs an actual ODRL Offer document
inside its retained message body; scalar service-term mirrors remain for the existing
negotiator and are checked against that RDF at the wire boundary. The payment gate reads
the admitted offer's duty nodes. The order carries the agreement and both original
message proofs. The provider independently verifies those proofs, the acceptance's
party-signed mandate, its exact intent and any required owner approval before its ledger
transfer. Owner payment approval additionally signs explicit terms/order digest bindings.
The receipt contains the bounded order scope, rather than recursively copying the
agreement proofs into another credential. Both payer and payee run the receipt check.
Every P1–P8 verdict carries a snapshot of the very dataset it read; the recorder resolves
those snapshots through `writeDocument` before publishing. Requests, orders and receipts
also retain the native-document inspector view. The RDF object and its lifted facts are
identified as the same document instead of duplicated. The successful mortgage stretch
is marked from its register lookup through the payee acknowledgement.

A wire round-trip exposed a concrete SDK JSON-LD streaming-reader problem: the four
amount-constraint properties were moved from their named subject to a new blank node.
`src/wire/document.ts` now uses the pinned standard JSON-LD processor to produce
context-free N-Quads, then the existing bounded SDK parser. Graph equality remains
mandatory. `src/wire/carriage.ts` also distinguishes the encoded envelope string's bound
from decoded payload-string bounds: a valid larger signed order fits the existing
64 KiB envelope limit, while its decoded fields retain the existing 16 KiB string limit.
No context admission, signature check or resource bound is disabled.

**Compatibility audit checkpoint.** `/tmp/brief24-oracle-audit.json` records **148 unchanged
existing paths**, exactly **seven changed paths in named scenarios**, **four new paths**,
and **zero unexpected differences** outside the two expense conflicts. Mortgage-main's
original 333-record prefix remains identical. `mortgage-injected-fee` still stops for its
old `not-authorised` reason: its original action is `compensate`, with an unpermitted
recipient, not a `pay` intent. `trip-booking` currently reserves through a multi-party
agreement and never sends `pay`; `invoice-finance` redeems an invoice capability with
`use` and never sends `pay`. There is no cash-payment path to migrate in those two files,
and no new fee, payee or payment authority was invented for them. `mortgage-core` remains
unchanged: adding the register, provider and valuation operations/world would have a
nonzero implementation and recording cost, so the brief's optional addition was omitted.

The current engine binds one owner approval to one action. Mortgage-main therefore asks
once before accepting the valuation and once for the subsequently created exact order.
Both questions show the service, registered valuer label, 450 USD and the 600 USD limit.
The second signature explicitly covers the terms digest and order digest. An earlier
acceptance signature is never silently reused for the later payment.

**Final implementation by part.** Part 1 registers settlement as the twelfth selection,
with `partOf: "commitments"`; `rdf-order` is executable and AP2 0.2, ACP 2026-04-17,
x402 v2 and MPP draft 01 are explicitly not run. Every configuration inherits
`rdf-order`. No settlement alternative configuration is declared. The old commitments
`payment` selection is removed, including its scenario references. Part 2 adds the
single ordered P1–P8 checker and receipt checker, three reason individuals and their
public reason codes, explicit signed payment approval bindings, persistent once keys,
recorded orders and receipts. The existing account-binding logic, `PaymentLimit`,
calendar-month reservations and approval exceptions remain. A limit attached to the
with-approval payment rule itself remains a cap; it is not authority to approve a
larger payment. P3 also checks accepted account ownership, even when an invoice falsely
retains the agreed payee's name. An instalment cannot be invented to evade replay: it
must be the instalment in the signed amount due.

Part 3 implements the four-method RDF suite over the existing provider-owned ledger.
The provider admits an authenticated order, independently verifies the carried contract
proofs and acceptance authority, authorizes its own ledger effect, signs the receipt,
and returns it through ordinary message gates. The payer admits and forwards that
provider-signed statement under the agreement; the payee admits it and acknowledges.
No private signing material or reusable payment credential is given to a core. Part 4
adds the paid mortgage valuation and its three refusal branches, plus the shopping
price-only refusal and an actual agent answer with the admitted price. Part 5 records
native objects and same-dataset lifted JSON-LD, individual payment verdicts, settlement
on every manifest row and payment segment boundaries. Packet summaries keep original
identifiers and bytes in inspector fields rather than copying them into sentences.
The recorder caches serialization by the exact graph/document fingerprint; a changed
live dataset gets a new snapshot. The stopped compatibility work is the expense conflict
already described, not an exception to P1.

**Recorded decision changes, exhaustive relative to the original oracle.**

| Existing path | Change and outcome |
|---|---|
| `mortgage-main/main` | The original 333 records are unchanged; registered lookup, authorized valuation agreement, invoice, approved 450 USD order, ledger receipt and payee acknowledgement are appended. |
| `pay-an-invoice/main` | A real supplier offer/acceptance replaces the missing contract; the provider now settles on its ledger and both sides admit its receipt. |
| `pay-an-invoice/branch-1` | Signed offer/acceptance precede the original rejected account-source path. It still stops for `source-not-authoritative`; no provider order leaves. |
| `pay-an-invoice/branch-2` | The signed offer and invoice both specify 700 GBP. The original limit exception is still held for the principal, now at P5 with `limit-exceeded`. |
| `shopping-agent/main` | A real merchant agreement precedes the payment and signed ledger receipts. |
| `shopping-agent/branch-1` | The signed offer and invoice both specify 600 GBP. The original budget/limit exception still asks the principal at P5, `limit-exceeded`. |
| `shopping-agent/branch-2` | The contract is formed first; the redirected account still stops for `not-authorised`, at the retained account-binding check P4. |

| New path | Actual outcome |
|---|---|
| `mortgage-main/fee-amount-differs` | Stop at P2, `amount-differs`. The agent tells Sam that the agreement says 450 USD and the invoice asks for 495 USD. No provider order is sent. |
| `mortgage-main/fee-payee-differs` | Stop at P3, `payee-differs`. The invoice's account is accepted as belonging to another party, even though the invoice retains the valuer as its claimed payee. No provider order is sent. |
| `mortgage-main/fee-no-offer` | Stop at P1, `no-agreement`, despite the ordinary text asking for immediate payment. |
| `shopping-agent/asked-to-look-not-to-buy` | Stop at P5, `not-authorised`; no payment reaches the provider. The agent answers: “The cheapest available price is 300.00 GBP.” |

**Manifest and oracle accounting.** The manifest has **178 rows: 158 `runs`, 11
`disagrees`, 9 `blocked`**, including eight existing transport-configuration rows.
Every row has `settlement: "rdf-order"`. These are artifact inventory totals: the two
expense `runs` rows and their traces are retained historical records, **not successful
runs under the new gate**. Current validation refuses those two paths at P1; their
recording was stopped to preserve the forbidden-to-change oracle scope. Therefore a
claim that all 169 recorded executable rows currently run would be false. The named
scenario recording commands execute 19 paths (three invoice, four shopping, twelve
mortgage including existing transport cells). No protocol stand-in is called a run.

The snapshot command was run **once**, after those recordings. Its filter now excludes
configuration alternatives, matching the baseline-only oracle test rather than
silently overwriting a baseline key with a transport alternative. It records **161
baseline paths**; exactly the seven existing paths above differ and exactly the four
new paths above are added. All other existing snapshot entries are unchanged. The
new SHA-256 is `b746fb3e085851de674c3cc05d47d9373f02df397f00f04bf687d367a34e799e`.
The two protected expense entries remain historical. The global live oracle is not
reported as passing. The independent live compatibility audit checks all other paths
and records the two actual P1 expense refusals separately.

**Brief 25 adapter handover.** `SettlementSuite` lives in `src/settlement/types.ts`.
`ProtocolObjects.objects` contains the actual native object/HTTP form for inspection;
`native` is a host-side association, never core authority. Adapters can retain their
configured protocol server, signing functions, key handles and profile in their
constructor, so the existing protocol functions do not need changed signatures.
`CheckedPayment.digest` binds the exact normalized order scope; the terms digest binds
the signed offer. The host must verify and pin the original protocol envelopes before
lifting, retain request/checkout/challenge-to-agreement associations, and supply the
selected adapter's closed-instruction guarantee for P8. A registered but unimplemented
settlement choice cannot release an RDF order as a silent fallback. Provider account
ownership comes from authenticated host configuration, never a core parameter. The
provider and payer must retain their replay state across exchanges. RDF lifting checks
that native objects were not changed after admission.

| Protocol | Mapping onto request / order / settle / lift |
|---|---|
| x402 | `paymentRequiredExchange` / `signAuthorization` + `encodePaymentSignature` / `X402Facilitator.verify` and `settle`, then `encodePaymentResponse` / requirements `payTo` and `amount`, authorization `to/value`, configured asset/network/currency/precision, and the agreement/once-key association. The provider signs the otherwise unsigned receipt separately. |
| MPP | `createChallenge` + `challengeExchange` / `createCredential` + `buildCredential` / `MppServer.verify` and `settle` + `encodeReceipt` / challenge charge fields and credential `to/value`, with `externalId` carrying the agreement digest and the challenge/nonce association. Preserve the shared EIP-3009 replay scope and authenticate the receipt in an ordinary provider statement. |
| AP2 | `signCheckout` / the two `issue*Mandate` functions plus `presentSdJwt` / `checkCheckoutMandate` and `checkPaymentMandate`, followed by the existing ledger transfer and a locally specified signed receipt / checkout and mandate money/payee, `checkout_hash`, audience, nonce and open-constraint associations. Brief 25p supplies no AP2 settlement/receipt producer; the receipt claim profile must be stated, not attributed to a missing specification. |
| ACP | `createSessionSchema` + `checkoutSessionSchema` / `delegatePaymentSchema`, `AcpPaymentProvider.delegate`, then `completeSchema` with the delegated token / `redeem`, a completed-session order and separately signed provider receipt / session/allowance totals, merchant, currency, session id and one-use allowance. Keep `signAcpExchange`/`verifyAcpExchange`, header checks and endpoint-scoped `IdempotencyStore.execute` around POSTs. |

Currency precision is explicit profile data; no live registry is inferred. Native
minor-unit amounts are converted to decimal strings in the protocol lift before gate
checks, using the existing money helpers. One literal requirement needs clarification
for Brief 25: `lift(objects): PaymentFacts` is a pure, one-way interface, so it cannot
also accept decimal model money and return a protocol order. A builder can reuse the
checked native units from its original request; if it must manufacture minor units
from a new model amount, the “to and from minor units inside lift and nowhere else”
wording needs a bidirectional conversion API. RDF order has no minor-unit conversion;
its JPY test and the unchanged protocol conversion tests cover zero-decimal currency.

**Model/generator boundary.** `src/model/payment.ts` contains live views for the
closed payment document shapes: `PaymentOfferNode`, `PaymentAgreementNode`,
`CompensationNode`, `PaymentConstraintNode`, `PaymentFacts`, and the accepted-account
projection `PaymentAccountNode`; `PaymentNode` supplies typed literal infrastructure.
The generator is still unable to project the complete engine model. The full diagnostic
file retains the exact admissions; the final generation has **158 errors** (and 18
warnings), including the payment shapes' unsupported closed/ignored-property and
constant-value constraints. No generator source or admission rule was weakened; the
one admitted `GrantRecord` continues to be generated, with byte drift checked. The
RDF pipeline guard remains exactly **15 hard core-model failures plus 213 bounded
allowances**. Neither protected guard file was edited. Complete message-body RDF
carriage remains the merged branch's separate, pre-existing boundary; the new payment
objects themselves are real RDF, signed inside the existing verifiable messages.

**Adversarial checks.** A temporary AST harness removed P1–P8 and the receipt guard,
one at a time. All **9/9** removals caused their targeted tests to fail, with source
restored after every attempt and in `finally`. Results and failing assertion names are
in `/tmp/brief24-mutations.json`; this is selected-guard evidence, not a claim of full
coverage. Additional real-message tests cover native offer carriage, ledger settlement,
both receipt admissions, all four new refusal paths, no provider order on failure,
readable approval questions, and a genuinely provider-signed wrong-amount receipt
refused only after the ordinary five inbound checks. Durable tests retain monthly
limits, new-month behavior, file-store restart and single-use approvals while adding
real signed agreements, explicit approval-binding attacks, denial and once-key replay.
All pre-existing protocol source files, ledger/money code and protocol tests are intact.

**Final commands and results.** `/opt/node24/bin` was first on PATH (Node 24.22.3).
All commands were offline; no dependencies were installed, no commits were made, and
no changes outside `packages/agent-reference` remain. The stopped portions above are
reported as incomplete rather than waived.

| Command | Result |
|---|---|
| `pnpm --filter @jeswr/agent-reference run codegen` | Generated the admitted model and refreshed input hashes; full admission diagnostics retained, as described above. |
| `pnpm --filter @jeswr/agent-reference exec tsc -p tsconfig.build.json`, then `node scripts/copy-generated.mjs` in the package | Passed. This is compilation, not a claim that the package's combined build/scenario script passes. |

## Brief 24b

Offline implementation under Node 24, without commits. Only the expenses scenario and
its profile are migrated; the shared payment gates and every other scenario stay intact.
A general trace-caption punctuation correction is described below.
The employee files the claim as a signed `propose` for `urn:op:expenses-file`, carrying
an actual ODRL Offer with a 40.00 GBP compensation duty to the employee and the original
merchant-signed receipt as evidence. The employee and finance receipt admissions remain.
Finance's acceptance intent explicitly names the admitted receipt as support, so a
receipt refused by its source check cannot authorize acceptance. The current legacy
proposal admission does not derive a receipt-use purpose from proposed terms before an
agreement exists. Finance therefore runs an explicit inbound admission of that same
merchant-signed receipt under the expenses purpose before receiving and accepting the
proposal. The attached copy remains in the signed proposal, and its ordinary inbound
checks still run; its pre-agreement terms result is indeterminate, not fabricated as a
pass. Acceptance relies on the separately admitted, purpose-scoped receipt. The employee admits
the signed acceptance and acknowledges it before issuing the reimbursement invoice.

The added finance permission is `use` on the expense-claim operation only, constrained
to the expenses purpose and this employee as recipient. The scenario's existing bounded
commitment mechanism caps acceptance at 50 GBP. Payment authority remains 50 GBP per
payment and 2,000 GBP per calendar month with the original approval exception. The
employee's invoice-request permission and the bank's local-settlement permission are
also restricted to their respective operation, purpose and recipient. No general service
acceptance permission is added. The merchant receipt retains its original `invoice`
claim/source rules; the employee's new `reimbursement-invoice` is a separate claim kind,
accepted only from the employee for the employer. Payment receipts are accepted only
from the reimbursement bank for the employer.

The invoice, order, provider ledger effect, signed provider receipt, forwarding under the
agreement, payee receipt admission and acknowledgement reuse Brief 24's `rdf-order`
flow. The existing reimbursement bank's agent is the provider, with separately configured
payer/payee ledger accounts. Its stand-in says exactly: “No money moves. The payment
provider is a fictional party with its own keys that keeps a ledger.” The scenario
selects `signed-offer-acceptance` and `rdf-order`, with no commitments `payment`
reference. Outlines are recorded from the actual gates, not manually scripted verdicts.

**Part 1 — partly done: adapter and installer done; full caller integration stopped.** The exact tarball is authenticated before extraction into ignored `.sparq/sparq-zk-result/`. **Part 2 — blocked by the delivered relation, not recorded as real runs.** The actual WASM admission function rejects the required issuer-scoped `GRAPH` form with `outside disclosure fragment: operator outside positive BGP/JOIN/FILTER`, and degree `FILTER IN` with `outside disclosure fragment: FILTER expression`. Flat positive queries do admit; those do not implement the required scoped query and are not substituted. Four paths also require hidden comparisons. Original rules, thresholds, membership values and catalogue expectations are retained. The profile now records the exact required query, which the verifier’s query compiler selects.

No supported, issuer-scoped query can be written for these unchanged rules in version 0.1.8. Query admission diagnostics are not proofs and are not counted as scenario runs. Three-run path proof/verification medians are therefore **N/A** for all six. Adapter tests, final totals, oracle audit and commands follow below.

**Adapter and source boundary.** `src/derive/adapters/sparq-zk-result.ts` dynamically imports the real Node ESM entry, registers `kind: "real"`, calls all four delivered functions, and rejects unknown fields in every returned object and nested term/status/presentation. The optional directory override is `ANSWERABLE_SPARQ_ZK_RESULT`; an absent installation registers no result method. A narrowly scoped Node 24 `registerHooks` resolver permits the directory override while keeping the dynamic import literal for the unchanged layer guard. Default discovery and switching to an override after loading the default were both checked. The tarball stays ignored; there is no package manifest or lockfile change.

`src/model/sparq-result.ts` writes scalar claim graphs through N3's N-Quads writer. The adapter accepts the seam's existing status-reference spelling as well as the native spelling, generates deterministic test issuer keys from the supplied seed, and calls native issuance. Native issuance adds fresh random salts. The adapter preserves the package's released rows as its seam value. A `verified: false` keeps the native reason; the source gate includes it in the refusal trace. Successful verification yields only `rule`, `issuers`, and `fresh`, with issuer keys read from the verifier-checked public slots. A substitution with another syntactically valid, receiver-trusted issuer key fails the real cryptographic verifier. The package does not return issuer attribution as a dedicated API result; that remains a requested upstream improvement.

The small shared changes are `ProofSystem.verify`'s optional synchronous `seenNonces` view, the derivation-only array bound of 16,384 for the delivered 14,656-byte proof array, profile-owned `proofQuery` compilation, and the existing source-phase verification call. The receiver's own durable request state backs `has`/`add`; native successful consumption persists synchronously. The existing pre-await `inFlight` claim remains in place and has a scheduling regression test. The expected query is recompiled from the verifier's installed rule; a differing stored request is refused. Failed native verification leaves the nonce pending.

`src/gates/inbound.ts` adds one result-method source check, delegated to `src/derive/result-trust.ts`. It requires previously admitted, identity-signed `proof.issuer-key` statements binding the exact key and its status-list names, and admitted `proof.status` statements binding the exact snapshots. Both remain fresh, fit and usable for the current purpose. Missing or mismatched material yields `source-not-accepted` before the real verifier is called. The test issuers sign these statements with independently pinned identity keys; their commitment-signing keys remain distinct. No RDF allowance or status inventory was edited.

**Caller integration remaining, and why it stopped.** The existing runtime's synchronous request builder still builds the older `issuerKeys/claims/status` policy, rather than the delivered native policy, and it has no admitted-memory argument for constructing that policy from authenticated key/status statements. Its nonce source is still the existing identifier source rather than a new CSPRNG draw for result requests. `src/runtime/agent.ts` is explicitly owned by the concurrent lane and was not edited. The existing engine world builder also hands one proof-credential array to all agents; moving issuance and wallet ownership to the holder needs a world-builder change outside the permitted local files. Those production caller changes are unfinished; adapter and inbound tests construct an authenticated receiver policy explicitly. These test fixtures are direct gate diagnostics, not separately running scenario agents. No production six-path wallet isolation or fresh-agent-request integration is claimed.

**Part 2 — stopped, with existing failure branches retained.** Each of the six paths has the required scoped query attempt in its profile and an explicit capability blocker independent of installation. Its native method declaration remains in the profile and proof steps; `requiredMethods` is empty for the blocked path so the explicit capability blocker remains visible even without the package. The six rules' operands, accepted degree values, assurances and catalogue expectations are unchanged. The requested per-path native credential issuance, prove/verify exchange, additional attack branches and timings stop at query admission. Eight existing ordinary paths in these six scenarios were rerun; none of their decisions changed. Their method stand-ins now contain the three required qualifications. The runner produced an unknown commit header in this sandbox; those eight headers were set to the actual existing HEAD read by `git rev-parse HEAD`, which does not contain these uncommitted changes. No six-path proof trace, implementation-derived outline or successful catalogue result was fabricated.

**Part 3 — done for the implemented adapter and boundaries.** Tests cover hash refusal before extraction, unknown fields at the adapter return boundary, missing identity-key and status statements before verification, the expected query coming from the verifier's profile, claiming the nonce before awaiting the verifier, persistent consumption and replay refusal, altered released rows, substituted issuer slots, retained native refusal reasons, and all six paths remaining blocked with and without installation. The previous toy-relation tests explicitly restore their old test-only query/blocker fixtures in memory; their prior gate assertions remain active, and they write no artifacts. Seven real-package tests skip with a stated research/credential/key notice when the optional installation is physically absent. All package-independent tests still run.

`SELECT DISTINCT ?subject WHERE { ?subject <urn:claim:identity.verified> "true"^^<http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#boolean> . }`

ontology/shapes and added protected context definitions, with the SHA-256 pin updated.
The full generator now reports **157 admission errors** (the complete reproducible list
is `src/generated/full-admission.json`); GrantRecord remains unchanged; the final audit also generates the supported
MessageAuthorship and InvokeBodyChallenge components. No generator or admission rule was weakened. Rejections include
closed/ignored-properties constraints, linked node kinds/composite links, exclusive
choices (`sh:xone`), list positions (`sh:minInclusive`), and kind constraints. Classes
using them keep hand-written dataset wrappers, with SHACL enforced at the wire boundary.
All existing **583 reference + 17 fixture tests** pass at this checkpoint.

Every open object field uses `awp:NamedValueCollection`, a schema.org ItemList of
PropertyValue entries: `schema:propertyID` identifies the claim/parameter,
`schema:name` retains the legacy key, `schema:position` preserves order and
`schema:value` holds a typed literal, IRI, or nested named collection. The open fields
are DescriptionBody.description, ProposeBody.terms, AcceptBody.selection,
InvokeBody.input, DeliverBody.output, ReceiptBody.observed, ActionIntent.parameters,
Agreement.terms and Agreement.selection; credential claim values are in the same
named-value structure under statementPayload. Arrays are ItemLists, explicit nulls are
NullValue nodes, and empty containers retain their class. An absolute domain key is
its own property IRI. An unqualified legacy key receives the explicit compatibility
identifier `urn:awp:legacy-field:<percent-encoded-key>`; this identifies the original key,
not an asserted mapping to a published domain vocabulary. A future profile mapping must
be assessed explicitly rather than guessed. Nothing uses rdf:JSON or a serialized JSON
literal. Original compact JWS bytes remain a proof string, not a claim-value blob.

The field table below covers all eleven bodies, the envelope, action, agreement and
supporting references. Cardinalities describe the object binding; ordered collections
use position-bearing nodes and presentFields retains an explicitly empty optional list.
Existing awp terms retain their established meanings. New awp terms are limited to
protocol-specific structure without a published equivalent; named domain values use
schema.org, authorship uses PROV, purposes use DPV and credentials use VC 2.0.

**Part 3 checkpoint — complete object model; default wire capacity remains partial.**
`src/model/message-nodes.ts` contains live wrappers and `toNode`/`fromNode` static
converters for all eleven bodies, Message, ActionIntent, Agreement, ContentPart,
ParticipantRef, AudienceRef, OperationRef, Basis, invocation challenge, challenge needs,
authorship and the runtime's additional intent evidence references. `evidence-item.ts`
provides the EvidenceItem converters. `CoreNode` is shared typed-field infrastructure;
`NamedValuesNode`/`NamedValueNode` implement the named-value/list model. No original core
declaration or runtime reader was changed. The public model export now exposes the
complete MessageNode; `MessageNode.create` preserves the earlier envelope-only builder
for compatibility, while **`MessageNode.toNode` constructs the complete graph**. Existing
MessageEnvelopeNode and IntentScopeNode remain projections and are not complete graphs.

The test-only hooks in `test/helpers/model-corpus.ts` wrap packetView, authorise and the
memory ledger append method, then restore them. They run all scenarios and declared
configurations without editing runtime/engine/gate code. The corpus contains **1,345
packet occurrences, 664 distinct full messages, 650 distinct ActionIntents and 32 distinct
Agreements across 165 executable paths**. All **1,346 distinct objects** round-trip with
strict equality, including optional/empty fields, lexical times, arrays and evidence.
Writing and reading each graph gives the identical RDFC-1.0 canonical N-Quads and passes
its SHACL shape. There are **zero value/model round-trip failures** in this corpus.

**Capacity qualification, not a runtime limit change.** Twenty-six expanded documents
exceed the unchanged default 65,536-byte wire envelope. Three large joint-computation
messages also need more than 512 flat included nodes. Six forum intents already contain
literal `parameters.request` strings of 103,480–149,099 characters; these are existing
byte-bound action parameters, not a new JSON encoding of the parameters object. The
object builder retains them, with a separate bounded construction cap. The corpus test
first exercises the default writer and records every refusal, then uses explicit
**test-only** bounds of 1 MiB per document, 262,144 characters per string, 50,000 JSON
nodes and 8,192 array members for those cases. Depth stays 16. No default bound in
`core/limits.ts`, no running configuration and no gate decision changes. The precise
(type, document/operation, source digest, refusal and expanded size) inventory is retained
in `test/fixtures/message-model-capacity.json`. These 26 documents **cannot round-trip
through the default wire limits**; 22d must address their carriage capacity before a
blanket runtime switch. The larger test bounds are not claimed as baseline admission.

The final codegen attempt has **182 errors and 23 advisory warnings** in
`src/generated/full-admission.json` (205 diagnostics in total), superseding the 157-error
Part 2 checkpoint. No generator code/admission was weakened. Exact rejected constructs
include `sh:closed`, `sh:ignoredProperties`, `sh:hasValue`, `sh:or`, `sh:xone`, `sh:class`,
shape-level `sh:node`, inline/unprojected composite links, `BlankNodeOrIRI` / `IRIOrLiteral`
node kinds, the digest pattern and byte-list numeric bounds. GrantRecord, InvokeBodyChallenge and MessageAuthorship
are generated from their complete admitted shapes, and complete inputs
remain pinned in the lock.

Hand-written class accounting: ParticipantRefNode, AudienceRefNode and OperationRefNode
correspond to their closed/ignored-properties reference shapes; the eleven `*BodyNode`
classes and MessageNode correspond to their closed, kind-constrained/composite shapes.
BasisNode corresponds to RequestBasisShape's exclusive reference choices; InvokeChallengeNode
and MessageAuthorshipNode are thin facades over generated component accessors, rather
than rejected classes; ChallengeNeedsNode corresponds to its
closed exclusive choices; ContentPartNode to its string/byte-list union. ActionIntentNode
and AgreementNode correspond to their open-value and composite links. IntentEvidenceRefNode
uses its closed reference shape. EvidenceItemNode uses the exclusive embedded/reference
shape. EmbeddedCredentialNode and its private EmbeddedSubjectNode use the rejected
credential/subject/proof links (CredentialNode itself is reused from solid-vc).
NamedValuesNode and NamedValueNode use NamedValueCollectionShape, ItemListShape and
NamedValueShape, rejected for closed/composite/union constraints. CoreNode and the older
RdfNode are infrastructure, not generated domain classes. Exact subjects and messages,
including normalized property-shape identifiers, are in the admission artifact.

**Part 4 — done for today's evidence and its legacy presentations.** A compact JWS is
projected to a VC 2.0 credential graph, viewed through solid-vc's CredentialNode. Its
issuer (or representation party), validity and issue metadata are explicit; its subject
holds the original signed fields as named domain values. The credential links through
`sec:proof` to a **named proof graph**, containing an `awp:CompactJwsProof` and the
**unchanged original compact JWS** under `awp:jws`. The named proof type explicitly says
that the issuer signed the original JWS payload, **not the translated RDF graph**.
`EmbeddedCredentialNode.verifyOriginal` verifies the original protected header/payload
bytes with pinned material and the caller's required typ, then compares the entire
credential/subject/value/proof closure to its deterministic projection. Thus a graph
claim cannot drift while borrowing the original signature. Parsing the payload alone
establishes no integrity, authority, authorization or truth.

EvidenceItemNode holds either that credential/presentation or a `credentialDigest`,
exclusively. The reference digest is `sha-256:<hex>` of the exact previously sent compact
JWS bytes. Resolution is injected, never fetched; missing/substituted bytes fail. The
legacy fromNode converter can take that resolver. RDF/JS reserves `.value` for the node
identifier, so the original evidence string is exposed as `.statement` (and through the
object converter), not by overriding the RDF term's value getter.

The corpus also contains selective-disclosure and mandate-chain strings, which are JSON
envelopes rather than compact JWS statements. These become VC presentations with their
actual signed credentials embedded; their original envelope bytes and named envelope
fields are retained. No envelope signature is invented. Verifying a presentation as an
individual compact credential fails; its nested credentials retain real proofs. 22d must
continue dispatching the original selective/chain mechanisms, including disclosure and
chain checks, on the retained bytes; verifying only a nested signature establishes less.

**Part 5 — done.** Six fixtures are in `test/fixtures/whole-messages/`: propose, deliver
with a credential, and invoke, each as JWS/JCS and eddsa-rdfc-2022. Their main-path source
ids are respectively `...sam-agent:0003`, `...north-agent:0005`, and `...sam-agent:0006`
under `urn:awp:engine-mortgage-core-main:`. The deliver carries the lender's signed
statement, not merely representation evidence. Each fixture fits default bounds. The
manifest retains the exact original object and public key; no private keys are stored.
Tests rerun mortgage-core to check the sources, reproduce the deterministic signatures,
read/validate/verify the fixtures, reject tampering and unknown keys, and verify the DI
versions again after lossless TriG reserialization. These are fixture documents, not a
claim that the running carriage uses RDF. No commit was made despite the brief's word
“commit” for fixtures: its explicit no-commit instruction is preserved.

`writeDocument` uses a flat `@included` layout for complete named values/proof graphs to
avoid artificial nesting growth; it still checks graph equality. DI verification now
reads the outer proof's exact six fields from its RDF proof graph and removes **only that
outer proof** from the signature input. Embedded credential proof graphs remain covered.
JWS continues to verify its exact JCS document. Tests cover unknown/extra/missing proof
options, ambiguous fields, wrong datatypes, suite/purpose/method errors and embedded-proof
tampering. Verification-method IRIs with a second fragment are refused: WHATWG URL
acceptance alone let JSON-LD drop such a method. Open legacy domain strings with those
old malformed identifiers remain strings, preserving their original bytes rather than
silently losing triples or repairing identifiers.

**22d integration notes.** AgentRuntime.emit must build/sign the exact complete node with
MessageNode.toNode, retain that dataset for release/replay, and bind approvals to the
selected suite's full-document digest. Do not send the compatibility create/envelope
projection. receiveInner and InboundGate.verifyIntegrity must call the selected wire
verify/readDocument boundary and pass MessageNode over that **same admitted dataset**;
read scalar and linked operands through the wrappers. Require Message plus exactly one
matching kind type at message ingress: the general document reader also admits the
credential, intent, agreement and supporting document classes. The outbound gate must inspect
that same complete graph, including named parameters, credentials and original content,
before signing/releasing it. Keep the issuer's original JWS verification/projection check,
role/typ and pinned-key resolution; a sender's outer signature does not establish source
authority. Cache and resolve prior credential digests explicitly. Preserve selective/chain
verification and their narrower conclusions. Resolve the 26 default-capacity refusals
before switching all traffic, keep named proof graphs in a lossless RDF format, and keep
the immutable decision oracle while replacing the 15 retained declarations. Native
RDF wrappers, not fromNode legacy object snapshots, must become gate operands. Current
ActionIntent action strings remain the existing literal ODRL-level labels in this model;
no action class or payment interpretation has been remapped in this lane.

No dependencies, lockfile, generator implementation, payment terms, gate/runtime/engine/
settlement/suite sources, profiles, scenarios or maintained outlines were changed.
Required build/scenario commands regenerate trace/manifest artifacts; those generated
rewrites are restored to HEAD after checking the command result. The RDF allow-list is
unchanged: **15 hard core declarations plus 213 bounded exceptions**, with no new finding.
Final command results follow after the last validation pass.

**Final vocabulary review.** Shared expiry, approval and nonce properties have resource
rather than single-owner domains, and operation has a resource range: body operation
fields constrain a versioned reference, while ActionIntent carries an operation IRI.
The owning shapes retain those distinctions. Basis kind individuals now have the separate
BasisKind class; they are not themselves request-basis nodes needing another kind/reference.
This changes no value or decision. The outer proof graph must have exactly one proof
owner; a shared graph is refused so excluding the outer proof cannot also exclude an
embedded credential's proof. Its adversarial test checks verification and digest refusal.

**Final status and results.** Parts 1, 2, 4 and 5 are implemented. Part 3's complete model,
converters and exhaustive actual-corpus round trips are implemented; its **default wire
capacity condition remains partial for the 26 explicitly listed documents**. Runtime
migration is intentionally not attempted in this brief. Those capacity cases are **12
invokes, 8 deliveries and 6 intents**, with expanded documents of **65,992–477,212 bytes**.
They pass object/graph equality and shape checks under the explicit test-only capacity;
none is silently admitted under the unchanged defaults. All three selected whole-message
fixtures fit the defaults under both suites.

Final reference tests: **624 tests in 63 files**, plus **17 unchanged evaluation-fixture
tests in one file** (641 total). Starting counts were 583 + 17. The complete corpus has
zero model/value round-trip failures. The immutable 157-path decision oracle passes,
with its original SHA-256 still
`5352929414096fbaeaf0958d95338c2a4192f7fb7ae0c491d867c34a5a6d0b8e`.
Required scenarios still report **165 live paths: 154 runs, 11 disagreements, 9 blocked**.
The final ODRL suite passes **912 tests in 13 files**, superseding the 898-test Part 1
checkpoint; the extra cases cover an empty query component following an r-component
and HTTP Unicode whitespace. Independent security review remains required before merge.

The aggregate Message/body graph still has the recorded admission failures. Full tests

above. The capacity inventory was refreshed from that final run; source digests for
fresh joint-protocol ciphertext can differ between runs without changing any decision.


## Brief 25

<!-- AUTHORED-BY Codex GPT-6 -->

Implementation is offline under Node 24 from `/opt/node24/bin`, without commits or
new dependencies. The required Brief 24 and 25p reports and the sole protocol research
file were read. All pre-existing protocol functions are retained. Work stays within
settlement adapters, their profile/configuration descriptions, tests and this append.

**Live wiring stopped at the ownership boundary.** The running outbound gate constructs
`new RdfOrderSuite()` in `src/gates/outbound.ts:paymentOrder`; its return value is a
single RDF document. There is no settlement-adapter injection point. The invoice and
provider paths in `src/engine/run.ts` also construct that concrete suite. Ordinary
receipt ingress and native-document trace production use `src/wire/payment.ts`.
The brief forbids editing the gate, runtime, wire and trace trees, and limits this lane
to settlement/profile/configuration work. Installing four configurations without those
changes would label RDF payments as protocol executions. That portion is stopped:
no new live mortgage run, manifest run row or baseline oracle entry is claimed.
The adapters and unchanged P1–P8/receipt checks are tested independently instead.

**Synchronous lift contract.** `SettlementSuite.lift` returns `PaymentFacts` immediately,
whereas the existing JOSE verifiers return promises. Following the explicit Brief 24
adapter handover, each async request/order/settle admission invokes the protocol's
verifier in its `lift.ts`, then pins the complete raw and shown objects. `lift()` accepts
only such exact admitted content and returns a fresh dataset snapshot. Caller-provided
`native` values confer no admission. A mandatory host `admit` callback is the seam for
the ordinary inbound checks; isolated tests authenticate their local test carriage but
do not claim to execute AgentRuntime's five inbound gates. Actual runtime integration
of this callback and protocol-key source admission remains part of the stopped wiring.

**Part 1 checkpoint.** `settlement/x402/index.ts` implements all four suite methods;
`x402/lift.ts` admits one exact/EVM requirement, signature and settlement header, with
explicit currency precision and account mapping. The provider verifies then settles
on the existing ledger and identity-signs a separate `payment-receipt` statement binding
its protocol transcript and order digest. x402 does not sign SettlementResponse.
`x402-suite.test.ts` passes seven isolated tests, including P1/P2, replay, source/key
refusal, pinned object tampering, receipt checking and `x402-recipient-swapped`:
`invalid_exact_evm_payload_recipient_mismatch` / engine `evidence-invalid`, zero debits.
Full shared-failure equality and later parts are recorded below as they finish.

**Part 2 checkpoint.** `mpp/index.ts` and `mpp/lift.ts` wire the HMAC-bound 402
challenge, JCS/base64url request, gate-created credential, EIP-3009 verification and
provider ledger receipt. `externalId` binds the request to the agreement digest; the
host binds the challenge to the engine once-key. MPP's receipt has no signature; the
local provider statement authenticates it. Six isolated adapter tests pass, including
`mpp-challenge-reused`: fresh challenge, 402 invalid-challenge problem, engine
`evidence-invalid`, exactly one ledger entry. HMAC and externalId tampering, missing
settlement-key statements, credential tampering and P1/P2 refusals are covered.

**Part 3 checkpoint.** `ap2/index.ts`, `ap2/lift.ts` and the explicitly local
`ap2/receipt.ts` implement person-present checkout, both ES256 SD-JWT mandates and
merchant/processor ES256 receipts. Existing `sdjwt.ts` and its disclosure tests are
reused unchanged. Identity-signed secondary-key statements are mandatory for the
person, merchant and second fictional processor. The receipt facts attribute settlement
to the processor, so the receipt check's expected provider must be that processor,
not the credential provider; the live runner currently installs only its RDF provider.
Five isolated tests pass, including a genuinely person-signed wrong transaction_id
refused as `terms-digest-mismatch`, tampered disclosure, missing key statements, receipt
pinning and concurrent single settlement. The optional autonomous shopping run has
not been implemented; its runtime/P5 mandate wiring is outside this lane's ownership.

**Part 4 checkpoint.** `acp/index.ts` and `acp/lift.ts` wrap session creation, delegation
and completion in endpoint-scoped idempotency stores. Agent POSTs and merchant responses
use the existing detached identity signatures and timestamps; bearer identity is bound
to the installed fictional account. ACP leaves signatures optional; this profile requires
them. Card details are replaced with `{type: account, account_id}`. Five initial isolated
tests pass, including signed changed-body completion returning 422 idempotency_conflict,
engine `evidence-invalid`, exactly one debit; identical retries return the exact cached
response, unsigned requests/responses stop with `unsigned-not-allowed`, and substituted
tokens cannot settle. The unchanged provider receipt check admits the separately signed
provider statement and completed session order.

**Shared checks and configuration checkpoint.** `suite-equality.test.ts` proves the
three isolated P1/P2/P3 refusals have exactly the same check and reason across
`rdf-order`, `x402`, `mpp`, `ap2` and `acp`: P1/no-agreement, P2/amount-differs,
P3/payee-differs. These fixtures deliberately isolate payment checking; their offer and
acceptance proof markers are not real mortgage negotiations. Separately, the actual
unchanged runner was invoked in memory with each proposed settlement configuration:
all sixteen mortgage paths are blocked before a trace is produced, with "no executable
runtime configuration installed". `settlement/configurations.ts` retains those exact
single-layer declarations as blocked candidates; it does not install wire definitions
or advertise them as running. No scenario, live trace, outline or manifest row is added.

`settlement/profiles.ts` records the version/subset, closed-instruction guarantee,
key/source requirements and exact stand-ins. Per-field `from` maps accompany each
adapter trace's JSON-LD facts, including explicit host origins for fields absent from
the protocol. Inlining those origin annotations into the facts graph itself would
require new vocabulary/shape/model work in the other lane; it is not faked with
unrecognised JSON-LD fields. The existing trace recorder does not yet attach native
protocol traces to actual messages; adapters expose `trace()` for that integration.

The immutable 161-path live oracle passes without a snapshot update; current oracle
SHA-256 is `b4c928de53dc96228087009792da1be646f760e705d6343b48a8441c841d6f25`.
The RDF guard remains exactly 15 hard failures and 213 allowances. Additional isolated
adversarial tests cover check-digest forgery, changed checked facts, cross-suite input,
provider-account substitution, parser/signature failures, returned-dataset mutation,
field origins, signer-handle mismatch before debit, whole-second EIP-3009 expiry,
zero-decimal JPY, and a second MPP challenge for an already ordered engine once-key.
Receipt tests separately prove wrong-key refusals, unsigned response transcript binding,
AP2 transaction binding and genuinely signed wrong-amount receipts reaching the
unchanged receipt-mismatch check.

**What each protocol gives the checks.** “Host” below means authenticated host state
and admitted source statements, never a field supplied as authority by a core. This
maps the isolated adapters; the four new live configurations remain blocked.

| Check | rdf-order | x402 | mpp | ap2 | acp |

| P2 amount and currency | `schema:price` / `schema:totalPaymentDue`, `schema:priceCurrency` | `accepts[0].amount`, authorization `value`; currency and precision supplied by configured asset profile | Request `amount/currency`, credential `value`; configured precision | `checkout_jwt.total_price/currency`, Payment Mandate `payment_amount.amount/currency`; configured precision | Session `totals[0].amount/currency`, allowance `max_amount/currency`; configured precision |
| P3 agreed payee | `schema:recipient`, admitted account ownership | `payTo`, authorization `to`; party ownership supplied by authenticated resource server and accepted account source | Request `recipient`, credential `to`; party ownership supplied by accepted account source | `checkout_jwt.merchant.id`, Payment Mandate `payee.id`; account ownership supplied by host | Session / allowance `merchant_id` is this profile's ledger account; party ownership supplied by host account binding |
| P4 accepted account source | Not in RDF invoice alone: admitted `payee-account` statement and its current terms | Not in protocol: admitted binding of `did:pkh:eip155:84532:<payTo>` to valuer | Not in protocol: admitted binding of `did:pkh:eip155:84532:<recipient>` to valuer | Not in protocol: admitted merchant-to-ledger-account binding | Not in protocol: admitted merchant-to-`merchant_id` account binding |
