Open-constraint P5 integration is unfinished | Not in protocol: host signed mandate/limits; allowance does not grant permission |
| P6 required approval | Not in payment protocol: gate's terms/order-digest-bound owner approval | Not in protocol: gate approval before EIP-3009 signing | Not in protocol: gate approval before credential signing | Person's ES256 closed mandates reflect the gate approval; approval decision and digest binding supplied by host | Not in protocol: gate approval before delegate_payment |
| P7 once | `awp:onceKey`, held agreement instalment and persistent engine record | Authorization `nonce` derived from `awp:onceKey`; shared signer/domain nonce ledger | Challenge `id` and credential `nonce` = challenge hash; host binds challenge to engine once-key and forbids a second instruction | Key-binding `nonce` and checkout `transaction_id`; host once-key association and provider consumed-transaction state | Every POST `Idempotency-Key` derived from engine once-key, endpoint cache and one-use `vt_` token; host session association |
| P8 closed instruction | RDF recipient/account/price/currency/validUntil and executable adapter | Authorization `to/value/validAfter/validBefore`, configured token domain/currency | Credential `to/value/validAfter/validBefore` bound to issued charge challenge/domain | Closed Payment Mandate `payee/payment_amount/payment_instrument/transaction_id`; expiry supplied by checked host order and key-binding freshness | Allowance `reason/max_amount/currency/checkout_session_id/merchant_id/expires_at`, single-use delegated token |
| receipt | RDF `awp:settles`, `schema:about/recipient/price/priceCurrency/actionStatus/confirmationNumber`, provider attribution | `SettlementResponse.success/transaction/payer/network`; missing amount/payee/agreement/order/source supplied by signed facilitator `payment-receipt` | `Payment-Receipt.status/reference/challengeId/chainId`; missing amount/payee/agreement/order/source supplied by signed provider `payment-receipt` | Processor Payment Receipt and merchant Checkout Receipt ES256 JWTs; all field names are the explicit local receipt profile in `ap2/receipt.ts` | Completed session `status/order`, plus signed provider `payment-receipt` for ledger reference, amount, party, currency, agreement and recorded order |

**Not from the specification.** The existing 25p choices remain exactly as documented
in its sections above: strict example-field subsets, HTTP/JSON bounds and duplicate
rejection, EIP-712 wire construction, money precision, MPP HMAC/hash serialization,
AP2 SD-JWT serialization/freshness and ACP session/signature/token formats are not new
confirmed specification material. This brief adds the following local choices:

- `AdapterProfile.admit` is a mandatory host integration seam, not a protocol operation
  or a substitute implementation of the five inbound gates. Tests sign a local carriage
  object with each fictional identity; they do not call it an ordinary live message run.
- Async admission plus a digest-indexed `LiftStore` implements the synchronous interface.
  Both decoded inspector content and raw protocol objects are pinned; `native` has no
  authority. Snapshots prevent callers mutating admitted datasets or pending requests.
  All stores and pending associations are in memory and must be retained across exchanges;
  process restart/distributed durability is not implemented here.
- The local identity-signed key statement is `awp-settlement-key+jws` with
  `type/party/suite/algorithm/publicKey/validFrom/validUntil`. It uses the pinned party
  identity, not an embedded key. `publicKey` holds the recovered EVM address for
  secp256k1 or a public P-256 JWK for ES256. Payer and AP2 person/merchant/processor
  bindings are checked before use. Signing-handle possession is internally checked
  before ledger effects, including non-extractable keys; this is not a protocol object.
- The valuation resource is `GET /valuation-booking` at
  `https://valuer.example/valuation-booking`; MPP realm is `valuer.example`. The EVM
  profile fixes the researched test network 84532 and takes the token address/domain
  and precision from explicit fictional configuration. No live asset registry or chain
  is consulted. EVM account IRIs use `did:pkh:eip155:84532:<lowercase address>`.
- EIP-3009 validity starts one second before signing. Checked expiry must be exactly
  representable in whole seconds. The sample profile uses at most 300 seconds, with
  MPP challenge expiry and all checked order expiries within the held agreement; every order rechecks expiry after
  asynchronous admission. The EVM adapter once-key-to-nonce derivation uses the existing JCS/SHA-256
  `digestOf` over the once-key string. MPP challenge `opaque` combines the invoice
  digest and a retained counter; fresh refusal challenges use a distinct counter.
- The local facilitator/provider `awp-payment-receipt+jws` claim contains
  `claimType/provider/reference/protocolDigest/orderDigest/payment`, where `payment`
  retains the normalized checked order scope needed by `PaymentFacts.receipt`.
  It signs the exact protocol transcript as well as the order digest. This supplies
  facts absent from the unsigned x402 and MPP responses and from ACP's order object.
  Genuine signed semantic differences remain for `checkReceipt` to reject.
- AP2 receipt field tables are unconfirmed. `ap2/receipt.ts` explicitly specifies the
  local processor claim `type/transaction_id/processor/reference/order_digest/payment`
  and merchant claim `type/checkout_hash/order_id/merchant/payment_receipt_hash`.
  Their JWT types are `ap2-payment-receipt+jwt` and `ap2-checkout-receipt+jwt`.
  Checkout ids follow the host invoice association; the sample has one fictional
  valuation line item and merchant website. `payment_instrument` is a fictional
  provider account (`type: account`); no card credential or FIDO claim is made.
  The closed mandate carries no researched expiry field: the host checks its recorded
  order expiry in addition to the existing key-binding freshness rule. Receipt facts
  identify the merchant's processor, not Sam's credential provider.
- ACP uses explicit uppercase engine currency / lowercase native ledger currency
  without conversion, no exchange rate, hash-derived `cs_` ids, sequence-derived order
  ids and fictional order permalinks. Path segments are percent-encoded. Bearer tokens
  are checked against installed account configuration. Provider delegation responses
  are signed too. `risk_signals` and `metadata` are empty; card data is replaced by the
  provider account reference. Every POST uses the same derived once-key in separate
  endpoint scopes. Errors returned by these wrappers map to `evidence-invalid`, except
  required-signature absence (`unsigned-not-allowed`) and AP2 checkout mismatch
  (`terms-digest-mismatch`); protocol codes and HTTP responses remain inspectable.
- Protocol object `form` labels and top-level per-field `from` maps are inspector
  metadata, not protocol fields or authority. The exact HTTP/no-money stand-in is in
  every proposed suite profile; no chain, live HTTP, card network or real money moves.

selection change, not a change to P1–P8. The engine's invoice/provider dispatch needs
the corresponding adapter methods. `AgentRuntime.receivedPaymentOrder` and the native

Source admission needs the secondary-key statement and accepted EVM account bindings;
AP2 additionally needs the separate processor and its expected receipt-source profile.
The trace recorder must use adapter traces instead of the RDF-only `paymentTrace`.

configuration runs recorded and their actual manifest rows published. None of these
other-lane files was edited. The optional autonomous AP2 shopping form is not wired.

## Brief 26b

<!-- AUTHORED-BY Codex GPT-6 -->

Work is offline and uncommitted. Suite implementation proceeds in the requested order. The storage stand-in uses a separately keyed fictional provider, authenticated in-process HTTP requests, SDK RDF/ACL/listing helpers, WAC on every supported operation, guarded fetch with a fixed fictional DNS mapping, byte limits, and an operator-visible access log. Four adversarial storage tests pass. The newly reused workspace dependencies `@jeswr/guarded-fetch` and `@jeswr/ldp-container` passed the offline package-policy check before addition. The listing package lacked installed workspace links in this sandbox; local ignored node_modules links were restored and its existing source was built without network or dependency lifecycle scripts.

**Specification limits identified before suite implementation.** WAC does not express an expiry time or a downstream purpose. Timed grants therefore require an enforcing withdrawal mechanism, and purposes/copying remain gate-enforced terms. Specification 10 still calls `solid-inbox` Sketch and specification 17 calls `solid-access-control` Sketch; those supplied source files are outside this working copy. Implementation and remaining gaps are reported here rather than silently claiming their status text changed.

**Suite 1 — `data-access-by-reference-solid`: NOT RUN.** The suite and the mortgage configuration are registered unresolved, with both `onlyUnder` failure branches and their prescribed expected checks. The local holder is explicitly not reused as Solid. Specification 11, by-reference bullets 1–2 and design 26 §5 require one outbound action coupling the exact WAC grant to the pointer, and a receiver read admitted as a signed message through all five inbound checks. The current runtime's `readReference` verifies a local `ResourceGrant` and calls statement admission, while the mortgage handler delivers by-value credentials and the outbox has no atomic storage-grant transaction. Implementing that transaction and reference-message model is unfinished here; it is not a claim that Solid cannot support reference access. Confinement and both failure outcomes are NOT RUN, with the exact blocker in the manifest. A regression test requires all three cells to remain blocked without traces. The general confinement test now distinguishes an explicitly unresolved configuration (required by design §2) from an executable one; executable configurations must still run and pass the unchanged comparison.

The by-reference cell also exposes a pre-existing registry/design disagreement: `SUITE_CONSTRAINTS` clause `by-reference-needs-access-control` requires changing usage control as well. Design §2 mandates exactly one changed layer. The actual matrix blocker is therefore this constraint, now reported by name and text instead of the old generic constraint message. It was preserved, not relaxed to fabricate a single-layer run. This constraint alone prevents the requested single-layer by-reference path even after the missing transaction work; the unresolved definition records the additional implementation gaps. Its regression test checks the actual constraint blocker.

**Suite 2 — `transport-solid-inbox`: RUNS.** The complete mortgage and payment journey runs between the existing separately keyed agents. Only Sam/lender carriage changes. Each message uses receiver-description inbox discovery, an authenticated POST, a receiver-authenticated container listing and GET, with the runtime's bytes and media type unchanged. Confinement passes with no verdict or final-outcome differences. The `inbox-item-changed-at-rest` branch mutates a stored signature without re-signing; the ordinary receiver integrity gate refuses with `bad-signature`, and packets name Solid inbox carriage. Two suite tests cover the real refusal and opaque JSON-LD carriage plus actual request identities. No runtime, gate, wire or trace-owned file was changed for this suite.

**Suite 3 — `accountability-pod-mirror`: RUNS.** The complete journey mirrors Sam's actual hash-chained ledger, entry by entry, as gate-key-signed resources at an independent fictional provider. Sam's principal key can read the record without the agent operator. The outbound evidence gate checks availability before transition/release, then mirrors the seal/decision/transfer entries before making the outbox entry visible. Effects use the same readiness seam. The runner also drains non-outbound entries after each completed script step and at completion. Confinement passes. In `mirror-unreachable`, the real evidence gate gives `indeterminate` / `record-unavailable`, the action waits, the fictional provider returns, and the journey completes. Tests require no outgoing message between the hold and recovery, plus principal-only reads, signatures, outage handling and conflicting-copy rejection. No baseline verdict changed.

The specification fixes the evidence verdict but not its core wait outcome. This implementation maps a recoverable record gap to `ask-more`, not a terminal stop; the fixture host restores the provider after the wait has been recorded. Persistent background recovery across runtime restart is not implemented. A permanently unavailable provider fails closed without exposing the action. No unrecorded external recovery or durable production service is claimed.

**Suite 4 — `usage-control-solid-access-control`: NOT RUN.** The suite, unresolved configuration and `translation-grants-more` branch (`data-terms` / `prohibited`) are registered. Specification 17's suite begins “The data stays in a pod”; design §2 retains the baseline `by-value` data-access layer in this cell. Adding an ACL alongside the existing by-value disclosure would not make that sentence true or protect the delivered copy. Coupling access and reference delivery requires the missing protected-reference transaction identified under suite 1. This part is stopped, rather than publishing a by-value trace under a pod-only claim. Confinement and failure outcome are NOT RUN; the manifest names that exact missing capability. The WAC expiry limitation recorded above also applies. No policy extension, permissive translation or weaker grant check is substituted.

**Resume after the machine restart, 14 November 01:19 UTC.** The saved implementation was read before continuing. Its TypeScript compilation passed; the first full build stopped on stale generated scenario schema output. Regenerating that schema repaired the build prerequisite. The RDF pipeline guard remains at exactly **15 hard failures and 213 allowances**, unchanged from the brief. The first reference-suite run exposed the old matrix test's fixed transport-only count and a pre-existing proof-fixture locator that examined only the first named graph; the matrix test now covers every registered configuration and explicit blocked cell, and the proof test searches all named graphs while retaining the same tamper/digest assertions. No oracle or maintained outline is updated.

**Suite 5 — `identity-webid`: RUNS.** Sam's and the lender's actor and principal profiles are served by a separately keyed fictional storage provider. `@jeswr/solid-vc` publishes and resolves Controlled Identifier Multikey verification methods through guarded, bounded, redirect-refusing fetches. An independently keyed issuer-only register signs the names, verification methods, thumbprints and explicit validity period, with no public JWK in its answer. The inbound integrity check verifies the actual message using the fetched key after matching the signed thumbprint commitment; outbound recipient discovery also checks that binding. Source authority and representation retain their ordinary separate checks. The complete mortgage configuration passes confinement. `profile-key-swapped` changes the lender's stored profile without changing the register commitment and the actual integrity gate refuses with `sender-key-mismatch`. Three focused tests pass, covering real signatures, both profiles, takeover, unknown names, expiry, unsafe fetches, confinement and readable trace sentences. The full-suite result and its inherited model failure are recorded below.

**Protected-file seams for the Brief 22d merge.** `src/gates/common.ts` adds host-injected `recordMirror` and `webId` dependencies. `src/gates/outbound.ts` adds `ensureRecord` and `webIdRecipient`; neither changes a baseline verdict when the dependency is absent. `src/runtime/agent.ts` calls record readiness before and after sealing an effect, before an outgoing conversation transition, and after sealing/recording transfers but before publishing the outbox entry. Its `emit` also calls recipient discovery for the WebID configuration. `src/gates/inbound.ts:verifyIntegrity` selects the injected fetched WebID key and records that verification mechanism; the existing signature, schema, sender, configuration, audience, time and replay checks still run. These are the only edits in the other lane's owned paths. No core, wire, trace or existing model file is edited; the new storage RDF view is `src/model/solid-storage.ts`. Inbox delivery continues to carry the runtime's bytes and media type without parsing or re-signing them. The current branch still has the earlier compact-JWS receive seam; moving that seam to signed RDF documents remains Brief 22d's work, not a parallel implementation here.

**Stand-ins.** Existing mortgage/payment stand-ins remain inherited. The added declarations name: (1) the fictional inbox storage provider, its public-address DNS mapping and authenticated in-process HTTP; (2) the independent fictional principal record provider, operated separately from the agent operator, with principal-controlled WAC; and (3) the fictional WebID profile provider plus the independently keyed issuer-only WebID commitment register. The unresolved usage-control world also declares its proposed fictional provider, but has no published trace. Every new provider/register is an actual separately keyed source party. The storage signs its HTTP audit receipts; that signature is not message authority. The operator observes each request, including the actor and resource of each read, and sees the stored message/record/profile contents. Nothing here claims encryption from the storage operator, a live pod, a network exchange, or production Solid authentication.

**Adversarial review fixes during resume.** Simultaneous mirror flushes could advance the cursor more than once per entry; serialization now prevents that gap, and the new regression demonstrated the old failure (cursor six for two entries) before passing with the fix. Storage request claims previously relied on a TypeScript cast: a signed object/array nonce could evade replay identity comparison, and a signed null payload could throw. Strict claim validation now refuses those with HTTP 401 before mutation. The test also changes the body and mutation headers, then proves the original request succeeds exactly once and leaves exactly one stored resource. These fixes are confined to the new stand-in files. The package README now carries the executable alternatives and explicit blocked-work roadmap.

**Not from a specification.**

1. The storage's `FictionalSignature` HTTP authentication is a local stand-in: Ed25519 JWS binds the requester, exact method/URL, body digest, relevant mutation headers, timestamp and fresh nonce. Its one-minute freshness window and nonce table are local choices; this is not Solid-OIDC or DPoP. The supported surface is GET/HEAD, POST to an existing container, PUT, DELETE and `.acl`, with own/nearest-ancestor WAC, Write implying Append, and Control separate from body access. PATCH, groups, notifications, arbitrary content negotiation and a complete Solid server are outside this subset. Resources used by these suites are UTF-8 documents.
2. General storage responses are capped at one MiB; profile/ACL parsing uses smaller bounds. The fictional DNS seam maps only the configured host to a public address. Every client still passes the SDK's private-address, scheme, DNS-pinning and redirect checks. Profiles refuse redirects altogether, a stricter choice than re-checking a redirect destination.
3. Inboxes have private receiver listing/read access and named-peer Append permission. The receiver's public RDF description names exactly one inbox. There is one authoritative stored structured copy, with no separate prose channel copy. Core outcomes and correlation remain inside the signed message; HTTP statuses establish carriage only. There is no automatic retry after an uncertain POST. An explicit retry must retain the signed message identifier and pass the ordinary replay check (TR-4/5/7/8). The host reads the created member only after finding it in the actual container listing.
4. Mirrored entries use deterministic sequence-number resource names, signed entry bytes and conditional create. An existing different copy is refused. Concurrent flushes are serialized so an entry cannot advance the cursor twice and omit a later entry; the regression test verifies a later independently read signed entry as well as the cursor. The evidence wait maps to `ask-more`; only the fixture host restores availability. There is no background retry daemon or persisted waiting-action recovery across restart. Principal reads use the principal key directly; the record is private and retained only for the in-memory run. Production retention/deletion governance and migration tooling are not supplied (AC-3/4).
5. WebID register commitments are a local signed JWS format, separate from source-authority answers. They carry actor/principal WebIDs, selected Multikey method names, thumbprints and a sixty-day window. No key supplied by an incoming message can replace them. Discovery uses only a size-bounded untrusted configuration hint, then verifies the exact original message using the fetched key. The supplied WebID profile is current, without a resolver cache. Expired commitments fail closed; historical-key archives and an interactive rotation/recovery service are not implemented (ID-4/5). Principals and agents retain distinct names and keys, operators retain their separate organization names, and names remain linkable across relationships (ID-1/6). Ancillary bank, index and payment conversations retain their explicitly advertised baseline/public configuration.
6. The immutable decision oracle and all maintained outlines are preserved. The pre-existing matrix/label tests were generalized for additional layers and honest unresolved cells; the proof-graph test only changes its fixture lookup. No existing outcome/reason assertion is relaxed. The design's short identity feed description differs from specification 12's `integrity`, `recipient`, `authority` list; the already-installed confinement feed table follows the specification and is unchanged.

**Artifact audit.** The initial manifest has 178 rows. The new manifest has **189 rows: 164 runs, 11 disagrees, 14 blocked**. The eleven additions are six actual runs (main plus failure for inbox, mirror and WebID) and five explicitly blocked cells (by-reference main/two branches and access-control main/branch). All 178 previous rows are identical apart from regenerated real-joint trace hashes. Baseline totals stay **150 runs, 11 disagrees, 9 blocked**, with 161 executable baseline paths. The oracle file remains byte-for-byte unchanged, SHA-256 `b4c928de53dc96228087009792da1be646f760e705d6343b48a8441c841d6f25`. Generated trace changes include the new supported-configuration advertisements and build metadata; the sandbox's Node child-process git lookup records `unknown`, never an invented revision. No maintained outline has a diff. A separate scan of all six new traces finds zero HTTP(S)/URN identifiers in captions, rule explanations or chat text.

**Full reference validation — not entirely green.** The final serial run completed in 501.92 seconds: **759 passed, 1 failed, 7 skipped; 79 files (78 passed, 1 failed)**. All Solid tests, confinement, generated-byte drift, the corrected proof-graph tamper test and the unchanged 161-path oracle pass. The one failure is `test/complete-model.test.ts`: the existing RDF model cannot convert a payment `accept` evidence envelope (`Unsupported evidence envelope`). The current 175-path corpus contains 909 messages, 747 intents, 54 agreements and **19 conversion failures**. Comparing its failure objects as a multiset with the retained untouched-HEAD log `/tmp/brief25-head-test.log` confirms that **all 16 earlier failure objects remain**, with exactly three more occurrences on the added mortgage configurations (two repeated main-path objects and the mirror-outage branch). There is no new error kind; the larger count is not described as unchanged. The prior HEAD archive had only 163 paths because it lacked optional runtime resources, so it is not claimed as a complete equivalent baseline run. This is the message-model gap already documented under Brief 25. Fixing it requires editing the other lane's model/wire migration, rather than a small storage/transport/resolver seam, so it is left intact and explicitly reported. Neither its assertion nor the oracle is relaxed, and no skipped test was introduced here.

The unsupported-envelope rejection originates in `src/wire/evidence-jws.ts:inspectEvidenceEnvelope` (line 35). That existing wire source and the failing model test remain unchanged.

Offline implementation in progress, no commits. No settlement source is edited. Existing
limits and the immutable 161-path oracle are preserved. Checkpoints below describe actual
completed work; later paragraphs supersede provisional results.

**Part 0 — done.** The 16 unsupported-envelope failures were the payment-era unsigned
`awp-action-intent/0.1` records, not unrecognised signatures on offers/invoices/receipts.
The converter now retains the exact unsigned record as a presentation and links the real
signed credentials carried alongside it. It invents no signature on the intent; its
binding to the acceptance remains the original outer message's signature. Signed parts
are materialised before presentations, irrespective of their retained list positions;
a standalone unsigned intent without signed parts is refused. Original source JWS bytes
and their deterministic projections are unchanged. The proof-graph regression was an
ordering ambiguity: the writer retained both graphs, but put the blank outer DI proof
graph before the named original-JWS graph. Flat `@included` now puts named graphs before
blank graphs, so the original compact proof remains directly inspectable. Both target
tests pass: complete corpus **1/1**, proof graphs **16/16**. Other reference tests:
**674 passed, 7 skipped in 66 files**. `tsc` passed before attachment implementation.
`node scripts/oracle-snapshot.mjs /tmp/oracle-check.json` plus parsed deep comparison:
**161 paths, unchanged**; the live oracle test also passes in the reference run.

**Part 1 — implemented checkpoint.** The unchanged default document bounds now carry all
capacity cases in the complete corpus. The writer extracts owned named-value trees whose
N-Quads representation exceeds **8 KiB**, and literal lexical values exceeding **8 KiB**.
Trees travel as `application/n-quads`; literals retain their exact UTF-8 lexical bytes
with `text/plain;charset=utf-8` and a signed datatype. The signed reference records
`awp:attachmentDigest` (`sha-256:<hex>`), `awp:byteLength`, encoding format and, for literals,
`awp:attachmentDatatype`. Original collection classes remain on graph references. Attachments
are canonical base64 in the JSON-safe packet, outside the signed JSON-LD document.
Final carriage caps are **2 MiB per attachment, 8 MiB per packet, 32 attachments and 32,768
quads per RDF payload**. The initial 1 MiB experiment was too small for an expanded RDF
payload, so these new carriage caps supersede it; no existing bound/configuration changed.
All declared/encoded sizes are checked before decoding/hashing, then all lengths/digests
before any attachment parsing. Missing, extra, duplicate, modified, truncated and oversized
attachments have named refusals. Referenced graph payloads are admitted under their digest;
live named-value wrappers resolve those admitted graphs without expanding the signed
reference dataset. The materialised projection is used only for SHACL and exact original-JWS
projection comparisons. Nine focused carriage tests pass; the complete corpus passes its
new default-bound attachment assertions, including the original 26 capacity documents.
Context terms/hash and shapes are updated. Original corpus equality tests remain.

**Parts 2–3 — substantial implementation; remaining adapters are explicit.** `emit`
constructs `MessageNode.toNode`, prepares attachments, runs the outbound release over its
live graph facade, signs the complete document with the configured wire suite, retains the
exact document/attachments in the durable outbox, and records that same graph. JWS can
detach its redundant encoded payload when retaining it would exceed existing bounds;
verification reconstructs only the JCS bytes of the already bounded document. No envelope
projection is emitted. New document ingress verifies with pinned actor material, admits
attachments, validates the complete materialised shape, requires Message plus exactly one
matching kind, and hands the same admitted dataset's live wrapper to the runtime/gates.
The wrapper facades enumerate protocol fields but read current RDF on every access;
`fromNode` snapshots are not gate operands. Native action scopes now retain their full
ActionIntent graph. Explicit copying is confined to persistence, host rendering and
record/mapping boundaries, where structuredClone cannot clone live views.

Original issuer JWS role/typ verification and pinned-key/source checks remain. Integrity
also checks each embedded credential against its complete deterministic original projection;
only structural credential/value links are traversed, so a domain IRI shared with the
message's OperationRef does not accidentally become part of a credential's closure.
Selective-disclosure/chain mechanisms still receive their exact original envelope bytes.
The hostile empty-chain fixture is preserved as an unsigned presentation alongside real
signed parts and still fails its original representation mechanism. Prior credential
references resolve from an explicit bounded 4,096-entry digest cache, never the network;
missing/substituted bytes fail. Retained original message proofs rebuild the cache after
restart. Tests cover live graph mutation, a sender-signed tampered issuer projection,
resolved/missing prior digests, and approval survival across restart.

Message-release approvals use the selected suite's full-document digest, recomputed from
the held graph on resume. **Not complete:** earlier action approvals (before a message or
an effect exists) still bind the established action/terms/order intent; changing that
requires moving their construction boundary. The legacy signed/unsigned input adapter is
retained for hostile/compatibility fixtures; it has not become context-pinned RDF ingress.
Policy evaluation, several stored-fact/mandate readers and the other existing function
exceptions are also not claimed as a completed RDF migration. Native received message
bodies/evidence and emitted release operands are live graph views. The fifteen core
model declarations now alias the wrappers' live surfaces; construction/persistence input
contracts are explicitly in `src/model/inputs.ts`. The unmodified checker/allow-list report
**0 hard failures, 213 existing allowances**; no allowance is added.

**Part 4 — implemented for native document packets; legacy inspector entries remain.**
`PacketView` has `jsonld` (the exact transmitted signed string, including its context),
`context` (IRI or IRIs), `rdf` (the same graph via n3.Writer/SDK serialization: TriG for
named proof graphs, Turtle otherwise), and attachment digest/mediaType/byteLength without
bytes. Existing layers remain. Blank labels are normalised within each dataset so parser
counters do not change reproducible traces. The new trace JSON Schema distinguishes
complete document packets from historical hostile-input inspector entries; it refuses

**Part 5 — implemented and tested checkpoint.** Both mortgage scenarios declare

The matrix test compares every ordered decision/check/reason tuple with JWS for every

Attachment admission now checks caps before document verification as well as before

**Part 2's remaining approval boundary — stopped without changing the design.**
`continueStart`, `applyReply`, `performPlanMessage` and `runEffect` still authorize/park
their action before `emit` constructs a message (and local effects need not emit a
message at all). Their approvals retain the existing exact ActionIntent/terms/order
bindings. There is a concrete self-reference in the paid-acceptance path:
`performReply` embeds the principal's approval JWS in the acceptance's evidence, and
`verifyPaymentProofs` requires that original approval. A digest of the entire acceptance
therefore includes that approval's own `actionDigest` and signature. Replacing its action
binding by that same whole-message digest would require constructing a cryptographic
hash fixed point. The selected document digest excludes the outer message proof only;
silently excluding this embedded approval would change what the design says is signed.
Moving the approval outside the protected document, defining an approved projection, or
changing the approval/contract exchange would require a design decision. None is made.
Non-payment pre-action approvals likewise have no existing exact message to bind at their
current construction boundary. Consequently **Part 2 remains partial**, although native
emit/receive and message-release approvals now use the complete document. This is not
a passing claim for the stronger "every approval names the exact future message" rule.
The 213 existing function/signature exceptions still expose the remaining legacy policy,
stored-fact and protocol adapters; no new exception is introduced to conceal them.

## Brief 26e

<!-- AUTHORED-BY Codex GPT-6 -->

Work is offline and uncommitted on the requested branch, using Node 24. No new dependency is added. The unchanged decision oracle starts at SHA-256 `b4c928de53dc96228087009792da1be646f760e705d6343b48a8441c841d6f25`. Suites are handled in the requested order.

**Suite 1 — `meaning-plain-language-upgrade`: NOT RUN.** Registered in the existing matrix with its `prose-as-evidence` onlyUnder branch (fitness / structured-form-required). Design 26 §8 and SM-2 require an actual prose email opening followed by the lender’s description and a persistent refusal to downgrade. The existing ScriptedMailbox is unsigned and has no correspondent agent, key, gate or description advertisement. The signed runtime instead starts with structured discovery; its existing content-reading upgrade is not a prose-to-agent negotiation boundary. That boundary remains unimplemented. Reusing the mailbox or attaching prose to an already structured mortgage request would not demonstrate the requested journey. Confinement and the declared failure are not run; both manifest cells explicitly remain blocked. This is a missing runtime capability, not a claim that the design is impossible. No change to the other lane’s runtime is made for this part.

**Suite 2 — `meaning-registry-lookup`: RUNS.** The lender’s signed counteroffer asks for employment status in its own versioned code list. The payroll issuer signs a qualified status in its own list, alongside the unchanged income series. The inbound fitness gate reuses `mapCodeClaim` to verify the separately keyed publisher, exact directed table, scoped source acceptance and fresh signed status. A separate interpreted value names the signed payroll parent and digest, mapping/version, original code and source/quality; the original signed payroll statement remains intact. The accepted code is required before income is admitted. Main-path confinement passes, with no change outside meaning’s feeds or to the final journey outcome. `mapping-from-nobody` reaches fitness indeterminate / unsupported-term and asks for an accepted mapping. The real failure witness and mapped provenance tests pass; removing the fitness hook fails these assertions. This uses the existing matrix and onlyUnder selection.

The suite-specific missing-term request is a small `InboundGate.requiredFailure` seam: the earlier gate discarded an optional failed claim and could still admit its envelope. This suite requires the employment field for the mortgage exchange, so it requests clarification rather than allowing that omission. SM-3’s indeterminate verdict is retained. Source-authority failure of the mapping publisher is translated to unsupported-term for this suite only; the existing general mapping mechanism’s reasons remain unchanged.

**Suite 3 — `reliance-corroboration`: RUNS.** The existing payroll statement and independently bank-signed balance statement carry twelve same-subject monthly income observations. The lender checks equal currency, exact period and month set, a stated absolute tolerance, accepted bank authority for salary credits, ordinary bank freshness/method/terms, independently installed controllers, and disjoint source-record ancestry before admitting income. Rejected observations do not enter income memory. Confinement passes; `one-source-twice` reaches fitness ask-more / evidence-missing when the bank’s signed root-record ancestry names the payroll record. The configured policy is lender-only, so Sam’s existing source gathering and approval sequence remain the same. This extends the existing admission-batch mechanism rather than introducing another scenario runner. Root ancestry is a source assertion: a dishonest accepted source can conceal a dependency, which this implementation does not claim to detect.

**Suite 4 — `reliance-stated-confidence`: RUNS.** Source-signed bank metadata is read through a new live RDF view: the statement has `dqv:hasQualityMeasurement`, the measurement has `dqv:isMeasurementOf`, decimal `dqv:value`, `dqv:computedOn`, source attribution and a recorded method. The measurement states epistemic or aleatoric uncertainty and calibration. The fictional bank’s 99/100 reconciliation observation metadata supports its stated 0.99; the task minimum is 0.95. This is a signed source assertion, not an independent accuracy guarantee. Missing/unaccepted methods, mismatched observations, wrong metrics and uncalibrated estimates meet no threshold. Existing evidence carriage retains the exact signed measurement fields; the DQV graph is the typed gate-side metadata view, rather than a new wire serialization. Main confinement passes. `model-is-sure` carries a source-signed synthetic model-output statement with input/implementation digests and explicit uncalibrated 0.99; Sam’s fitness gate asks for calibrated evidence with confidence-too-low before admitting it. The failure branch ends at that hold. Tests verify source signatures, forwarding preservation, native DQV links, both calibration outcomes and adversarial threshold cases. No real model or calibration service is called.

**Suite 5 — `reliance-quality-certificate`: RUNS.** A separately keyed certifier signs the index identity, dataset, registered-residential-lender category, immutable accurate/current/complete policy and validity window. Both the index and Sam accept that certifier under explicit dataset-scoped source rules. Sam also requires authenticated carriage from that index; copying the dataset label onto another sender’s record is insufficient. The main path deliberately retains a product’s old publication date and completes without running per-record freshness on covered records. Confinement passes. In `certificate-does-not-cover`, the certificate supplied to Sam covers business lending. Normal fitness runs, rejects the stale residential record with stale-data and keeps it out of admitted memory. **The envelope outcome is admit**, retaining the other valid record; it does not become a whole-message refusal. This is the existing optional-claim behavior, explicitly tested and reported. Design §8 requires the stale record’s refusal, which occurs; no gate behavior is changed to force a different envelope outcome. The certificate’s independently signed scope and source acceptance are checked before bypassing quality. Certificate generation/audit is a declared fictional stand-in, not an actual certifier service.

**Suite 6 — `meaning-negotiated`: RUNS.** The lender’s signed evidence request names “irregular income”; the variable synthetic monthly series still totals the baseline annual gross income. Sam’s agent signs a proposed definition, the lender’s agent signs its acceptance, and the separately keyed fictional platform verifies and records both. The two fictional principals sign the exact proposal digest, term, exchange and expiry; neither actor key can sign as a principal. The gate verifies that the term in the actual received offer is the approved term and uses only the platform’s checked, exchange-scoped evidence and accepted source role. Main confinement passes. `agents-agree-between-themselves` records zero principal approvals and Sam’s outbound disclosure check gives ask-principal / approval-required before acceptance or disclosure. A generic action approval cannot replace the term evidence; the message boundary rechecks it. Tests reject another conversation, recipient, definition, proposal digest, expired signature and an agent signing as a person. The fixture host supplies fictional principal signatures, as the existing approval fixtures do; nobody clicks on a real platform.

**RDF and merge seams.** New suite checks read live typed RDF views of the existing open-domain named-value model, through `src/model/suite-values.ts`; confidence additionally has its native DQV statement/measurement view. Source parsing/conversion stays in model ingress, and existing signature, status, terms and code-mapping mechanisms are reused. The new model views do not introduce another serialization or runner. The only edits in the other lane’s owned files are: `src/gates/common.ts` adds optional suite dependencies; `src/gates/inbound.ts:claimChecks` calls the new meaning/reliance evaluations at fitness, `requiredFailure` retains the suite-required holds, and `Admission.need` carries the verified sender for certificate scope; `src/gates/outbound.ts:release` and `checkMessage` enforce term approval at disclosure. No `src/runtime/`, `src/core/`, `src/wire/`, `src/trace/` or existing `src/model/` file is edited. The domain facade’s strict evidence/request schemas admit only the new qualified fields. The RDF pipeline guard remains unchanged and is checked again at close.

**Resume after restart, 14 November 03:00 UTC.** Read the saved report, status/diff and required design/specifications before continuing. The first build caught an unfinished mapping-version change: `interpretEmployment` required a version-pin map that its gate caller did not yet supply. The gate now owns that per-agent-lifetime map; a regression verifies real publisher signatures, refusal of changed content at the same version, retention of the original pin, and acceptance of an explicitly selected new version. The initial full run additionally exposed old generated label totals. Final validation follows below.

**Resume audit fixes.** The certificate's admission-batch prepass still calculated ordinary product freshness even though final admission used the certificate. The new `InboundGate.qualityIsCertified` seam precedes that optional prepass as well as final fitness; a spy regression proves ordinary product fitness is never called for the covered journey, while the category-mismatch branch still fails freshness. Confidence decimal writes now reuse `model-runtime`'s datatype mapper; boundary and subnormal values produce valid decimal literals. A separate `vocab/meaning-reliance.ttl` defines the two local DQV annotations (`uncertaintyKind`, `calibration`) without changing the other lane's pinned main ontology, shapes or context. The ontology-extension test verifies their label, definition, domain and range.

SM-2 also needed exact vocabulary declarations in signed descriptions. `OperationDescription` now optionally declares its implemented versioned vocabulary IRIs; `buildDescription` collects those without altering any baseline declaration. The registry suite's new handler wrapper publishes its accepted list and directed source list under that seam, and the main-path test checks the lender's signed description. The engine uses the same wrapper for configured mapping receivers. No runtime/core/wire/trace or existing model file is edited. These audit fixes are in addition to the original gate seams listed above.

**Stand-ins.** Every new trace inherits the mortgage profile's fictional separately keyed lender, bank, payroll provider, product index, register and identity provider, plus its fictional payment provider and ledger (no money moves). Its own `standIns` additionally declares:

1. Registry lookup: an issuer-only employment vocabulary publisher and fresh signed status service, served by the existing in-process resolver. Both versioned code lists and their governance are fictional.
2. Corroboration: the bank's independently signed twelve-month salary-credit observations. These synthetic credits equal gross payroll; no gross-to-net conversion is claimed. Root-record ancestry is a signed source assertion; independent controllers are installed local trust facts.
3. Confidence: the bank's synthetic 99-of-100 historical ledger reconciliations and its stated calibration method. The failure's payroll-issued model estimate is synthetic and explicitly uncalibrated; no model or accuracy audit is called. The native DQV view interprets preserved signed fields.
4. Quality certificate: an issuer-only certifier with its own key and explicit dataset-scoped source authority, signing a synthetic accurate/current/complete policy. There is no actual audit or certification service.
5. Negotiated meaning: an independently keyed deliberation platform, signed proposal and agent acceptance, and fixture-host signatures from both fictional principals in place of real people's clicks. The stable term name includes the SHA-256 of the definition text. No real platform is contacted.
6. Plain-language upgrade: its blocked world declares the missing prose upgrade boundary; it publishes no trace and claims no running mailbox negotiation.

**Specification and design findings, by clause.** Design 26 §8's five structured alternatives run as recorded above. The quality-certificate failure refuses the stale **record**, while retaining the valid record and admitting that envelope; the per-record requirement is met without turning it into a whole-message refusal. Design §8 / SM-2's plain-language opening and persistent upgrade remain unimplemented, as explained under suite 1; UNS-5's declared failure cannot run honestly without that boundary. Design §1 also requires the source specification's suite status to read “Defined”. Specifications 13 and 16b still call these alternatives “Sketch”; those supplied files are outside the writable working copy. They have not been edited and that documentation condition is not claimed complete. No additional reason code is introduced. Existing baseline precedence, self-report handling and onward evidence preservation are retained (RL-4/5/6); the new suites do not establish the truth of an accepted source's assertions.

**Not from a specification.**

1. The employment list names, publisher, version strings, exact PAYE-to-employed entry, current-status freshness window and accepted-code policy are local fixture choices. The existing `mapCodeClaim` supplies signature, directed scope, status, loss and provenance checks. The suite additionally pins a mapping digest per table/version for one agent lifetime (SM-1); the pin is not a durable registry or an external publication archive.
2. Corroboration uses an absolute tolerance of one USD unit per month across the same twelve-month set. The existing admission batch, source/controller trust, ordinary quality and terms machinery is reused. A new comparison is needed because the two observations have different enclosing claim types (payroll and balance), unlike the older exact-value corroboration scenario. Missing/mismatched observations give the design's `evidence-missing`, without changing that older mechanism's reasons.
3. Confidence has the task minimum 0.95, a locally named versioned metric and method allowlist, epistemic uncertainty on the bank sample, and source-signed calibration metadata. A calibrated result additionally requires finite observation counts with the stated score equal to correct/total. This simple method is a fixture choice, not a general statistical calibration algorithm or an accuracy guarantee. The failure adds the income-estimate task threshold to demonstrate the model rule; the bank's threshold is unchanged. Evidence retains its original signed fields and the native DQV graph is a gate-side view, not a second evidence wire format.
4. The certificate uses a local signed `QualityCertificate` schema, one immutable accurate/current/complete policy identifier, residential-versus-business scope, an explicit lifetime and configured resolver references. It replaces per-record quality only on authenticated index carriage (or that index's own dataset admission); source signatures and terms still run. No revocation protocol, audit process or native DQV certificate wire encoding is invented.
5. The proposed definition sums the twelve variable monthly gross payroll amounts without changing the annual total. Its name is derived from the exact definition text. Both principals approve the proposal digest, exchange, term and expiry; ordinary action consent is separate. The platform's checked in-memory record is the gate's approval source, accepted for that exact term. The signed platform receipt is retained in the trace, but there is no independently fetched platform service, public comments workflow, global vocabulary publication or durable proposal recovery.
6. The host wraps the existing lender counteroffer handler to expose each suite's required field. Policy settings live in each configuration's existing `world` patch and are injected only under its selected layer/receiver; no alternative scenario runner or permissive replacement profile is added. All checks still run between the existing separately keyed agents. The RDF migration and unsupported evidence envelopes remain the other lane's work.
7. `test/labels.test.ts` changes only its generated alternative totals from 19/14/5 (all/run/blocked) to 31/24/7. Its 150/11/9 baseline totals, every caption/rule identifier assertion and the exact-content review test remain unchanged. New tests also prove the outbound message boundary cannot replace a principal's term approval with the generic `approved` flag.
8. The optional operation-level vocabulary declarations use the local relations `implements-versioned-code-list` and `maps-from-versioned-code-list`; their IRIs contain the exact immutable list version. These descriptive relations grant no authority and do not replace signed-table checks. The two local confidence annotation definitions are a small published extension of the same namespace, not a replacement ontology or a new wire context.

**Selection and carriage.** `selectedSettlement` in `src/settlement/configurations.ts`
selects the host-installed suite by the configured identifier and refuses an unavailable
choice. The engine installs one retained protocol adapter per run and injects it into
the runtime/gates; the RDF suite remains the default. Invoice production, gated order
production, provider dispatch, ingress and trace production all use that same choice.
The four wire definitions are executable, but a runtime advertises a settlement wire
configuration only when its matching adapter is installed and accepted. This preserves

| Configuration | Actual native exchange | Settlement and receipt source |
|---|---|---|
| `settlement-x402` | PaymentRequired from valuer to payer; PaymentPayload to valuer; individual `/verify` and `/settle` requests/responses between valuer and facilitator; SettlementResponse to payer. | Real EIP-712 verification, fictional ledger transfer and the facilitator's separate identity-signed receipt statement. |
| `settlement-mpp` | HMAC-bound challenge to payer, EIP-3009 credential to valuer, Payment-Receipt response to payer. | The retained MPP server verifies and settles; the separate provider statement authenticates the otherwise unsigned receipt. |
| `settlement-ap2` | Merchant checkout JWT; Checkout Mandate to merchant and Payment Mandate to credential provider; processor Payment Receipt to merchant and then forwarded to payer; merchant Checkout Receipt to payer. | Distinct fictional credential provider and merchant processor. Processor `urn:fictional:merchant-processor` is the expected receipt source in both profile and runtime order records. Both ES256 receipts use the previously documented local claim profile. |
| `settlement-acp` | Individual signed checkout creation/session, delegation/201 token and completion/session exchanges, routed to merchant or provider as specified. | Endpoint-scoped idempotency stores and one-use delegated token on the fictional ledger; provider statement binds the receipt/order scope. |

Each carried protocol component retains its exact native string or HTTP method, path,
headers and body. A transcript is inspector output, never a send unit. Ordinary invoice,
order and receipt messages carry one native component; the retained adapter resolves only
an exact previously verified component to its companion objects and facts. A core-supplied
`native` field confers no authority. Normal order/invoice/receipt ingress lifts only after
ordinary enclosing-message admission; changed objects stop with `evidence-invalid`.
The provider independently verifies the held original offer/acceptance and acceptance
mandate/approval before its ledger effect. Native auxiliary exchanges use the dedicated
serialized host ingress: complete signed RDF messages, all five ordinary inbound checks,
configuration/audience/time/replay checks, no core dispatch and no ledger effect.

**Source admission.** The existing identity-signed `awp-settlement-key+jws` publishes
`type/party/suite/algorithm/publicKey/validFrom/validUntil`. x402/MPP publish Sam's
secp256k1 address; AP2 publishes separate person, merchant and processor ES256 public
JWKs. Each publication is carried with a party-signed `settlement-key` source statement;
other native objects use `settlement-object`. Those ordinary source statements bind
`objectDigest` to the exact enclosing message content and pass the configured inbound
source check. The adapter then verifies the original key publication with the pinned
party identity and matches party, suite, algorithm, public key and validity before use.
A valid signature without the source statement/authority is `source-not-authoritative`.
ACP directly uses the parties' pinned Ed25519 identity keys. EVM accounts are admitted
through the provider's signed payee-account bindings, using the corresponding did:pkh
account IRI. No core assertion supplies account ownership or expected receipt authority.

the marked payment segment and the final journey outcome. Their ordered shared P1–P8
and receipt verdicts are also identical to baseline. Additional ordinary admissions of
native exchanges occur inside the paid segment. Each main path performs one fictional
ledger transfer and passes both receipt checks. The manifest's main-path `actual` summary
still names the mortgage's earlier `authority/assurance-insufficient` hold, as the baseline
does; the complete trace continues through approval and successful payment.

Each refusal stops before a provider order or ledger transfer. The amount branches ask

**Approval release.** Before principal/role review the runtime saves the exact intent
digest and the continuation's intended message fields and terms; absent selections and

prepared `MessageNode` and `ActionIntentNode`, checks the approved intent/terms, validates

share only the exact approved intent, terms and evidence selection. Resumed continuations
cannot remove or substitute their intent. Local effects retain their existing exact
intent/parameter authorization; they need not emit an instruction message.

At release the **existing `message-out` ledger entry** gains `approvalBinding`, containing
approval IDs, the intent/terms digests, message ID and selected-suite whole-message digest.
The check occurs before signing; the binding is persisted before exposing the outbox. No

release path remains intact. Tests alter both a real paid mortgage acceptance's carried

actual outgoing packet. `approval-mismatch` is added to the vocabulary/context/shapes;

any generator rule.

## Brief 29

<!-- AUTHORED-BY Codex GPT-6 -->

Performance implementation in progress, offline, no commits. Baseline working tree was
clean on `claude/project-thread-ee3kq8-speed`. Node 24.22.3 is selected with
`PATH=/opt/node24/bin:$PATH`. Original trace, outline and manifest artifacts were saved
under `/tmp/brief29-original-*` before any source optimization. The immutable oracle and
RDF allowance list are not edited.

Added `scripts/profile-journey.mjs`: `--iterations=N` runs all mortgage-main paths under
baseline and authenticity-data-integrity, printing wall milliseconds for each configuration.
`--main-only` isolates the main journey; `--cpu-prof` writes V8 CPU profiles (default
`/tmp/agent-reference-profile`, override `--cpu-prof-dir=...`). `--output=...` retains
comparison snapshots and `--compare=...` asserts identical ordered decisions and exact
packet `jsonld`, `rdf` and attachment metadata. Compile with `pnpm exec tsc -p
tsconfig.build.json`; the script copies the committed generated JavaScript runtime into
dist before importing the compiled engine. No trace build is needed to profile.

Initial inspection: pinned contexts already load and hash once per process, returning
independent clones. Retain that implementation. SHACL shapes are parsed once, but every
root admission reconstructs the validator and compiled constraint graph. Emit also reads
its newly signed document and attachments again to construct the trace. Measurements
and completed validation follow below as each change is checked.

**Baseline profile (all four mortgage-main paths, one iteration, CPU profiling enabled).**
JWS **58.798 s**, DI **63.139 s**. V8 sampled 122.932 s including imports. Inclusive
costs overlap: named-values `entryFor` 23.465 s (19.1%), wrapper `one` 16.267 s
(13.2%), SHACL construction 15.014 s (12.2%), `copyData` 13.698 s (11.1%), JSON-LD
active-context cloning 12.498 s (10.2%). GC self time 24.186 s (19.7%); N3 index
find/count self time 24.295 s (19.8%). Profile: `/tmp/brief29-before/CPU.20261114.072623.22.0.001.cpuprofile`.

**Change 1 — compiled SHACL reuse.** A bounded two-entry idle pool keeps compiled
shapes. Every root still validates against all previously required shapes with maxErrors=8.
Each checkout is exclusive; return clones the validation engine (fresh reports, counters,
errors and recursion state, same limits) and drops the data pointer. This is necessary
because this library's validateNode does not reset those fields. After: JWS **39.900 s**
(**18.898 s / 32.1% saved**); DI **43.727 s** (**19.412 s / 30.7% saved**).
`--compare=/tmp/brief29-before.json`: **172 exact packet occurrences per suite**,
including signed JSON-LD, RDF and attachment metadata; all ordered decisions identical.
Focused tests: **20/20 in 5.25 s**, including interleaved valid/invalid admissions and
60 consecutive validations before a refusal (past the validator's 50-check counter).

**Changes 2–4 — live reads and byte-derived conversion.** Direct subject/predicate
reads avoid allocating wrapper Sets merely to read terms; named-value copying uses the
existing explicit model projection at the host/store boundary. Alone these measured
41.947 s / 42.988 s (a small/noisy change versus Change 1; focused tests overlapped
that measurement). They enable the cross-graph indexes below; no standalone saving is
claimed. A 16-entry conversion cache then retains the normalized dataset from the
writer's checked JSON-LD round trip. Keys include SHA-256 of the exact JSON bytes plus
all limits; admission checks still run and every returned graph is independent. After
conversion reuse: **37.446 s JWS / 34.610 s DI**; 344 packet occurrences and all decisions
again match the baseline. This reuses the graph produced when writing without bypassing
the receiver's key/signature checks or its attachment and shape admission.

Cross-graph read indexes now supplement N3's per-graph indexes for singleton/collection
reads and named-value lookup. They contain lexical RDF term IDs, preserve graph-order
and duplicate-quad semantics, and return fresh terms. Native Store remains authoritative;
all add/remove routes invalidate the indexes, including same-size graph edits. A graph
still being constructed uses native reads until eight reads occur without a write.
After indexing, with CPU profiling enabled: **26.449 s JWS / 26.081 s DI**, a further
**10.997 s / 8.529 s** versus conversion reuse and **55.0% / 58.7%** versus baseline
(**2.22× / 2.42×**). All **344 packet occurrences** are byte-identical and decisions
unchanged. The 37 focused tests pass in 6.65 s, covering native/indexed read equality,
named-graph duplicates, equal-size replacement, bulk writes/deletes, caller term mutation,
attachment attacks and live graph mutation. RDF guard remains **0 failures / 213 unchanged
allowances**.

After-profile: `/tmp/brief29-index/CPU.20261114.073551.15.0.001.cpuprofile` (53.478 s
sampled including imports/comparison). GC self time **5.551 s** (from 24.186), N3 find/count
self time **6.393 s** (from 24.295), named-value entryFor inclusive **6.521 s** (from
23.465), copyData **3.608 s** (from 13.698), JSON-LD context cloning **7.884 s** (from
12.498). Remaining costs are context processing, live policy/model reads and persistence.
RDFC/signature routines were not dominant, so their directional-literal and blank-node
preflights remain intact; no cryptographic algorithm was reimplemented.

**Routine corpus policy.** Default selection is deterministic: retain every path that
contains an original capacity message and every forum-operation path (the six large
intent occurrences), then sort with main paths first and scenario/configuration/path
lexically and select paths that add a recorded message kind, executable trace
configuration, or scenario-specific declared alternative. Add companion branches needed
to witness every selected world's required stress checks; the engine's existing stress
assertion stays enabled. The live capture must reproduce the complete recorded coverage
set (47 executable configurations, 12 scenario/alternative pairs and ten observed kinds).
The corpus also checks a model-only indeterminate message, covering all eleven kinds.
All 26 original capacity-occurrence assertions remain. `FULL_CORPUS=1 pnpm exec vitest
run test/complete-model.test.ts` retains the exhaustive capture and its size assertions.

Additional regression checks pass **4/4 in 3.20 s**, including streamed import/removal,
removeMatches/deleteGraph, and same-size mutation of an admitted attachment after its
index is warm. Package typecheck and lint pass (zero lint errors; existing warnings and
informational diagnostics remain). The global test/hook timeout is restored from 120
to 60 seconds; the mortgage JWS/DI matrix timeout drops from 600 to 180 seconds. The
corpus timeout drops from 1,200 seconds to 600 (sample) / 900 (full). Final timed runs
with these settings follow. No production deadline or limit is edited.

The first path-sampled corpus passed in **481.13 s** (477.87 s tests), while the full
trace build ran on another core. The final default additionally keeps the first
SHA-256-digest-ordered message per (wire configuration, message kind) and intent per
(operation, action), retaining **every** original capacity message and every intent for
the capacity operations; all captured agreements remain. This removes repeated
round-trips of the same model structure. FULL_CORPUS bypasses both sampling stages.
The final default/full runs below supersede this preliminary sample timing.

**Full build — PASS, 8m 12.650s wall / 8m 16.618s user CPU.** This checkout now
generates **192 live paths**, with 181 runs / 11 disagree / 14 blocked in the manifest.
The earlier approximately 15-minute build is the brief's reported baseline, not an
independently timed full pre-change build. Log: `/tmp/brief29-build.log`.

The first artifact audit found **181 completely identical event traces**. Besides

Offline implementation on `claude/project-thread-ee3kq8-identity`, without commits. Node 24 is selected explicitly. Suites are completed in priority order; the immutable 161-path oracle and the 213 RDF allowances are preserved. Later results below supersede checkpoints.

**Suite 1 checkpoint — `authority-mandate-credential`.** Sam's original principal-signed operational mandate now includes two Bitstring Status List entries and the actor key thumbprint. Its signed representation carries that same complete mandate, materialised as an embedded credential with standard status links. Both gates verify the principal-signed lists using `@jeswr/solid-vc`; the outbound action and final message-release boundaries stop on suspension, revocation or unconfirmed status. Each host turn refreshes the verified outbound status snapshot, which expires after one minute. The receiver resolves status afresh before accepting authority. The face-check step-up remains. The initial two focused tests pass, including main-path confinement and the suspension branch. The definitive test additionally requires the lender's separate refusal after Sam's own stop. The branch uses an authentic application already queued before suspension, never a forged successful send after the local stop.

SDK assessment: `agent-mandate`'s verifier fixes a single revocation URL ending `/agent/mandates/status.ttl` and its evaluator treats unmatched rules as confirmation; the existing engine requires its established deny-by-default decisions and this suite needs both suspension and revocation. The existing credential/model and ODRL engine are therefore retained; signing, compression, bit updates and list verification reuse `solid-vc`. No dependency is added.

**Not from a specification — choices so far.** List slot 42, a one-day list lifetime, two separate purpose-specific list URLs, the host's per-turn outbound refresh and one-minute maximum snapshot age are local choices. The fictional status host serves only the configured current lists in process. The mandate/representation binding property is local vocabulary, labelled and defined. Selective disclosure is conditional on the separate authenticity suite and is not claimed in this single-layer configuration. No live status server, network or hardware-backed principal custody is claimed.

**Suite 1 definitive focused result:** 2/2 tests pass, including both ordered stops and no later message from Sam. Confinement passes. Missing status purposes refuse representation; revocation cannot be cleared. The RDF guard remains 0 failures / 213 allowances. `solid-vc` supplies the standard status vocabulary and contexts; the credential shape's old singleton status limit is corrected to permit both purposes, and codegen is regenerated (219 full-model admission errors, no generator admission rule relaxed).

**Suite 2 — `identity-did`: RUNS.** Two focused tests pass. All existing party, principal, actor and operator names in the selected world are explicit did:web aliases, propagated exactly into the same profile constraints and source statements. The fictional resolver serves the method's HTTPS document paths in process; SDK Multikey decoding supplies the actual verification key, checked against an independently signed actor/principal key commitment with a sixty-day lifetime. Only method `web` is accepted. Confinement passes, including the complete payment ending and unchanged face-check step-up. `did-method-not-listed` carries a real agent-key-signed document whose actor uses did:key; integrity refuses with `unsupported-configuration` before sender attribution. Tests also refuse host-key substitution and expired commitments, check root/path DID resolution, and scan every new human sentence for raw identifiers.

Two existing reference handlers hard-coded the original index/bank recipient names, and the bank handler hard-coded the original account-holder name. Their recipients now come from the bound conversation, and the bank's account-holder expectation comes from its provisioned signed statement; defaults preserve existing fixture behavior. These are identity parameterisation fixes, not relaxed authorization. Baseline verification follows in closing checks.

**Not from a specification — DID choices.** This is a closed did:web subset: fictional `.example` DNS hosts, root and simple unescaped path identifiers, one Ed25519 Multikey/assertion method per document. Encoded ports/paths, redirects, method updates, automated recovery and historical document archives are not implemented. Current commitments expire closed; no past-signature resolution is claimed (ID-4/5). Names remain globally linkable (ID-6); operators retain distinct names. The resolver and commitment register are separate fictional issuer-only parties with distinct keys. DID documents use the DID and Multikey context identifiers without fetching contexts. No live DNS, HTTPS, ledger method or did:key resolution is claimed. The source specifications remain outside this writable working copy; their status text is not edited.

**Suite 3 — `identity-email-endpoint`: NOT RUN.** The suite already exists in the catalog; the mortgage cell and `mailbox-reply-as-evidence` onlyUnder branch are now explicitly registered unresolved. Its regression test passes and requires both paths to have no trace. Design 26 §6 requires a new principal-approved exact-text outbound action, mailbox-control challenge, prose reply and structured follow-up. Specification 12 lists integrity, recipient and authority as identity feeds, while those extra required outbound actions also add mandate, disclosure, data-terms, limits/approval and evidence verdicts. The ordered confinement rule in design §2 cannot preserve the unfed sequence. This is a source-level conflict, not a claimed measured passing/differing confinement run. Separately, the current `ScriptedMailbox` has no challenge-control proof: a principal-signed address pin establishes a prior preference, not control of the payroll mailbox. That missing mechanism is not replaced by a source-authoritative prose figure. Confinement and the prescribed failure outcome are NOT RUN, with the exact blocker in the configuration/manifest.

**Suite 4 — `identity-anonymous`: NOT RUN.** The registry already contains anonymous public reads. A new mortgage cell and its `anonymous-asks-for-more` onlyUnder branch are explicitly unresolved, with a passing no-trace regression. The product search already omits both the requester identifier and signature, and its service reply is signed. However, design §6's statement that the lender-register step is already an equivalent public exchange is false of this checkout: `TrustResolver` resolves source/recipient questions synchronously over local unsigned root/register tables, with no register request/answer message. Reusing that table or labelling the product query alone would leave half the required behavior absent. Building and admitting a separately keyed signed register exchange into that resolver is unfinished; this is an implementation limit, not evidence that anonymous discovery is inherently unbuildable. Confinement and the suite-specific refusal are NOT RUN, rather than attributed to an exchange that never happened.

**Suite 5 checkpoint — `authority-authorization-server`.** The real mortgage main path and the actor-missing branch pass their focused test. Both gates verify issuer acceptance through the existing trust lookup and bind principal, actor, key, audience and validity. The outbound gate's actual loaded policies are lifted from the verified native grant, with the original grant digest and both resulting ODRL policy sets shown in a trace record. The receiver requires the requested operation to appear in those grant policies. Main-path confinement passes; `token-as-the-person` uses a server-signed grant missing the actor and is refused at authority with `representation-invalid`. The existing principal-signed mandate remains supplementary consent evidence for the unchanged paid-contract verifier; the agent's authorization decisions read the lifted server grant, not that supplementary mandate. This is disclosed explicitly because the payment verifier requires that principal proof and is outside this lane. Existing exact-action approval and face-check gates remain.

## Brief 26d
