Offline implementation, no commits. The existing configuration matrix and configuration-only branches are reused. The immutable 164-path oracle and the RDF guard's 213 allowances are preserved. Suites proceed in priority order; the paragraphs below record completed checkpoints.

Data Integrity already had its executable configuration and mortgage runs. Added `graph-reordered`: the lender's signed description is delivered with reordered JSON members and included graph entries, then Sam's protected application timestamp is changed by one millisecond without re-signing. Both travel through the ordinary separately keyed sender/receiver runtime. Focused validation follows.

**Suite 1 — Data Integrity: RUNS.** The new graph-reordering/changed-triple branch passes its real-receiver regression. Existing JWS/DI main-path confinement is retained. Statements embedded as original issuer JWSs remain original JWSs, as documented in Brief 22d; design §7’s literal “every statement” carrying DI is therefore not claimed. This branch adds no cryptography or allowance.

**Suite 2 — pinned list: RUNS.** Sam’s own recipient and source choices use independently reviewed keys, exact scopes and expiry, with register fallback disabled in the resolver for this configuration. Main-path confinement passes. The index independently accepts and returns a separately keyed fictional stranger; Sam’s source check refuses that record with `source-unknown` and builds a readable principal question before retaining any claim from it. The existing optional-record behavior admits the envelope containing the two valid products; this is not a whole-envelope refusal. Two tests pass, covering the actual index exchange, confinement, unknown/expired/out-of-scope/blocked entries and absence of register lookup paths. Removing the own-list-only dispatch or source question fails the tests.

**Suite 3 — selective disclosure: NOT RUN.** Registered unresolved in the existing matrix, with both required configuration-only failures. Design 26 §7 / AY-5 needs an annual-income/employer projection, while the mortgage handler requires the complete monthly-series statement, verifies compact issuer statements through `HandlerContext.verify`, and recomputes annual income from twelve admitted monthly inputs. Its strict evidence parser and annual-only admission/source-assured annual policy do not support that projection. The existing salted-disclosure mechanism is reused by older scenarios and remains tested, but attaching it to this mortgage would either disclose the withheld inputs or fail assessment. That missing integration is stopped and reported; it is not a claim that selective disclosure is impossible. Confinement and both failure outcomes remain not run, with an explicit manifest blocker. The supplied specification also still calls this suite Sketch; it is outside the writable working copy.

**Suite 4 — origin hosted: NOT RUN.** Registered unresolved, with the address-substitution and forwarding branches. Design 26 §7 and specification 15’s three origin-hosted bullets require an independently signed source-address register answer, a verifier-initiated unsigned-product fetch, and a fact type whose origin cannot be asserted to a third party by forwarding it. The current index and product evidence path require issuer-signed statements; neither unsigned retrieval admission nor that non-transferable fact boundary exists. The WebID resolver establishes keys, not product origin, so it is not relabelled as this suite. Those missing boundaries remain explicit blockers; confinement and the two actual failure paths are not run. Three selective mechanism/matrix regression tests passed before starting this suite; origin’s blocked-cell regression is run next.

**Suite 5 — trusted index: RUNS.** The index appends the exact source-signed record bytes, signs a prior and current head, and carries a separately signed receipt per returned record. RFC 9162 domain-separated SHA-256 leaf/node hashing, inclusion and prefix-consistency paths are verified independently of source acceptance and source signatures. Signed coverage identifies the sources, register and source-submission ordering; ordering is never advice. Main-path confinement passes. `index-shows-two-histories` gives valid signatures and inclusion paths for a fork that fails consistency against the independently held checkpoint: Sam refuses both records at source / `evidence-invalid`, with neither entering memory. Both complete signed heads and proofs remain in the actual received packet and the verification ledger. The ordinary optional-claim behavior admits the envelope; no acceptance of either record is implied. Three focused tests pass, including every leaf and prefix for tree sizes one through thirty-two, forged/missing proofs, changed source bytes, signed-head lifetime, coverage, unaccepted service and fork refusals.

The log is in process and JSON encoded, explicitly a stand-in for SCITT COSE, with no public service or gossip. Source statements keep their existing freshness/fitness checks. No cryptography package or dependency was added; SDK inspection found no existing Merkle log. Source-address retrieval remains explicitly blocked as recorded above; its blocked-cell test passes.

**Suite 6 — federation: RUNS.** The fictional authority, superior and lender independently sign OpenID Federation entity-statement JWTs (`iss`, `sub`, `iat`, `exp`, `jwks`, `authority_hints`, `metadata`, `metadata_policy`). The lender supplies its chain at product publication and with its own signed messages. Sam independently holds the anchor key. Verification authenticates each hop before using its keys, rejects expired/missing/reordered/substituted statements, binds the final self-configuration to the superior’s authorized keys, and intersects the supported `subset_of` policies for purpose and claim types. Sam’s lender source and recipient lookups use this presented proof and never fall back to its register. Main confinement passes. The expired intermediate gives source / `lookup-failed`; the business-only policy gives source / `source-not-authoritative`. Both records stay out of memory, while the envelope can retain the other valid product. Two focused tests pass, including real sender-presented chains and adversarial chain/key/policy cases.

The existing `@jeswr/federation-trust` package was inspected: it issues W3C Solid federation membership VCs, not OpenID entity-statement JWTs, so it is not relabelled as this protocol. Existing `jose` is reused. The custom `answerable` metadata type, purpose/claim lists, fixed maximum eight presented statements, one-day fixture lifetime and thumbprint JWT key identifiers are local profile choices. Only the declared narrowing operators are accepted; unsupported policy syntax fails closed. The supplied specification still says Sketch and is not writable here. No claim of full OpenID Federation interoperability or discovery support is made.

**Suite 7 — reputation: RUNS.** The accepted, separately keyed fictional service signs an evidence-referenced agent-behaviour score bound to the lender’s actor and independently introduced key. Sam’s rule requires at least 0.8, with a current service signature and exact source scope. The ordinary register/own-list result is evaluated first and can only be tightened by reputation. Main confinement passes. The actual index exchange returns a second separately keyed lender whose signed score is 0.99; Sam still refuses its product at source / `source-unknown` because no register entry establishes that lender. The rejected product never enters Sam’s memory; the envelope retains its valid products. Two focused tests pass, including scores below threshold, missing/forged/unaccepted/expired/future scores, wrong actors/keys, and the high-score/unregistered case. The service’s score and evidence review are declared synthetic stand-ins; the implementation does not invent a standardized rating algorithm, audit or governance service.

**Selective-disclosure follow-through — supersedes suite 3’s stopped checkpoint.** The annual-only admission is now implemented. The existing salted-disclosure mechanism signs the payroll/employer projection and separate hidden national-insurance/monthly claims. The gate verifies the disclosed commitment and reads the annual record through the model; the mortgage context verifier validates its schema after the disclosure proof. The lender explicitly uses source-assured annual income, keeping the annualization rule unchanged for baseline runs. The selected configuration supplies its annual vocabulary/purpose scopes without changing the baseline profile. Main confinement passes. The withheld-number branch carries an optional lender request, refuses an actual extra-disclosure proposal at disclosure / `excessive-scope`, then continues the permitted projection to the final receipt. The changed-disclosure branch has a hostile holder change its salt and sign its own outer message; the real receiver refuses at integrity / `bad-signature`. Tests for both branches pass. The path classifier records the first rejected extra-disclosure action as `stop` while its declared continuation is `go`, so that branch is honestly marked **disagrees** despite reaching the final receipt; the classifier is unchanged.

The wire envelope inspector also needed a real fix: a dotted claim name such as annual gross income matched its overly broad compact-JWS heuristic. Selective envelopes now inspect only their defined issuer-credential field, leaving claim values as data. The projection remains linkable, uses the existing reproducible fixture salts, and claims neither standard SD-JWT/BBS interoperability nor proofs about hidden values. No new vocabulary IRI or allowance is introduced. The earlier unresolved-cell test is replaced by the actual journey/refusal tests.

**Origin-hosted follow-through — supersedes suite 4’s stopped checkpoint.** The signed source-address answer, bounded guarded HTTP fetch and local non-transferable observation are now implemented. The lender’s product is never signed; the index carries only a locator and the independently signed register answer. Both the index and Sam fetch the source themselves. The index’s cached observation is used only to check its pointer/metadata release against terms; it is not a transferable source proof. The look-alike address in the index reply is ignored, recorded and never used as a fetch operand. The main mortgage and look-alike branch pass confinement. The forwarding branch stops earlier at **mandate / not-authorised**, since Sam’s unchanged mandate permits disclosure to the lender and not the valuer. Its design expectation remains evidence-invalid in `catalogueExpected`, and the manifest reports **disagrees**; no permission is widened to force the desired check. An independent gate regression gives an exact action permit and confirms that unsigned origin observations still fail the outbound evidence check. Unknown/forged/expired/misbound register answers, unsafe addresses, redirects and changed source identity are rejected. The source fetch is an explicitly declared in-process HTTPS stand-in, with SDK guarding and fixed fictional public DNS, not actual network/TLS authentication.

All seven suites now have executable configurations and their declared configuration-only branches. The two original blocked checkpoints above document the implementation sequence; they are not final not-run statuses. The specification’s Sketch labels and literal requirement that every embedded statement use DI remain source/documentation differences as identified above.

**Not from a specification.**

1. Own-list configuration entries use the fixture’s independently reviewed keys and a common expiry at the start of January 2027. Every added source rule names exact claim classes/subjects; there is no all-source or all-purpose acceptance. The unknown-record question is gate built and does not itself add a pin. Existing signed owner trust-decision machinery is retained, rather than a new approval mechanism.
2. The selective payroll presentation reveals one signed claim containing annual gross income, employer name, currency and the required period/issuance metadata. Separate commitments hide the monthly series and insurance number. The annual projection is source assured; it is not a threshold proof or a computation over hidden inputs. Deterministic fixture salts preserve reproducibility and do not claim deployment-grade hiding against someone who knows the fixture. The optional private-number request and its rejected extra-disclosure proposal are local scenario choices.
3. Source-address register answers use a local Ed25519 JWS type, one record/address/source binding and a one-day lifetime. Source products are size-bounded JSON admitted into the existing named-value RDF model. The SDK-guarded in-process HTTPS fetch uses fixed public fictional DNS, no redirects and a 64-KiB cap. Only the signed register address is a fetch operand. The observation is local, current and non-transferable; no historical HTTPS proof, TLS deployment or origin-key rotation service is claimed.
4. Index leaf bytes are the exact UTF-8 source JWS, with SHA-256 prefixes zero for leaves and one for internal nodes. Heads and receipts are local Ed25519 JWSs over JSON. The policy independently seeds the agent with an earlier signed checkpoint at setup; the index then presents the later head and proofs. Coverage lists exact sources and one register; source-submission ordering is not advice. A head may be at most one day old. The fixture log is bounded at 4,096 entries, proofs at 64 hashes; no public gossip, global completeness or production SCITT service is claimed.
5. The OpenID Federation subset uses a custom `answerable` metadata type for exact purpose and claim-type lists and only `subset_of` narrowing operators. Unknown metadata/policy structure fails closed. Entity identifiers and the principal’s anchor are fixture supplied; statement signing uses Ed25519 and thumbprint key identifiers. Presented chains are bounded to eight statements. Cached acceptance expires at the earliest statement expiry. Discovery, enrollment, authority governance, complaints and key-rotation procedures are not standardized by this fixture and are not implemented as online services.
6. Reputation is on a zero-to-one scale, with Sam’s minimum 0.8, a source-signed 0.93 for the accepted lender and 0.99 for the unregistered stranger. The accepted service, method name, actor/key binding, evidence-review reference and one-day lifetime are local choices. The evidence review and score are synthetic; the trust suite verifies the service’s statement, not the truth of its review or an objective rating algorithm. A score only tightens ordinary source acceptance.
7. Optional source records are refused individually while their signed envelope can still be admitted. Configuration-only branch classification retains the existing first-diverging-decision rule. It therefore reports the selective extra-disclosure stop separately from the later permitted journey, and the origin branch preserves the earlier mandate refusal instead of broadening permission to reach the design’s proposed evidence failure.
8. No new RDF vocabulary term was minted: the additional protocol/domain values use the existing named-value model. The original RDF contexts, ontology, generated model, oracle and allowance list are unchanged. The scenario schema is regenerated from its source. All new parties, services, synthetic scores, source observations, checkpoint seeding and encodings are declared in the relevant trace’s stand-ins. No new dependency, installation or network request is used.

**Validation checkpoint.** Forty targeted tests in seventeen files pass, including all seven suite journeys, the new refusals, the original selective-disclosure tests, all sixteen proof-graph tests, mortgage regressions, both existing JWS/DI matrices and the unchanged **164-path live oracle**. The separate full seven-configuration run additionally executes every inherited payment branch; its annual-assessment references use the existing keyed world patch. Source compilation and package lint pass. RDF guard remains **zero hard failures / 213 allowances**. Final artifact counts and closing commands follow after trace generation and broader confinement/model checks finish.

**Additional clause limits.** AY-5’s projection proof is the verified selective envelope and issuer-credential digest. This local mechanism does not encode a separate complete DER-7 derivation record, and it is not advertised as a standard SD-JWT VC or BBS implementation. TF-2’s unknown-source question does not itself grant authority: this scenario ends at refusal/question and does not implement interactive acceptance and replay of a relayed source record. Existing sender-pin approvals remain separate. Design §1’s source status requirement cannot be completed in the read-only supplied specifications: selective disclosure, federation and reputation still say Sketch there. These limits are not hidden by the executable registry entries.

Its assertion set is unchanged. The matrix now includes 34 more live paths, so only that aggregate test budget is raised to fifteen minutes; individual suite limits, the global test default, all production limits and the confinement feed table are unchanged. native credential before signing or sending its carrier. An authentic carrier that

## Brief 31

<!-- AUTHORED-BY Claude Opus 5.5 -->

Offline implementation on `claude/project-thread-ee3kq8-confine`, without commits, Node 24
first on PATH. Brief 31 was read completely. Priority item 1 (the revised confinement rule)
is done. Items 2 to 4 (`usage-control-human-approval`, the lender register exchange with
`identity-anonymous`, `accountability-regulator-claim`) were not started in code: the
findings below say what each needs. The blocked reasons of the cells not built now use the
wording of brief 31 section 2. No suite moved from blocked to runs.

**Schema, trace and recorder.** A script step's `segment`/`segmentEnd` may now be `"added"`
as well as `"payment"`. A configuration entry gains `replaces` (`"<kind>:<id>"`, kinds
`party`, `statement`, `step`, `mandate`), `added` (`[{ segment: <first step id>, closes?:
{ agent, kind, outcome } }]`) and `requires` (one configuration id). The recorder stamps
`addedBy: "<configuration id>"` on every event recorded while an added segment is open and
marks the first and last such event with `segment`/`segmentEnd: "added"`; a segment that
records nothing marks no baseline event. Only a configuration run can open an added segment
(the engine throws otherwise); added segments cannot nest. The scenario JSON Schema is
regenerated (`--check` passes).

**Confinement (`src/engine/confinement.ts`).**
- C1/C4: `confinementDifferences` drops every `addedBy` event before building the rows and
  before computing the final outcome; otherwise unchanged (the settlement payment-segment
  exclusion stays).
- C2/C5: `addedSegmentDifferences(trace, entry, parties)` splits the trace into its added
  segments (matched in order to `entry.added`) and checks every gate run inside each: the
  checks recorded before each decision must include, in order, `INBOUND_CHECKS` for the
  inbound gate, and the general part of `OUTBOUND_CHECKS` (mandate to evidence) for the
  outbound gate, plus P1 to P8 when the run is a payment release. The lists come from the
  gate's own constants in `src/trace/types.ts`. Every recorded verdict must be `pass` or
  `not-applicable` and the decision `admit`/`go`. A run that asks the principal is accepted
  only when a principal reply for the same agent follows in the same segment and a later run
  of the same agent's gate passes the check that asked. The last inbound message of each
  segment must match `closes` (agent mapped through the party's `traceId`; outcome is the
  body's `outcome`/`reason`, else the receiving gate's decision); a `refuse` never matches.
  A segment that declares no `closes` must carry no message (a record-only segment).
- C3: `patchDifferences(scenario, entry)` compares the parsed worlds (so schema defaults
  never read as edits): every baseline party (its advertised `configurations` excepted, as
  that is the configuration's own wire choice), statement with its terms, mandate rule
  (keyed `mandate:<agent>#<list>/<rule id or index>`, plus `#limits`) and script step must be
  canonically equal unless listed in `replaces`; a listed item that does not change is also
  reported; baseline step order must be kept; a transport configuration may replace nothing;
  and no fault or test hook (`settlementFault`, `documentFaults`, `evidenceFaults`,
  `agreementCredentialFault`, `authorityFault`, `mandateStatusFault`, `inject`, `stopAfter`,
  `solid.fault`, `carriage.fault`, `trustedIndex.fork`, `federation.expired`/`businessOnly`,
  `originHosted.pointerFault`) may differ from the reference. With `requires`, the reference
  is the prerequisite's world.

**Matrix (`src/engine/matrix.ts`).** `matrixWorld` applies the prerequisite's patch first
when `requires` is set (one level only, on a different layer; the dependent is advertised
where the baseline or the prerequisite was), then refuses a patch that adds a step outside
an added segment, puts a baseline step inside one, leaves one open, or adds any segment
under a `transport-` configuration. `matrixConfiguration` and the suite-constraint check
take the prerequisite's layer into the configured layers.

**Existing configurations under the new rule.** `meaning-negotiated` added its
`term-deliberation` step outside any segment; that step is now a declared record-only added
segment (`added: [{ segment: "term-deliberation" }]`, also marked in its branch's literal
script). Seven executable configurations list what they replace, each an item of their own
layer: `meaning-registry-lookup` and `meaning-negotiated` (`statement:income`),
`reliance-corroboration` (`statement:income`, `statement:deposit`),
`reliance-stated-confidence` (`statement:deposit`), `reliance-quality-certificate` (both
product statements), `identity-email-endpoint` (`party:paywell`, the mailbox binding) and
`authenticity-selective-disclosure` (`statement:income`, `step:apply`, `step:assessed`).
Whether a replaced item belongs to the changed layer is recorded here for review; the test
cannot decide layer membership mechanically.

**Tests (`test/confinement.test.ts`).** Every executable configuration in every scenario is
run through all three functions, against the prerequisite's main run when `requires` is set
(none does yet). Separate tests: the old `confinement-wrong.json` transport patch is now
refused both outside a segment and when marked as an added segment (and `runScenario`
rejects it); `requires` applies the prerequisite first and refuses a chain. Four new wrong
fixtures beside `confinement-wrong.json`, each detected:
- `added-exchange-check-missing.json`: an added register re-lookup after the paid segment.
  As a control the untouched run passes C1 to C5; with its first outbound `recipient` check
  removed from the trace (as a gate that skipped it would record) C2 reports it missing.
- `added-exchange-check-fails.json`: an added balance-statement request sent to the valuer
  register; Sam's mandate fails it and nothing closes the segment.
- `patch-edits-mandate-rule.json`: narrows Sam's baseline `urn:rule:use:payment-request`;
  C3 reports the unlisted change.
- `added-exchange-changes-later-verdict.json`: the same ordinary lookup inserted before the
  bank request, with twenty days passing inside the segment. C2 passes the added exchange;
  C1 reports the later baseline face-check admission turning from `fitness pass` to
  `fitness fail (stale-data)` and the changed final outcome (both runs stop after the
  step-up approval). It is attached to `usage-control-odrl-model-tighten`, whose fed checks
  do not include fitness.

**Manifest and traces.** `scripts/run-scenarios.mjs` writes `addedExchanges` (the number of
`message-out` events with `addedBy`) on every configuration row with a trace; it is 0 on
all 87 such rows. Only the two `meaning-negotiated` traces change (the segment marks and
`addedBy` on the platform's record event, plus the commit field). The rows were regenerated
per configuration with `--scenario=mortgage-main --configuration=<id>` for
`meaning-negotiated` and the nine re-worded blocked cells; the merged manifest keeps the
original row and index order. Counts are unchanged: **281 paths, 237 runs, 14 disagree,
30 blocked** before and after; `test/labels.test.ts` counts need no change.

**Not built, and why.**
- `usage-control-human-approval` (section 2.1). The rule now admits the exchange, but the
  engine has no path that turns an undecided data-terms use into a question for the
  principal: `OutboundGate.dataTerms` stops on `indeterminate`, and an item without
  terms is refused for distribution under the default terms (`purpose-not-permitted`). The reply release in `checkMessage` can
  already park a message for a digest-bound approval (`release-message`), which is the
  path to reuse. Further findings: the `mortgage-payment` profile must declare the new
  operation and list the property-access claim type under `urn:purpose:valuation` (else
  the reply's disclosure check is `excessive-scope`), with a claim rule for its method;
  both are changes to the shared baseline profile. The `supplier-terms-hold` branch will
  measure `disclosure`/`excessive-scope` rather than `data-terms`/`prohibited` unless the
  valuation purpose lists `deposit.balance`, and Harbour's terms reach `not-authorised`
  (registered-recipient requirement) before any prohibition; the brief's expected reason
  would need re-registering. `approval-reused` needs a second recipient able to receive:
  `other-valuer` has no agent.
- `identity-anonymous` (2.2) and `accountability-regulator-claim` (2.3): need the separately
  keyed lender-register agent and the resolver reading its admitted answer; not started.
- 2.4 to 2.9 are in the brief's Later list; 2.10 and the mortgage selective-disclosure cell
  are left for the maintainer (`needs:jesse`), with their texts unchanged.
- C6 (a failure branch compared with its configuration's main path) has no new function:
  branches are still judged by the existing `firstBranchDivergence`. The explorer label
  "added by this suite" belongs to the explorer, which is not in this repository; traces
  carry `addedBy` for it.

**Validation.** `test/confinement.test.ts` 7/7 pass (9 min 47 s). Focused: the six
blocked-cell regressions, `solid-reference-status`, `configuration-matrix`,
`meaning-negotiated` 17/17 pass; `labels`, `traces`, `oracle-snapshot`, `oracle`,
`meaning-reliance-alternatives`, `recorded-joint` 32/32 pass. `tsc --noEmit` passes; biome clean on changed files;
`export-schemas --check` passes. Oracle: 164 paths, parsed JSON equal to
`test/oracle/pre-rdf.json`. RDF guard: 0 failures, 213 allowances, unchanged.
No vocabulary term was added, so codegen was not run. No commit, network request or
dependency addition was made; the full suite and `generate-traces.mjs` were not run.
